# Fork changes

This fork of [herdrdev/herdr-app](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr-app) is the Herdr build used day to day. It carries small, focused changes that each go upstream as their own pull request, and it tracks upstream by merging `upstream/master` into `master`.

This file is the inventory of what diverges. Update it in the same commit that adds, removes or upstreams a change.

Where a change belongs (app Rust core, mods or herdr-core) and the order changes go upstream are decided in [FORK-ADR-001](fork/adr/ADR-001-fork-architecture-stance.md), an architecture decision record (ADR). Each change's section records its answers to upstream's three questions and its upstream verdict. The per-feature audit behind it is [fork/upstream-audit-2026-11-08.md](fork/upstream-audit-2026-11-08.md).

## Branches

- `master`: `upstream/master` plus every change below, merged in. This is what gets built and installed. Base: upstream release 0.1.15 (tag `v0.1.15`, `9950887`).
- `feat/<slug>`, `fix/<slug>`: one change each, branched from `upstream/master` so its pull request carries nothing else.
- `FORK.md`, `scripts/fork-sync` and `mise.local.toml` (untracked) exist only on `master`.

## Setup

- Toolchains match upstream CI (continuous integration): Rust 1.95.0 and Zig 0.16.0, pinned per folder in `mise.local.toml` (listed in `.git/info/exclude`). Run commands through `mise exec --`.
- `just install` builds a release into `~/Applications/Herdr.app` and points `~/.local/bin/herdr-app` at it. Quit and reopen Herdr to use it. herdr-core is installed separately and is not rebuilt.
- `just notices` needs cargo-about 0.9.3: `cargo install --locked cargo-about@0.9.3 --features cli --root ~/.local/share/herdr-app-tools`, found through `CARGO_ABOUT` in `mise.local.toml`.
- App home stays `~/.config/herdr-app`, shared with the release build.

## Workflow

1. Start a change: `git switch -c feat/<slug> upstream/master`.
2. Make it, gated behind a setting wherever practical, and commit.
3. Merge and check it on `master`: `git switch master && git merge --no-ff feat/<slug> && mise exec -- just check`. Upstream's code is not `cargo fmt` clean, so match the surrounding style rather than running `cargo fmt`. The same goes for `defaults/AGENTS.md`: upstream hard-wraps most of it at about 75 columns, so fork text there is wrapped the same way, which keeps merges clean, and stays a map of rules with short examples, the detail going here and in `defaults/herdr.d.ts`.
4. Try it: `mise exec -- just install`, then restart Herdr.
5. Push the branch: `git push -u origin feat/<slug>`. Bigger changes get an upstream issue first (upstream `CONTRIBUTING.md`).
6. Open the pull request: `gh pr create -R herdrdev/herdr-app --head abhijit-s:feat/<slug>`.
7. Keep it current when upstream moves by merging, never rebasing: `git switch feat/<slug> && git merge upstream/master && git push origin feat/<slug>`. A merge keeps review comments anchored and needs no force-push.
8. Sync and reinstall: `scripts/fork-sync`. It lists branches whose commits are not yet upstream. Once a change lands upstream, delete its branch and its entry below.

## Upstream syncs

Each merge of `upstream/master` into `master`, with the conflict choices worth remembering.

Upstream's `unzoomed_neighbor` measures at the fork's `settings::ui_scale()` (zoom times `ui_zoom`, as `displayed_area` does) rather than `zoom()`, and walks the tab through `Tab::panes` and `Tab::path_to` (floats included), as `float_pane::tests::tiled_only_walks_stay_inside_tab` requires.

Since the 0.1.15 sync terminal rows place glyphs with `ShapedLine::with_cell_positions` and pass no forced width (`src/terminal.rs` row, cursor, input-method and fallback shaping all pass `None`; gpui-pre's `shape_text` has no such argument). The one live caller that forces a width, gpui-kit's input measuring its longest line (`vendor/gpui-kit/crates/base/src/input/base/element.rs`, upstream code), reads only the line's `width`, which the pass never changes, and the forced width is part of the layout cache key, so no drawn line ever came from the pass; besides that it ran only in two tests. Those now shape as `row_ops` does: `ligatures_change_glyphs_without_moving_terminal_cells` (Linux) checks the shaper's own glyph positions sit on the advance grid, then places each cell's cluster at the advance and at the rounded cell, and `macos_fallback_symbols_are_fitted_by_their_ink` measures the fitted glyph drawn at its cell's left edge. Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? No: nothing the app draws goes through the pass. (2) Can only the app see it? Not applicable. (3) Can a mod not do it? Not applicable. Verdict: none needed; the fork matches upstream here.

## Changes

### Build identifier

`feat/build-tag` (branched from `master`, fork only): a fork build says which commit it is, beside an unchanged version. `build.rs` runs git (`fork_build.rs`) and sets `HERDR_FORK_BUILD` to `fork.<short sha>`, with `.dirty` when tracked files differ from HEAD; without git (a tarball) it is unset. No `Cargo.toml` edit, so upstream syncs stay clean.

- Shown: `herdr-app --version` prints `herdr-app 0.1.13 (fork 0990622)`; Settings shows `Herdr 0.1.13 (fork 0990622)` and `herdr-core 0.1.18 (fork 8cc5ec1)`, and This computer `hc 0.1.18 (fork 8cc5ec1)` under Machines. The core's build is read from the `build` field of its `server.status` (the herdr-core fork adds it), kept in `update::CoreInfo.build`.
- The version stays upstream's everywhere it is compared or sent: the core compatibility check (`hc::check` on `hello`), app and core update checks (`update::newer`, `core_bin::update_to`, `after_update`), telemetry, feedback, layout `cloned_from` and update notes all read `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` or the core's `version` alone. `herdr-app status` and `ls` report no version. Readers of a `--version` line cut a fork build's ` (fork …)`: `core_bin::version` (finding and checking a core) and `update::version_of` (a staged app update).
- Rebuilds: build.rs reruns when HEAD, its reflog, its branch or `packed-refs` change (paths from `git rev-parse`, so worktrees work), and on edits under `src` and `defaults`. Not on index changes: editors and `git status` rewrite the index, and each rerun recompiles the app. So an edit elsewhere, or a staged-only change, does not mark the build `.dirty` until something else reruns it. Worktrees need their own target dir (the default): Cargo hashes a package by its path within the workspace, so a shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` could keep another worktree's build. After an in-app core update the core's build is cleared until its status arrives on reconnect.
- Tests: `fork_build::tests` (the `--version` line, a well-formed build, git in a temporary repository: no git gives none, a commit gives its sha, an edit adds `.dirty`, untracked files do not; the core's version and build reach Settings apart), `core_bin::tests::only_a_binary_that_says_hc_counts` (a fork core's line still reads as `hc 0.1.1`), `scripts/settings.test.ts` (`forkTag`).
- Files: `build.rs`, `fork_build.rs`, `src/fork_build.rs`, `src/main.rs`, `src/update.rs`, `src/core_bin.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/core-bin/herdr-core-fork`
- Upstream: none (fork only)

### Pane and agent labels

`feat/agent-label` (branched from `master`, fork only): what a pane or agent is called is one app-level value, `label`, with `session` beside it, on `panes()`, `agents()` and `herdr-app ls`, so mods stop carrying their own copies of the "telling title" rule. `src/pane_label.rs` holds the resolver and the rule.

- `label`, first that applies: the pane's `pane.rename` name (one with a letter or digit), its `auto_name`, its title when telling, `session`, the program's name, the terminal's address. A mod pane: its name, else the mod. `title` stays the raw title.
- Telling: judged on the title less what leads up to its first letter, digit or path (`shown_title` already cut spinners), shown trimmed but otherwise as set, so `✓ build passed` and `[3/5] Building` keep their lead: not empty or only digits, not the program's name (any case), not its command line (first word the program's key or name, by basename), not a path (`~`, `/`, `C:\`, `C:/`, `\\server`), no `@`, and not `THROWAWAY`: one documented regex constant for anything holding a UUID (universally unique identifier), `claude-prompt-…`/`tmp.…` files and an editor's ` - Nvim`/` - VIM` title (Neovim as Claude Code's Ctrl+G editor leaves `claude-prompt-<uuid>.md + (/private/tmp/…) - Nvim`). Not a setting: the patterns describe tools' own titles, and a setting would add a Settings row, validation and docs for something nobody has asked to tune.
- `auto_name` second, not after `session`: it is only set at a bare shell whose title is generic, which is never an agent, and the shipped `pane` and `terminals-pane` headers already put it before the title (a folder-name title such as `src` would otherwise beat it).
- `session`: the session's own name from the program's session file, a programs-table field `session_name` (`{file, field, when?}`, the `resume.session` shape, checked the same way; Claude Code ships `~/.claude/sessions/{pid}.json` `name`), for the terminal's foreground process, and only while that process is the foreground (a cached name is matched on pid, so an agent that quit never lends its name to the shell). `pane_label::Sessions` caches it per terminal by pid and file mtime: a look runs after a terminal listing or every 3 s (`SESSION_RECHECK`), stats the files off the main thread and reads one only when its pid or mtime changed; a name that changed publishes. Terminals on other machines get null. It replaces pane-named's JS (JavaScript) lookup and its `claude.sessionNames` shared key; the `herdr.session.claude` label is not used (it can vanish from a live terminal).
- Perf: labels are resolved in `publish` (titles, names, foregrounds, listings), never per frame; `herdr-app ls` reads the published map.
- Shipped mods: `agents` (`title()` and its rows), `terminals-pane`, `win98` window titles and the `pane` header read `label`; their local `clean`/title checks are gone. The `pane` header keeps the program's name for an unnamed agent or bare shell. `tabs`, `peek` and `picker` show a program's kind (its name beside its icon), not what a pane is called, and stay as they were. With telling titles, every shipped mod's description under the populated render fixture is byte-identical to before (all 26 entries and win98's window role diffed), and glyph-led titles (`✓`, `[3/5]`) are pinned in the render test. Intended differences: a renamed pane's name wins over its title in `agents` rows and `win98` titles, and `agents` keeps a leading `✓` or `[` rather than cutting it.
- Tests: `pane_label::tests::{telling_titles_are_shown_as_the_program_set_them, titles_that_say_nothing_are_not_telling, a_label_takes_the_first_that_applies, a_session_files_name_counts_when_it_names_something, session_names_are_read_once_per_process_and_again_when_the_file_changes}` (fixture session files: missing, bad JSON, renamed, another pid), `programs::tests::session_name_files_are_checked_and_a_persons_replaces_the_shipped_one`, `mods::render_tests::mods_name_panes_by_their_label`, `window::tests::pane_labels_follow_changes_not_frames` (own process: 30 frames resolve no label; a title change and a new foreground each resolve both panes once), `scripts/agents.test.ts`. Each guard mutation-checked.
- Files: `src/pane_label.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/programs.rs`, `src/resume.rs` (`SessionFile::check`), `src/terminal.rs` (`frame()` crate-visible for the test), `defaults/programs.json`, `defaults/mods/{agents,pane,terminals-pane}/main.js`, `defaults/mods/win98/window.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `src/SKILL.md`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `scripts/agents.test.ts`

### Rex theme importer

`feat/rex-themes` (branched from `master`, fork only): `herdr-app rex-themes [--dry-run] [--from <themes.json>]` writes `themes/rex-<id>.json` in the app home (`HERDR_APP_HOME` honoured) for each theme of the installed Rex app, read from `/Applications/Rex Beta.app/.../SuperDesignTokens.bundle/.../themes.json`. Rex is closed source, so its theme data is never committed: the importer reads the app on every run and writes only into the person's themes folder, and its tests use an invented fixture in Rex's schema.

- Why a subcommand, not a script: it reuses the app's own theme parser and `app_dir()`, writes with `persist::write_atomic`, and its tests run in `just check` against the real theme loader (`settings::theme_file_problems`, test only); a bun script would have to re-implement both.
- Palette: Rex's `source` (sRGB hex, in the standard red-green-blue colour space) gives `foreground`, `cursor`, `selection` (`selectionBackground`) and `ansi`. Rex's `palette` is the same in display-p3 floats. `background` (the terminal's) is `--super-term-bg`, the pane color a step off the window (`--super-bg`, which becomes `canvas`), so panes read as cards on the window as they do in Rex; a theme without an opaque `--super-term-bg` falls back to `source.background`.
- Named colours: one table, `COLORS` in `src/rex_themes.rs`, maps a Rex token to a Herdr colour only where the meaning matches. Values come from Rex's `cssVariables` (sRGB, its design values); its `semantic` tokens are display-p3 and more saturated, so converting them clips in sRGB. `focus` and `ring` (both from `accentBorder`) are written only when opaque and at 3:1 on the background, else Herdr's readable defaults apply. Setting `surface` and `popover` makes Herdr derive what's left unset the classic way (`paints_own_panels`), which is why `raised` and `ring` are mapped too. `canvas` is Rex's `--super-bg`, which equals the terminal background in 39 of the 41 themes (Rex draws its terminals on `--super-pane`, a step lighter); the palette's background stays the terminal's.
- Light and dark: Herdr tells them apart by the background. A theme Rex calls light whose background reads dark (or the reverse) is skipped with a message; none of the 41 shipped Rex themes is.
- Idempotent: an existing file with the same text is left alone (unchanged), a different one rewritten (updated); nothing but `rex-<id>.json` is touched, and `rex-*` files without a matching Rex theme are kept. A theme with a colour that isn't `#rrggbb` or a CSS (Cascading Style Sheets) `rgb` value, or without 16 ANSI (American National Standards Institute) colours, is skipped with the reason and the rest still written (exit 1). A missing Rex is an error (exit 1), not a crash.
- Tests: `rex_themes::tests::{maps_the_palette_and_the_matching_tokens, light_themes_load_as_light_and_a_mismatch_is_skipped, a_second_run_changes_only_what_changed_and_only_rex_files, a_missing_or_unreadable_rex_file_is_an_error, a_bad_color_skips_that_theme_only, a_focus_and_ring_under_3_to_1_or_translucent_are_left_to_herdr, ids_that_make_no_file_or_the_same_file_are_skipped, the_installed_rex_themes_all_load}` (the last imports the installed Rex's themes into a temporary folder when Rex is on the machine, and checks each loads with `ring` and `focus` at 3:1; nothing kept). Each guard mutation-checked.
- Files: `src/rex_themes.rs`, `src/main.rs`, `src/settings.rs` (`is_light` public, `theme_file_problems`), `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none (Rex-specific)

### Focus colour

`feat/focus-colour` (branched from `master`, fork only): a named theme colour `focus` for the active pane's border, and a person setting `focus_border_width` (1 to 3 px) for its width. The shipped Herdr layout's pane frame draws `"focus": {"color": "focus"}` rather than `"accent"`.

- Colour: `resolve` adds `focus` under both `panel_colors`: the accent in use (`ansi[4]`, or a theme's, look's or settings.json's `colors.accent`, alpha dropped, so a translucent accent draws an opaque border) when it reads at 3:1 on the background, else the same fallback as `ring` (`readable_accent`, shared with `ring`). A theme file's, look's or settings.json's `colors.focus` replaces it with the usual precedence. With an accent at 3:1 or better nothing changes; the five public themes in the author's themes folder whose accent sits under 3:1 (ayu-light, everforest-light-med, melange-light, nord-light, zenburn) draw a readable border.
- Width: `focus_border_width` replaces the layout's `focus` width (`Settings::focus_width`, clamped to 1..3, out of range reported). Every framed pane sits in by the wider of its `border` and `focus` widths (`frame_inset`), and `frame_pane` pads the narrower line out (showing the frame's `background` if it sets one; a hover header is inset by the same pad), so moving the focus never resizes a terminal. Layouts with equal widths (all shipped ones) need no padding; a layout with a wider `focus` than `border` doesn't resize terminals on focus, and one with only a `focus` line leaves that gap around unfocused panes too. Hidden tabs refit when the width changes (`Geometry`).
- Shipped layout: `main.json` changes, so an untouched installed copy is replaced through `installed.json`'s fingerprint (`sync_layouts`), and an edited one is kept as `main-edited` as for any shipped layout change; no snapshot is needed. As with any change to a shipped pane frame, saved pane-frame clicks for `main` (header height, border and focus widths: `PaneClick` in `layout_sync.rs`) are dropped once.
- Settings window: Appearance > Panes: "Focus border" (From theme, or one of the shown theme's ANSI 1 to 6 and terminal foreground at their current values, written to `colors.focus`; a hand-written colour shows as its own swatch) and "Focus border width" (Layout, 1, 2 or 3 px).
- Perf: `focus` is resolved with the rest of the theme at `settings::set` and `reshade`, never per frame; drawing reads it with the same map lookup `accent` took. `frame_border` and `frame_inset` read `settings::get()` (an `Rc` clone) and compare two floats per framed pane per frame, with no allocation.
- Tests: `settings::derivation_tests::{focus_is_the_readable_accent_unless_set, classic_panels_are_the_first_derivation_exactly}` (the golden classic panels leave `focus` out), `settings::tests::focus_border_width_stays_out_unset_and_reports_a_width_outside_1_to_3`, `window::pane_size::tests::{focus_border_width_replaces_the_layouts_focus_width_within_1_to_3, moving_the_focus_never_resizes_a_framed_pane}`, `window::tests::moving_the_focus_keeps_framed_terminals_their_size` (own process), `mods::render_tests::focus_border_rows_show_the_pick`, `scripts/settings.test.ts` (focus border width, focus border). Each guard mutation-checked; the hidden-tab refit on a width change has no test.
- Files: `src/settings.rs`, `src/window.rs` (`frame_pane`), `src/window/pane_size.rs`, `defaults/layouts/main.json`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`
- Upstream: none yet

### Hung alert command test

`fix/alert-hung-test` (branched from `master`, fork only): `alert::tests::a_hung_alert_command_is_killed_after_its_limit_with_what_it_started` failed intermittently with "killed, not finished" while other jobs on the machine started and stopped processes. Its shell line ran `sleep 30 & echo $!; wait`: a bare `wait` returns 0 however its child ended, so a `sleep` killed from outside made the shell exit cleanly, which read as Herdr not killing it. The child is a subshell looping on one-second sleeps for 30 s, which a stray kill of a `sleep` (pkill, killall) doesn't end and which a missing kill turns into a failure rather than a hang, and the shell waits on it (`wait $!`); the test requires the shell to end by SIGKILL and says in its message what the shell's status was, how long it took and whether its child was already reaped. No cause inside the test binary was found: no code sets SIGCHLD to ignored, and every other kill in it is `Child::kill` on a child not yet waited for. Herdr's own code is unchanged.

- Tests: the test above; reverting the kill entirely, or keeping only the shell's kill without its process group's, fails it.
- Files: `src/alert.rs`
- Upstream: none yet

### Pane text zoom

`feat/pane-zoom` (branched from `master`, fork only): `pane.zoom_text_in`, `pane.zoom_text_out` and `pane.zoom_text_reset {pane?}` zoom one terminal pane's text (default: the terminal with the keyboard), as Ghostty's per-surface font size does, while `app.zoom_*` keeps zooming everything. macOS binds Ctrl+= (and Ctrl++), Ctrl+- and Ctrl+0 in `global`; Cmd+= and friends stay `app.zoom_*`. Linux and Windows leave the commands unbound, because Ctrl+= is already `app.zoom_in` there.

- Steps: `zoom_step` as for the app: `"point"` moves the pane's own size (`font_size × pane zoom`) by whole points from where it is (the nearest point first from between two), `"tenth"` moves the pane zoom by 0.1 (`Settings::next_pane_zoom`, sharing `step_zoom` with `next_zoom`). Back at 1 the pane is at the default again (None). The drawn size is `font_size × pane zoom × zoom` (`pane_font_size`, each factor snapped to a whole point within float error at `"point"`), so the app's zoom multiplies on top and resetting either keeps the other. `terminal_cell_width` scales the pane's own whole advance; padding stays the app's.
- Where it lives: `TerminalView::text_zoom`, for the life of the view (a pane's terminal; a peek or swapped-in view of the same terminal has its own, and a pane that gets another terminal starts at the default). Not persisted, as in Ghostty.
- Per-pane cell: `text_metrics(window, pane zoom)` returns the cell and text px. At the default it is exactly the old path (`cell_size(window)`, `settings::font_size()`), so panes at the default size do no extra work per frame; a zoomed pane measures with `measure_cell_at` at its own size, following `terminal_cell_width` and `cell_rounding`. The grid stores the text px beside the cell (`Grid::font`), and everything that drew or mapped by the global size reads the grid instead: `row_ops`, `fallback_fit`, the block cursor's glyph, the input method's composing text and the kept-row cache key (`painted_with`). Selection, mouse and pixel mouse reports, wheel scrolling, links, the scroll thumb, kitty image placement, sprites and Nerd icons already read the grid's cell. `fit_hidden_tabs` fits each hidden terminal at its own size (`TerminalView::fit(area, window)`, from `shown_area`); new terminals still start at the window's cell. A zoom change reflows on the next prepaint through `Grid::fit`, which resizes the screen and tells the program, as a pane resize does.
- Keys and passthrough: with the setting on, Ctrl+- (^_, undo in zsh, readline and emacs) and Ctrl+0 don't reach programs; turn `pane_zoom_keys` off to give them back. The root's `ShellAction` handler, and its `CommandBinding` handler for object-form bindings (`{"command": "pane.zoom_text_in"}`), propagates a `pane.zoom_text_*` action when `pane_zoom_keys` is off or no terminal has the keyboard, so GPUI (Zed's user-interface framework drawn on the graphics card, vendored here as gpui-pre and gpui-kit) hands the keystroke to the terminal's key handler and the program gets Ctrl+= as if it were unbound (the same route Cmd+C takes with nothing selected). The commands still run from the palette, mods and `herdr-app do`.
- Upgrade: `fork-07fcfec-keys.macos.json` (the fork's macOS defaults before these keys, master 07fcfec) is a shipped snapshot in `SHIPPED_MACOS_KEYS`, so an untouched keys.json from then is replaced with the shipped defaults and gains the keys; an edited one stays as it is and needs the four lines added.
- Hidden panes: a pane zoomed by command while its tab is hidden refits at once (`TerminalView::fit` to its `shown_area`), since an undrawn view would keep its program's old grid until shown. A swapped-in view lives only while shown (hiding its tab drops it), so it never needs this; the refit still strips `#swap` from the view's slot before looking up its pane, for a view zoomed in the frame it was made.
- Ends: the drawn size stays within the app zoom's own range, `font_size × 0.5` to `× 3`, however the pane and app zooms multiply (`pane_font_size`), as `ui_scale` caps its product. The pane's steps stop where the text does (`next_pane_zoom` keeps the pane factor within 0.5 to 3 and within `0.5 / zoom` to `3 / zoom`; at whole points, the largest whole point of the pane's own size inside the cap), so every press moves the text toward the end it heads for or leaves it at that end, never back the other way, and the first press back moves it. Where an end isn't a whole point of the pane's own size (a factor stored past the room after the app zoomed, or a layout's 12.5 pt), the press stays at the end rather than landing on the point inside it, which is behind the text; `step_zoom` is shared, so the app's own zoom keys get the same guard (12.5 pt at zoom 0.5 or 3). A factor stored past the room steps from the edge. Each zoom still resets alone; if the app zoom drops, the pane keeps its factor.
- Setting: `pane_zoom_keys` in settings.json (default true, left out of the file while true). Settings window: "Pane zoom keys" toggle on the Text > Zoom page under Zoom steps.
- Indicator: `panes()` has `text_size` (px, app zoom included) while the pane's own zoom sets it; the shipped `pane` header shows it as "16pt" beside the folder. Pane header heights don't change. It follows the view on screen: `panes()` reads `pane_term`, the swapped-in view when there is one, which is what the keys zoom.
- Palette: "Pane text bigger", "Pane text smaller", "Reset pane text size".
- Tests: `settings::tests::{pane_text_zoom_steps_like_the_zoom_keys_from_its_own_size, app_zoom_multiplies_the_panes_own_text_size, a_pane_zoomed_on_a_zoomed_app_stops_at_the_largest_size, no_pane_zoom_press_is_lost_at_the_ends, a_press_at_an_end_off_a_whole_point_never_turns_back, pane_zoom_keys_default_on, the_guide_explains_every_setting}`, `terminal::tests::{a_pane_zoomed_to_fourteen_points_gets_ghosttys_cell, a_zoomed_panes_cells_take_the_cell_width, kept_rows_rebuild_when_the_text_size_changes, a_zoomed_pane_reflows_draws_and_selects_at_its_own_size}`, `window::tests::pane_text_zoom_keys_zoom_the_focused_pane_or_reach_the_program` (own process: keys, the other pane untouched, `panes()`, app zoom on top both ways, a hidden tab's fit, a hidden pane zoomed by command refitting, the palette holding the keyboard, a swapped-in view zoomed and reported, an object-form binding, passthrough when off for both binding forms), `keys::tests::untouched_fork_keys_gain_the_pane_text_zoom_keys`, `mods::render_tests::{pane_header_shows_a_panes_own_text_size, pane_zoom_keys_toggle_shows_the_setting}`, `scripts/settings.test.ts` ("pane zoom keys"). Each fails with its fix reverted (mutation-checked).
- Perf: headless paint A/B (the windowed one needs an unlocked screen). A temporary test, never committed (`zz_paint_bench`), draws a 200 × 60 screen of mixed text (colour, bold and background runs, one wide glyph per row) in a GPUI test window at `font_size` 12, alternating master 07fcfec and this branch, 3 processes each, release `cargo test`. Each process takes 300 warm-up frames, then 5 rounds of 200 frames that re-shape every row (new output, then `window.draw`) and 200 frames with nothing changed (the kept rows). Medians of per-round medians, with the round range: re-shaped frames: master 867 µs (856 to 909), branch 892 µs (870 to 905), branch zoomed to 14 pt 878 µs (869 to 899); unchanged frames: master 265 µs (263 to 275), branch 270 µs (265 to 276), zoomed 272 µs (266 to 281). The branch is within 1 to 3% of master and inside master's own round range, so no cost is measurable at the default size. Limits: GPUI's test text system (not CoreText, so no real glyph rasterising) and no GPU (graphics processing unit; the scene is built, never presented); the timing is the window's prepaint and paint, shaping and the row cache.
- Files: `src/keys.rs`, `src/keys/shipped/fork-07fcfec-keys.macos.json`, `src/terminal.rs` (`text_zoom`, `zoom_text`, `text_size`, `text_metrics`, `measure_cell_at`, `Grid::font`, `fit`), `src/settings.rs` (`pane_zoom_keys`, `step_zoom`, `next_pane_zoom`, `pane_font_size`), `src/window.rs` (dispatch, `pane_zoom_keys_apply`, `panes()` `text_size`), `src/window/pane_size.rs` (`shown_area`, `fit_hidden_tabs`), `src/commands.rs`, `src/mods.rs` (test read of `panes()`), `defaults/keys.macos.json`, `defaults/mods/pane/main.js`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/frame/palette/main.js`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `docs/commands.md`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`
- Open: Linux and Windows have no default pane zoom keys (Ctrl+= is `app.zoom_in` there); a person can bind `pane.zoom_text_*` in keys.json. Left for when someone runs the fork off macOS.
- Upstream: none yet

The setting is named `terminal_line_height`, upstream's name, and `line_height` is read where that is unset. It replaced this fork's own `feat/line-height`, which supported settings.json only.

### Bold terminal weight

`feat/font-weight-bold` (branched from `master`, fork only): `font_weight_bold` in settings.json or a layout's look (100 to 900) is the weight bold terminal cells draw at. Unset, bold draws at 700 or `font_weight` when that is heavier, so a light `font_weight` keeps a far heavier bold: at 250, bold jumps to 700 and inks about 9% heavier than Ghostty's bold. `Settings::bold_weight` resolves it; `cell_font` draws bold at it. Kept rows are keyed on the bold weight beside the regular cell font, so changing either redraws them.

- Settings window: a "Bold weight" stepper (steps of 25) under "Weight" in the Text > Fonts page's "Terminal text" group. Stepping back to the unset weight leaves the key out of settings.json; when the layout's look sets one, the row says so.

### Terminal cell width

`feat/terminal-cell-width` (branched from `master`): upstream pull request #962's `terminal_cell_width` (by glappen, refs #171, open upstream, head `19d601a`), applied to the fork's own sizing. A multiplier, default 1, clamped to 0.8..2, set in settings.json or a layout's look (the layout wins). It maps directly onto Ghostty's `adjust-cell-width` as a percentage: `-5%` is `0.95` (the same cells with `cell_rounding` `"nearest"`; outward rounds the advance down first, where Ghostty rounds it to the nearest). Names, range, clamping and wording are #962's, so settings files and looks are interchangeable with upstream; the pixels match #962's except where noted below.

- The rule, as #962's `font_cell_metrics` states it: `width = round(round(face_width) * cell_width)`, as Ghostty's `Metrics` modifier applies a percentage to `cell_width`. Here it is `cell_width(advance, terminal_cell_width, cell_rounding, scale)` in `src/terminal.rs`, called by `measure_cell_at`: the font's advance on whole device pixels, times the multiplier, to the nearest whole device pixel. `cell_rounding` chooses how the advance rounds: `"nearest"` is #962's (and Ghostty's), `"outward"` rounds it down. The product rounds to the nearest in both modes, as #962 and Ghostty round it: rounding it down as well made the outward stepper uneven (JetBrains Mono 12 at 1x, 7 px: 105% and 110% changed nothing, 95% took a whole pixel, 14%). Height, baseline, underline and strike are unchanged; row height stays the fork's (`row_height`, `terminal_line_height`).
- Where it differs from #962, and why: the fork sizes cells in `measure_cell_at` (the font's `M` advance, a pane's own text zoom, `cell_rounding`) before `cell_metrics` centres the face in that cell, so #962's change to `font_cell_metrics` would size nothing here (that function only gives the face's metrics). `face_width` (the widest ASCII advance, as #962 and Ghostty read it) and the `M` advance are the same for monospaced fonts. The product is taken in f64 from the percent the multiplier stands for (`ghostty_percent`: 0.95 is `1 + -5 / 100`), as Ghostty's `Modifier.parse` and `apply` compute it, so a width landing on a half pixel rounds as Ghostty's does (10 × 0.95 is 9.5, to 10; 10 × 1.05 is 10.5, to 11) rather than by the f32's last bit; #962's f32 product rounds both down (to 9 and 10), so at such ties the fork's cell is a device pixel wider than #962's. A font whose widest ASCII glyph is wider than its `M` would also differ from #962. `CellRounding::whole` rounds in f64 for that, which leaves every other value as it was (an f32 rounds the same widened).
- Glyphs: `glyph_x` (#881, the fork's #40) already computes the glyph move from the cell's difference from the unrounded advance, so glyphs re-centre in the wider or narrower cell with Ghostty's CoreText / FreeType rules, as #962 says. No vendored GPUI change: `with_cell_positions` already places each glyph on its cell, and all the fork's text-rendering patches (subpixel `lcd.rs`, terminal and interface thickening, variable weights, contrast shaders) are untouched.
- Live: `cell_size` and `text_metrics` read the settings on every frame, and each `Grid` keys its `CellMetrics` and kept rows on the cell (`Grid::metrics`, `painted_with`), so a change reflows on the next frame through `Grid::fit`: columns, the program's PTY (pseudo-terminal) size and cell pixels, selection, the cursor, pixel mouse reports and kitty images all read the grid's cell (`terminal_cell_width_applies_live`). A settings.json edit reaches it through `reload_config`, `apply_look` and `settings::set`, whose change refreshes the window; hidden tabs' terminals are refitted by `fit_hidden_tabs` on the window's half-second tick once the cell is the same two ticks running.
- Retired: `letter_spacing` (px added to the cell, `feat/terminal-letter-spacing`, issue #171, the fork's pull request #336) and `cell_width` (`"font"` or `"pixel"`, `feat/cell-width-pixel`). Cells are always on whole device pixels. Either key, with any value, in settings.json or a layout's look, still loads (`Person` and `Look` keep it as written, so a rewrite of the file keeps it) and is reported by `Settings::problems` until removed (`cell_problems`, as other invalid keys are), and changes nothing. A `terminal_cell_width` outside 0.8..2 is clamped and reported. What moves for a file that had neither key (the old default, `"font"` widths): the cell goes from the font's fractional advance to whole device pixels, by `cell_rounding`. Outward, the default, rounds down, as `"pixel"` drew: JetBrains Mono 12 at 2x is 14.4 to 14 device px (−2.8%); the worst case is up to a device pixel narrower, about −10% at 1x (JetBrains Mono 13 at 1x, 7.8 to 7 px; 16, 9.6 to 9). `"nearest"` keeps any cell within half a device pixel of the font's advance, as upstream does.
- The person's settings (JetBrains Mono 12 at 2x, `cell_rounding` nearest, `line_height` 1, `cell_width` `"pixel"`) draw pixel-identically to master: `grid_dump` (an ignored test, `HERDR_GRID_DUMP=<settings.json>`) prints, for a settings file at 12 to 24 px and 1x and 2x, the cell, every `CellMetrics` field and, for four lines regular and bold, each glyph's device origin and subpixel variant and CoreText's ink per device row, as the grid paints them; its 204 lines are the same on master and this branch.
- At 0.95 the cell is Ghostty's with `adjust-cell-width = -5%` (`cell_width_at_ninety_five_percent_is_ghosttys_minus_five`: JetBrains Mono, Geist Mono and Menlo, 9 to 24 pt, 1x and 2x; the width and Ghostty's CoreText glyph move; its reference transcribes Ghostty's arithmetic, so it checks the face width, the `M` advance against the widest ASCII glyph, and the glyph move, while half-pixel ties are covered by `cell_width_scales_the_whole_advance_and_rounds_again`). JetBrains Mono 12 at 2x: 14.4 to 14, × 0.95 = 13.3 to 13 device px, the glyphs moved −0.7 device px, which GPUI paints at −0.75 (it places glyphs on quarter device pixels).
- Settings window: a "Cell width" stepper (5% steps, #962's) under Scroll speed on the Text > Grid page; 100% leaves the key out of settings.json, and the row says when the layout sets its own. The Letter spacing stepper and the Font / Whole pixels choice are gone.
- When #962 merges upstream: take its `font_cell_metrics` change, tests and docs as they are at that sync (its `cell_width` argument and `width` line are then upstream's `font_cell_metrics`, which the fork calls for the face's metrics only, so a multiplier there would scale the face's width, not the cell); keep the fork's `cell_width` as the one place the cell is sized, and the Settings window's row on the Grid page (#962's is a Look row).
- Tests: `terminal::tests::{cell_width_scales_the_whole_advance_and_rounds_again, nearest_rounding_keeps_the_grid_within_half_a_device_pixel, cells_follow_each_fonts_metrics_by_the_rounding_rule, row_glyphs_sit_where_ghostty_centres_them, cell_width_at_ninety_five_percent_is_ghosttys_minus_five, a_zoomed_panes_cells_take_the_cell_width, terminal_cell_width_applies_live, grid_dump}`, `window::tests::a_cell_width_in_settings_json_refits_every_terminal` (the real path: settings.json read by `reload_config`, a shown pane, a pane at its own text zoom and a hidden tab's terminal all refit, their programs told), `settings::tests::{terminal_cell_width_defaults_inherits_overrides_and_clamps, retired_cell_keys_are_reported_and_kept, the_guide_explains_every_setting}`, `mods::render_tests::terminal_text_rows_show_whose_value_is_in_use`, `scripts/settings.test.ts` ("cell width steps by 5%").
- Files: `src/settings.rs` (`Person`, `Settings`, `CELL_WIDTH_RANGE`, `cell_problems`, `CellRounding::whole`), `src/layout.rs` (`Look`), `src/terminal.rs` (`measure_cell_at`, `cell_width`, `ghostty_percent`, `row_height`), `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `src/SKILL.md`, `README.md`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? Yes: it sizes the terminal grid, the program's PTY size and every cell-mapped input. (2) Can only the app see it? Yes: the cell is measured from the font in the app. (3) Can a mod not do it? A mod can't size the terminal grid. Verdict: the setting is upstream's (#962, not ours), so nothing goes upstream from here; the fork's application of it in `cell_width` is D (fork only), as long as the fork sizes cells itself (`measure_cell`, `cell_rounding`, pane text zoom).

### Cell rounding

`feat/cell-round-nearest` (branched from `master`, fork only): `cell_rounding` in settings.json, `"outward"` (default) or `"nearest"`, for how the grid snaps to whole device pixels: the cell width (the font's advance, then that × `terminal_cell_width`; see Terminal cell width) and the row height (ascent + descent, × `line_height`).

- Rule (`CellRounding::whole`, used by `row_height` and `cell_width`): `"nearest"` puts each dimension at the nearest whole device pixel of the font's own metric, at least one. That is the closest a pixel grid can get to the font's designed advance and line height: never more than half a device pixel off, either way. `"outward"` (the advance down, the row up) is up to a whole pixel off, always in the same direction, so zoomed text drifts from the font's spacing (JetBrains Mono 13 at 1x: 7.8 × 16.9 becomes 7 × 18 outward, 8 × 17 nearest). Ghostty sizes cells the same way (`Metrics.zig`, `@round` of the face's width and height), which confirms it rather than being the reason.
- Default `"outward"`: rows stay where they were, and widths are those `cell_width` `"pixel"` drew (see Terminal cell width for what that moves for files that had the old fractional default). `"nearest"` moves the grid by a device pixel at many sizes, so it is opt-in.
- What `"nearest"` moves, by `cells_follow_each_fonts_metrics_by_the_rounding_rule` (JetBrains Mono, Geist Mono, Menlo, Monaco and Courier New, 9 to 24 pt, 1x and 2x, line height 1): 83 of the 120 font/size/scale cells change by one device pixel in width, height or both (counted once from the test's metrics; no test asserts the count). JetBrains Mono at 12 (1x and 2x) and 15 (1x) stay as they were.
- A row rounded below the font's height: the font's face (its height from the hhea or OS/2 tables, line gap included, as Ghostty reads it) is centred in the row on a whole device pixel, with Ghostty's baseline inside it (`cell_metrics`, `font_cell_metrics`), so glyphs that reach the font's ascent or descent overhang about equally above and below, at most half a device pixel each at `line_height` 1 (a `line_height` below 1 overhangs by design, either way; `a_shorter_row_overhangs_half_a_device_pixel_each_side_at_most` measures the ink of `É` and `g` at that baseline, which for JetBrains Mono at 9 to 24 pt, 1x and 2x, stays inside the row). The underline and strikethrough sit where the font's post and OS/2 tables put them, a whole device pixel at least, and can reach into the next row at small sizes, drawn over its background rather than cut. Program backgrounds paint before any text, so they don't cover the overhang; the cursor, the input method's composing background and images drawn above text can, by that much. Box and block sprites, Nerd icons and fallback symbols are sized from the cell itself, so they fit whatever it rounds to.
- Settings window: a "Cell rounding" choice (Outward / Nearest) under "Cell width".
- Tests: `terminal::tests::{nearest_rounding_keeps_the_grid_within_half_a_device_pixel, cells_follow_each_fonts_metrics_by_the_rounding_rule, nearest_keeps_twelve_point_and_meets_ghostty_when_zoomed, a_shorter_row_overhangs_half_a_device_pixel_each_side_at_most, cell_rounding_applies_live, cell_width_scales_the_whole_advance_and_rounds_again, line_height_stays_within_its_range}`, `settings::tests::cell_rounding_defaults_to_outward`. The font tests use CoreText directly (macOS only); the test window's text system gives every font the same metrics.
- Files: `src/settings.rs` (`CellRounding`, `Person`, `Settings`), `src/terminal.rs` (`measure_cell`, `row_height`, `cell_width`), `defaults/frame/settings/main.js` (`CELL_ROUNDINGS`), `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Settings search

`feat/settings-search` (branched from `master`, fork only): a search field at the top of the Settings window's sidebar. It finds a setting by its name, its description or the settings.json key it writes, and filters every page in place to its matches.

- Results: every page with a match shows under its own title (a click opens the page), with only its matching settings under their section headings, controls working as on the page. Filtering in place rather than a list that jumps to a row: the setting can be changed right in the results, and a list would need a scroll-to-row the mod API (application programming interface) lacks. Notes and spacing between settings are left out of results. With no match it says "No settings match".
- Matching (`searchWords`, `wordSpan`, `matchSetting` in `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`): case-insensitive, with settings.json's `_ - .` read as spaces, so `cell_rounding`, "cell rounding" and "rounding" all find Cell rounding. Every typed word must fall in the setting's title, keys, description or section heading, as a prefix or a substring. Titles and keys also forgive one typo (a letter added, dropped, changed or two swapped) in a whole word of four letters or more that starts with the same letter, so "roundng" finds Cell rounding and "sound" doesn't find "round". Descriptions only match as typed, since a typo there finds too much. The palette's matcher (a word's letters in order anywhere in the text) is looser than a filter of settings wants.
- Marks: matched text is tinted (background only, no weight change, so nothing moves while typing). Results show each setting's settings.json keys under its name, marked where they matched. A description has its matches marked too, drawn a word at a piece so it still wraps.
- Keys: cmd-f (ctrl-f off macOS) focuses the field and selects its text. Typing a character with nothing focused that takes keys (no field, font list or rename open) starts a search with it; a lone space doesn't. Esc clears the words (and closes a font list or rename opened in the results, whose page isn't the one shown after), then leaves the field, and only then does its usual job (closes a font list, then the window). Off macOS an Alt key is a shortcut, not a character to search for. A click on a page in the sidebar leaves the search.
- Index: each page's rows record their title, description, keys and heading through `row()` and `block()` (pickers and lists that are settings: Layout, Dark and Light theme, Option key, each machine, Add a machine). The index is built the first time a search runs, by one pass over the pages, which also starts reading the machine list and the layout file if their pages haven't yet. It's built again only when what the pages draw from changes (`settings()`, `updates()`, `telemetry()`, `layoutSpacing()`, settings.json, the machine list, the layout file and its name), so a description that changes, such as an update check's, or a row that appears is found as it reads at that moment; a keystroke matches the words against the index and draws the pages with only their matches. A row drawn that the index lacks is matched as it draws and indexed next time. With the field empty none of this runs: `row()` returns at its first check, and the pages draw as they would without the search field.
- Perf: not benchmarked. The index pass runs each page's builder once; a keystroke runs `matchSetting` once per indexed row and draws the matching pages, which is the same element-building work as showing a page.
- Tests: `scripts/settings.test.ts` (word folding, prefix and substring and typo matching, every word required, marked runs, only matches shown under page and heading, finding by key and with a typo, the no-match state, the index built once and again after a write, a page not yet opened read in, a changed description found, nothing indexed with the field empty, cmd-f, typing into the field left to it, esc and alt), `mods::render_tests::settings_search_filters_every_page`.
- Files: `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Settings groups

`feat/settings-groups` (branched from `master`, fork only): the Settings window's sidebar is five groups that open and close, holding fifteen pages, and themes have a page of their own with a grid that draws only the rows on screen.

- Groups and pages (`GROUPS` in `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`): Appearance (Themes, Layout, Window, Panes), Text (Zoom, Fonts, Terminal grid, Rendering), Input (Keyboard on macOS, Mouse), Agents (Activity, Alerts), System (Machines, Updates, Privacy). The old Look page split into Themes (Appearance, Dark and Light theme), Layout (layout picker, Spacing), Window (App icon, Panel colours, Sidebar shade, Notification position, Inactive window) and Panes (New tabs arrange, Name panes automatically, Focus border, Focus border width); Mouse took Focus follows mouse and its delay; Activity took Working spinner (from Look) and Resume agents after restart (from Alerts), and holds Header info. The old Text page split into Zoom (Zoom, Zoom steps, Pane zoom keys, Interface zoom, Interface text size), Fonts (the interface and terminal fonts, weights, Ligatures), Terminal grid (Line height, Letter spacing, Cell width, Cell rounding) and Rendering (thicken, contrast and smoothing for interface and terminal text, Faint text, Subpixel order).
- Sections: `app.settings {section}` takes a page id; `look` opens Themes and `text` Zoom (`ALIASES`, `pageFor`), and anything else Themes, as an unknown section opened Look before. `keyboard` off macOS isn't a page.
- Open groups: the shown page's group opens with the window; a click on a group opens or closes it, and going to a page opens its group. Which are open is kept in shared.json (`settings.groups.open`), so the sidebar opens as it was left. The sidebar scrolls when every group is open.
- Search: a group with a match opens while the words are typed, without changing what's kept (a click on a group does nothing while searching), and each group and page shows how many of its settings match; pages with none dim. Results are headed "Group › Page". On the Themes page only the two slot tabs are results; the grid isn't drawn while searching.
- Keys: up and down go to the page before or after (opening its group), left and right close and open the shown page's group, when no field, font list or rename has the keyboard. Enter does nothing: the page under the selection already shows. Keys typed into the theme filter stay there, and esc leaves the filter rather than closing the window.
- Themes page: two tabs, Dark theme and Light theme, each showing its theme as currently set, pick the slot the grid lists for (the one in use when the window opens). Chips (All, then Standard and Rex with counts) pick the source: a theme named `rex-<name>` is Rex (`REX_PREFIX`, what `herdr-app rex-themes` writes), the rest Standard. A filter field narrows by name. Cards show the theme's background, a `❯ ls` line in its accent and foreground, and ANSI 1 to 6; Rex cards drop the `rex-` from their name. A theme named like a layout stays that layout's own. The grid is a `v_virtual_list` of rows (a heading per source while two show, which closes with a click, then five cards a row) in a scroll area of its own that stops the page's wheel, as the font list does. It's scrolled to the picked theme (`VirtualListScrollHandle.scroll_to_item`, centred) when the page opens and when the slot, source, filter or closed sources change, not on every draw, so a click on a card leaves the grid where it is.
- Selection (option E): the selected page, slot tab, source chip and theme card draw a tonal pill: the accent (or `ring` without one) at 16% over the dialog's `popover`, its label in the accent at semibold, moved toward the foreground in tenths until it reads at 4.5:1 on that fill (`tonal`, `contrast`, `over`). Tabs, chips and cards add a ring of the accent at 50%; cards keep the ✓. Hover is the foreground at 4%, never the accent. While the window isn't active (`window().active`), the selection is a neutral grey (foreground at 10%) with foreground text.
- Perf: the Look page built a card for every theme on each render (125 for the author's themes folder) and its render ran into the 50 ms script budget ("interrupted at themePicker") on a loaded machine. The Themes page builds no card in its render: the list asks for the rows on screen only, each through its own call. Measured with `window::tests::theme_grid` (120 themes, a test build, three runs, on a loaded machine): the Look page laid out 120 of 120 cards on opening, in 4 script renders taking 613 to 679 ms in script; the Themes page lays out 30 (six rows), in 2 renders taking 52 to 88 ms, and four wheel scrolls lay out the rows they reach with no script render.
- Tests: `scripts/settings.test.ts` (sections and aliases; open groups read, kept on each open and close, opened by going to a page; search opening groups with counts per page and leaving what's kept; arrows; the theme filter keeping its keys; the Themes page by slot, source, filter and closed source, a layout's own theme left out; the grid scrolled to the picked theme once per listing, to the top when it's filtered out, and again on coming back, given row heights and keys and drawing only the rows asked for; a long name cut with the whole name on hover; the tonal pill on page, tab, chip and card and a neutral hover; 4.5:1 on light and dark themes, the ring without an accent, grey in an inactive window), `window::tests::theme_grid` (own process: opens on the picked theme, at most 30 cards laid out, none rendered by a scroll), `mods::render_tests::theme_cards_are_rows_of_a_virtual_list`, and the `mods::render_tests` settings tests cover these pages. Upstream's `long_theme_names_stay_inside_their_card` render test is deleted here: its cards moved to the Themes page, where `scripts/settings.test.ts` checks a long name is cut with the whole name on hover. An upstream merge that touches that test conflicts or brings it back; keep it deleted. `window::tests::copied_toast` presses the slot tabs rather than a stepper: a stepper's write to settings.json wakes the file watcher's thread, which the test scheduler refuses (the stepper an earlier version of the test pressed sat below the dialog's fold, so it wrote nothing). Each guard mutation-checked: every mutation fails a test, the window test also against the Look page.
- Files: `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `src/window.rs` (tests), `src/commands.rs`, `docs/commands.md`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Theme look tags

`feat/theme-tags` (branched from `master`, fork only): the Themes page tags every theme by look, read off its own card colours (`background`, `terminal_foreground`) while the page draws, and a second row of chips filters by them: Warm, Neutral, Cool, then Soft, Standard contrast, High contrast. Nothing is stored: no setting, no theme-file field, and the picks last while the Settings window is open, as the source chip does.

- Temperature, from the background in OKLCH (`oklch`, `themeTemperature`): chroma under 0.005 (`NEUTRAL_CHROMA`) is Neutral whatever the hue, since dark greys carry a tint nobody sees (gruvbox-material-dark `#282828`, kanagawa-dragon `#181616`, tomorrow-night-eighties `#2d2d2d`; tomorrow-night `#1d1f21` sits just under the line at 0.0049 and is pinned in the tests); above it, hues from 330° round through red, orange and yellow to 115° (`WARM_FROM`, `WARM_TO`) are Warm (melange-dark `#292522`, squirrelsong-dark `#372920`, espresso-libre `#2a211c`), the rest (greens, teals, blues, purples) Cool (ayu-mirage `#1f2430`, nord, tokyonight).
- Contrast, the OKLCH lightness gap between text and background (`themeContrast`), so light themes read as dark ones do: under 0.59 Soft (`SOFT_GAP`: `#cccac2` on `#1f2430` is 0.58, `#b8a898` on `#2a211c` 0.48), from 0.68 High (`HIGH_GAP`), and text within 0.05 of white is High whatever the background (`NEAR_EXTREME`: rex-whitby-bay's `#f7f7ee` on `#2c2f3c`, a gap of 0.67); near-black text on a light theme already clears 0.68, so it needs no such rule. A lightness gap rather than the WCAG (Web Content Accessibility Guidelines) contrast ratio, which sorts these themes much the same: OKLCH lightness is built to step evenly as seen, so the cut-offs read as plain distances. On the author's 173 themes: Warm 37, Neutral 65, Cool 71; Soft 63, Standard 77, High 33.
- Chips: one pick per row, a second click clears it; rows combine with each other, the source chip and the name filter (AND). Each look chip counts what it would list with the source and the other row's pick; the source chips count with the looks. Look chips never hide, and the picked source chip stays (at 0) when a look or the filter empties it, so a pick listing nothing stays clearable ("No themes match."). The grid scrolls to the picked theme again when a look changes (its listing key includes them). Origin headings still close.
- Perf: only while the Themes page draws. Tags are kept per colour pair (`themeLook`'s map), so a redraw is a map lookup per theme; a theme not seen before is a few `cbrt`s. 200 unseen themes tag in about 0.65 ms under Node on the author's Mac; `scripts/settings.test.ts` asserts under 16 ms (a frame). Tags are frozen, so no caller can change a shared one. No Rust change: the colours were already on `themes()` for the cards.
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? No: it only filters what the Settings page lists. (2) Can only the app see it? No: the colours reach the page through `themes()`. (3) Can a mod not do it? It is a mod (the Settings frame mod). Verdict: belongs in mods, fork only, like the source chips it extends.
- Tests: `scripts/settings.test.ts` (19 real themes' temperature and contrast, light ones included; translucent and unreadable colours; the per-pair memo; 200 themes timed; look chips with counts, AND with source and name filter, one pick per row, toggle off, closed sources, an empty look kept clearable with the picked source chip at 0, the light slot; the grid scrolling again on a look pick).
- Files: `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`
- Upstream: none

### App icon

`feat/app-icon` (branched from `master`, fork only): `app_icon` in settings.json, a built-in variant (`"classic"`, default, `"midnight"`, `"mirage"`, `"glass"`, `"phosphor"`, `"graphite"`), `"theme"` (see Theme icon below) or an absolute or `~/` path to an `.icns`, `.png` or `.svg` (relative paths are refused: Herdr runs from `/` when the Dock starts it and the install scripts from the checkout, so one would name two files). One key rather than a "custom" value plus a path key: a path can't be mistaken for a name, since names have no `/`.

- Artwork: `scripts/app-icons.py` draws the five variants other than Classic from `assets/icons/herdr-ui-icon-clean.svg` (keeping its Apache-2.0 attribution metadata) into `assets/icons/variants/<name>.svg`, builds each `.icns` with rsvg-convert and iconutil, and renders the Settings previews (`defaults/frame/settings/app-icons/<name>.png`, 96 px). Classic is `assets/icons/Herdr.icns`, as in upstream. The SVGs (Scalable Vector Graphics files), `.icns` and previews are checked in, as `Herdr.icns` is, so builds and installs need neither tool; `--check` (run by `just test`) fails when the checked-in SVGs differ from what the script draws. Sizes up to 32 px are drawn without glows, shadows, sheens and scan lines, which blur a small icon into its tile (Phosphor's glow and Glass's sheen did); Phosphor's glow is also tighter at every size (blur 6 in the artwork's units, scan lines at 20%). Every drawn variant sits on macOS's icon grid as the system draws other apps' icons (measured from macOS 27's own renderings of Terminal, Notes, Ghostty and Rex): an 824 px tile with 100 px margins on the 1024 canvas at every size, corners that are superellipse quarters (radius 256, exponent 2.5, within a pixel of the system's mask), and the system's drop shadow (black at 25%, blur 16, 8 down, a blurred copy of the tile rather than `feDropShadow`, so Linux renderers and AppKit's CoreSVG draw it too) baked into the margin. The artwork keeps its own 896 px tile and is scaled onto the grid (0.92). The shadow has to be in the image: `setApplicationIconImage` hands it to the Dock and app switcher as it is, while Finder's bundle icon is masked and shadowed by the system either way, so it looks the same with or without one. Classic is upstream's `Herdr.icns`, unchanged: on the grid at 64 px and above, but its 32 px images are 28 px tiles (Apple's are 26) and it has no shadow. Settings previews classic from that `.icns` (sips) so the six tiles share one size.
- Runtime (macOS): `app_icon::follow`, called from `settings::set`, hands the image to `NSApplication.setApplicationIconImage` at start-up and whenever the setting changes, from bytes built into the binary (about 1.5 MB for the five), so any build shows them. Off the main thread it does nothing. It remembers an icon only once AppKit took it, so a file that can't be read or decoded logs a warning, leaves the Dock as it was and is tried again with the next settings. AppKit decodes SVG files itself.
- Install: `scripts/app-icon` reads settings.json (`HERDR_APP_HOME`, else `XDG_CONFIG_HOME/herdr-app`, else `~/.config/herdr-app`) and writes the icon: `scripts/install-macos` into `Contents/Resources/Herdr.icns` (a `.png` is scaled into an iconset with sips and built with iconutil; an `.svg` keeps classic in the bundle), then touches the bundle so Finder reads it again; `scripts/install-linux` into the launcher's scalable icon, or the `.desktop` file's `Icon=` names a `.png` file's path. Whatever can't be used (no python3 to read settings.json, a missing or relative path, a PNG (Portable Network Graphics file) sips can't read, an `.svg` for the bundle or an `.icns` for Linux) installs classic with a warning, and the install goes on to sign the bundle. `scripts/package`, `mac-dmg` and the release installer are unchanged: release builds and self-update keep the classic icon.
- Theme icon (`feat/theme-icon`): `"theme"` draws the same artwork in the colours of the theme showing, while Herdr runs. `scripts/app-icons.py` writes it as two templates on the same grid, `assets/icons/variants/theme.svg` and `theme-small.svg` (sizes up to 32 px, without the artwork's shadow), whose `{tile_top}`-style fields `app_icon::ThemeColors` fills from the resolved theme's `background`, `terminal_foreground` and `accent`. The tile is the background as a vertical gradient (a dark one lifted 12% toward its foreground at the top and 35% toward black at the bottom; a light one 40% toward white at the top and 16% toward its foreground at the bottom, so it stays apart from a light Dock), with a line of the foreground just inside its edge (14% on dark, 22% on light). The artwork is the accent, the thing that tells themes apart, as a diagonal gradient fading 45% toward the foreground; both ends move toward the foreground's side until they read at 3:1 on the whole tile. resvg (already in the tree through gpui-pre) rasterises each .icns size in-process, into an .icns built in memory, so `follow` shows it through the same AppKit path and the grid tests check it too. `settings::set` hands `follow` the resolved theme, so a light and dark switch redraws it; focus only moves `chrome`, which the icon doesn't read. The colours are the cache key: an icon not drawn yet is drawn on its own thread and shown back on the main thread through GCD (Grand Central Dispatch, `dispatch2`) if the settings still ask for it; the last four stay cached, so switching light and dark again doesn't redraw. The bundle keeps classic (`scripts/app-icon`). The Settings preview is the icon in Graphite, written and checked by `app_icon::tests::theme_preview_is_the_drawn_icon`.
- Settings window: an "App icon" row on the Window page (Appearance group), a previewed tile per built-in and Theme with the pick outlined; a file shows as its path.
- Tests: `app_icon::tests::{app_icon_reads_names_and_image_paths, built_in_icons_are_distinct_icns_files, appkit_reads_every_built_in, every_built_in_has_every_icon_size, drawn_variants_sit_on_the_macos_icon_grid, a_file_icon_reads_the_file_with_home_expanded, appkit_reads_an_svg_file, following_off_the_main_thread_does_nothing, theme_colors_keep_the_artwork_readable_on_the_tile, theme_colors_come_from_the_resolved_theme, an_unchanged_palette_is_not_drawn_again, a_drawn_icon_shows_only_while_still_wanted, unreadable_overrides_fall_back_to_the_theme, theme_templates_are_filled_completely, theme_preview_is_the_drawn_icon}`, `mods::render_tests::app_icon_picker_outlines_the_pick`, `scripts/app-icon.test.ts` (each built-in lands in a bundle, classic for no or broken settings, files, PNG conversion, Linux, the generator check).
- Files: `src/app_icon.rs`, `src/settings.rs` (`Person`, `Settings`, `set`), `Cargo.toml` (objc2, objc2-app-kit, objc2-foundation, already in the lock through gpui-pre-macos), `scripts/app-icons.py`, `scripts/app-icon`, `scripts/install-macos`, `scripts/install-linux`, `assets/icons/variants/`, `defaults/frame/settings/app-icons/`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js` (`APP_ICONS`, `iconPicker`), `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `justfile`
