### Variable font weights

`fix/variable-font-weight`: draws a variable face at the requested weight. Herdr bundles JetBrains Mono as one variable file, so its family has a single face and every weight (Medium text, Bold, `font_weight`) drew at the default instance. A `HERDR:` change in `vendor/gpui-pre-macos/src/text_system.rs` (`at_weight`, `variation_weight`) copies the face at the asked weight on its `wght` axis and maps CoreText's runs back to that copy by PostScript name and weight. Desk-verified: bundled JetBrains Mono at 225 matches Rex's 225 (same grid, ink within 4%).

- Files: `vendor/gpui-pre-macos/src/text_system.rs`
- Upstream: pull request #333 (on macOS the bundled JetBrains Mono drew Medium and Bold at Regular)

### Interface font weight

`feat/ui-font-weight` (fork only): `ui_font_weight` in settings.json (100 to 900, default 400, GPUI's normal weight that the panels draw at unless something sets one) sets the weight of every glyph outside the terminal grid: panels, tabs, overlays, toasts, every mod and the Settings window. It is an offset, not a default: every run the window shapes moves by `ui_font_weight - 400`, within 100 to 900, so text that asks for its own weight keeps its distance from regular text and bold never draws lighter than regular (at 900 they meet). A default-only weight would leave a `SEMIBOLD` label at 600 under regular text at 700. The offset sits where runs are shaped, not in the text style, so it also reaches weights that skip the style cascade (gpui-kit's markdown and highlight bold, plot labels). `HERDR:` changes in `vendor/gpui-pre/src/text_system.rs` (`WindowTextSystem::weight_shift`, `replace_weight_shift`, `resolve_run_font`, used by every shaping path) and `vendor/gpui-pre/src/window.rs` (`Window::set_font_weight_shift`, `with_font_weight_shift`). `AppWindow::render` sets it from `Settings::ui_weight_shift`, beside `set_glyph_thicken`; the terminal grid paints inside `with_font_weight_shift(0)`, so terminals keep `font_weight`. The line layout cache keys on the shifted face, so a change applies live. A variable `ui_font` draws every step through `fix/variable-font-weight`; a family of static faces draws the nearest one it has. The bundled Geist is a single static Regular face, so with nothing else installed it draws every weight (bold included) as Regular; a variable Geist installed on the system, or a variable `ui_font`, draws them all.

- Measured (`tests/weight_ink.rs`, variable Geist Mono for the panels and JetBrains Mono 500 for the grid, 12px at 1x, dark on light): regular / semibold / terminal ink 0.0416 / 0.0613 / 0.0630 at shift -200 (`ui_font_weight` 200), 0.0613 / 0.0767 / 0.0630 at 0, 0.0840 / 0.0974 / 0.0630 at +300 (700). Semibold stays heavier at every shift, and semibold at -200 inks as regular at 0 (both shape at 400).
- Settings window: an "Interface font weight" stepper (steps of 25) under "Interface font" in the Text > Fonts page's "Interface text" group. 400 leaves the key out of settings.json. Layouts don't set it.
- Files: `src/settings.rs` (`Person`, `Settings::compose`, `ui_weight_shift`, `problems`, test), `src/window.rs` (`AppWindow::render`), `src/terminal.rs` (the grid's paint), `vendor/gpui-pre/src/{text_system.rs,window.rs}`, `tests/weight_ink.rs`, `Cargo.toml`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`
- Upstream: fork only

### Font thickening

`feat/font-thicken`, then `feat/font-thicken-setting` (the upstream pull request's branch, merged over it): `font_thicken` (`"auto"`, `true` or `false`) and `font_thicken_strength` (0 to 255, default 255), person settings for how terminal glyphs rasterise. `"auto"` (absent, the default) is GPUI's own: it picks the smoothing level from the text color's luminance, so dark text on light draws at the lightest level and light text on dark at the heaviest. `true` draws as Ghostty's `font-thicken` does: CoreGraphics font smoothing on, a gray fill of strength / 255 in linear gray, whatever the text color, then tinted. `false` draws as Ghostty's `font-thicken = false`: smoothing off (`setShouldSmoothFonts(false)`), plain antialiased coverage, the same for every color. `true` and `false` are JSON booleans; the app never wrote `false` (the toggle removed the key), so `true` and an absent key mean what they did, and a `false` written by hand or by an agent turns smoothing off rather than drawing as the default; any other string is a `problems()` entry and draws as `"auto"` (`settings::Thicken`, which keeps it to name). `ui_font_thicken` and `ui_font_thicken_strength` (same values and defaults) do the same for every other glyph in the window: panels, tabs, overlays, toasts, every mod and the Settings window. `HERDR:` changes in `vendor/gpui-pre` (`GlyphThicken`: `Auto`, `Strength(u8)` or `Off`, in `RenderGlyphParams::thicken` and so in the glyph atlas key; `Window::with_glyph_thicken`) and `vendor/gpui-pre-macos/src/text_system.rs` (the rasteriser, and 1 more pixel of raster room for `Strength`, as Ghostty pads thickened glyphs). `Window::set_glyph_thicken` sets the thickening each frame's paint starts at, deferred draws and tooltips included; `AppWindow::render` sets it from `ui_font_thicken` (`Settings::ui_glyph_thicken`), beside `set_rem_size`. The terminal paints its grid inside `with_glyph_thicken` at its own (`Thicken::glyph`), which replaces the window's and restores it after, and the row cache's `painted_with` key carries it. The atlas key holds it, so interface and terminal glyphs smoothed differently never share a tile.

- Measured (`tests/thicken_ink.rs`, JetBrains Mono 12px at 1x): font smoothing steps at strengths about 112, 192, 224 and 255, so Ghostty's 30 is its lightest level. Weight 400 with strength 30 inks both polarities alike (0.126), as Ghostty does; weight 250 without thickening inks light on dark 0.134 and dark on light 0.107.
- Measured, interface (same test, dark on light): `ui_font_thicken` at 255 takes panel text from 0.126 to 0.152 ink, while a terminal grid inside it with `font_thicken` auto stays at 0.126.
- Measured, smoothing off (same test, JetBrains Mono 400 at 12 device px, headless 2x window): `false` inks 0.0511 in both polarities, against `"auto"`'s 0.0711 light on dark and 0.0588 dark on light, and strength 30's 0.0588 in both. `ui_font_thicken: false` takes panel dark-on-light text from 0.0588 to 0.0511 while a terminal grid inside it stays at 0.0588.
- Settings window, Text > Rendering page: under Interface text, a "Thicken interface text" picker; under Terminal text, a "Thicken" picker. Each picks Auto, On or Off (`choicePicker`, `THICKENS`) and shows a "Thicken strength" stepper (steps of 16, so it lands on each smoothing level above) while it's On. Auto and 255 leave their keys out of settings.json; On and Off write `true` and `false`. Layouts set neither, so the rows never say a layout sets its own.
- Files: `src/settings.rs`, `src/terminal.rs` (`glyph_thicken`, `paint_grid`), `src/window.rs` (`AppWindow::render`), `vendor/gpui-pre/src/{text_system.rs,window.rs}`, `vendor/gpui-pre-macos/src/text_system.rs`, `vendor/gpui-pre-{wgpu,windows}` (the `RenderGlyphParams::thicken` field), `tests/thicken_ink.rs`, `tests/text_contrast.rs`, `tests/subpixel_text.rs`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`
- Upstream: pull request #547 (`feat/font-thicken-setting`: `font_thicken`, `font_thicken_strength` and the off value, terminal text only), awaiting review. `ui_font_thicken`, the Settings window rows and subpixel thickening are fork only.

### Text contrast

`feat/text-contrast`: `text_contrast` and `ui_text_contrast` (0 to 1, default 0), person settings that sharpen glyph edges when they're blended, without widening the strokes. The monochrome-sprite fragment shader moves the glyph's coverage toward smoothstep by the setting: `alpha' = mix(alpha, alpha² (3 − 2 alpha), contrast)`. The curve is symmetric about 0.5, so faint edge pixels fade, nearly solid ones fill, and a stroke keeps its ink. GPUI's wgpu and DirectX backends already apply Zed's DirectWrite-style `enhance_contrast`, `alpha (k + 1) / (alpha k + 1)`, with a global, luminance-scaled `k` and a gamma correction. The Metal backend has no curve. Zed's curve is not reused here: it raises every partial pixel, so it adds ink as thickening does. Measured on Metal at k = 1, it added 14% ink to light on dark and 17% to dark on light. Its luminance factor is 0 for light text, so it would leave light-on-dark terminal text unchanged. The wgpu and DirectX backends apply the S-curve after their own correction, so 0 leaves every backend as it was.

`HERDR:` changes are in `vendor/gpui-pre/src/scene.rs` and its cbindgen copy `vendor/gpui-pre-apple/vendor/gpui/src/scene.rs`. They add `MonochromeSprite::contrast` and an 8-byte-aligning `contrast_pad`, making the sprite 30 words. They are also in `vendor/gpui-pre/src/window.rs` (`with_glyph_contrast`, `set_glyph_contrast`, and `paint_glyph` stamping the sprite), and in the `sharpen_coverage` function in `vendor/gpui-pre-apple/src/shaders.metal`, `vendor/gpui-pre-wgpu/src/shaders.wgsl` and `vendor/gpui-pre-windows/src/shaders.hlsl`. The WebGL decoder reads 30 words. On DirectX, subpixel sprites read their own `SubpixelSprite` buffer, which has no `contrast`, instead of `MonochromeSprite`'s. Contrast is applied at blend time, so it isn't in the glyph atlas key: interface and terminal glyphs share tiles. SVG icons draw at 0. `AppWindow::render` sets the window's contrast from `ui_text_contrast`, beside `set_glyph_thicken`. The terminal paints its grid inside `with_glyph_contrast(text_contrast)`. The row cache keeps shaped lines, not sprites, and re-reads the contrast each paint, so `painted_with` doesn't carry it.

- Measured (`tests/text_contrast.rs`, JetBrains Mono at 12 device px, headless Metal). Edge share is the share of inked pixels with coverage between 0.2 and 0.8, and ink is the mean coverage over the text's box:

| Text | Contrast 0 | 0.5 | 1 | Ink at 0 → 1 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Light on dark | 0.440 | 0.390 | 0.360 | 0.0711 → 0.0719 (+1.1%) |
| Dark on light | 0.503 | 0.433 | 0.374 | 0.0588 → 0.0590 (+0.3%) |

- For comparison, `font_thicken` 255 takes dark-on-light ink to 0.0711 (+21%). `ui_text_contrast` 1 takes the panels' edge share from 0.440 to 0.360, while a terminal grid inside it at 0 stays at 0.440.
- Settings window, Text > Rendering page: under Interface text, an "Interface contrast" stepper; under Terminal text, a "Contrast" stepper below Thicken. Both step by 0.1 within 0 to 1. At 0, the key is left out of settings.json. Layouts set neither.
- Files: `src/settings.rs`, `src/terminal.rs` (the grid's paint), `src/window.rs` (`AppWindow::render`), `vendor/gpui-pre/src/{scene.rs,window.rs}`, `vendor/gpui-pre-apple/{src/shaders.metal,vendor/gpui/src/scene.rs}`, `vendor/gpui-pre-wgpu/src/{shaders.wgsl,shaders_webgl.wgsl,wgpu_renderer.rs}`, `vendor/gpui-pre-windows/src/shaders.hlsl`, `tests/text_contrast.rs`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`
- Upstream: fork only


### Pane names

`feat/pane-rename` (for issue #322, pull request #384): `pane.rename {pane?, name}` names a pane like `tab.rename` and `space.rename`, asking with the shipped prompt when there's no name; an empty name goes back to the program's own. The name is the pane block's `title` in its tab's tree, so it saves and syncs with the layout document. `panes()` exposes it as `name`, and the shipped `pane` and `terminals-pane` headers show it.

- Files: `src/commands.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/model.rs`, `src/mirror.rs` (tests), `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/mods/pane/main.js`, `defaults/mods/terminals-pane/main.js`, `docs/commands.md`
- Upstream: pull request #384, for issue #322 (which asks for `terminal.rename`)

### Automatic pane names

`feat/pane-auto-names` (branched from `master`, fork only): with `pane_auto_names` on, a pane at a bare shell that nobody named shows two calm words of its own, such as "hazy-inlet", in the flavour of Rex's session names, instead of the shell's name. Shells all read "zsh", so a split of three shells had nothing to tell them apart by until someone renamed them; a stable name gives each one an identity for free.

- Precedence: the person's `pane.rename` name, then an agent or a running command, and the automatic name only at a bare shell whose title says nothing: empty, the terminal's address (its title before the shell sets one), the shell's name (`zsh`, `-zsh`, `/bin/zsh`), or only a folder (`~/src`, `/tmp`, `C:\src`, the cwd or its last part, any of those after `user@host:`). Decided once in Rust (`pane_names::shown`, which knows the shell list in `programs.json`), so every mod gets the same answer: `panes()[i].auto_name` is the name when the pane should show it and null otherwise (always null with the setting off, and for mod panes). The shipped `pane` and `terminals-pane` headers read `label` (see Pane and agent labels), where `auto_name` comes second.
- Stable: 64-bit FNV-1a (Fowler–Noll–Vo hash) over the pane id's bytes (fixed, unlike std's per-process SipHash keys), modulo adjectives × nouns. Two panes of one tab that land on the same name: the one whose id sorts later takes the next index in the list. Pane-id order rather than tree order, since pane ids are random and stable while a pane's place in the tree changes when panes move. Every pane of the tab takes part, whether it shows its name or not, so a neighbour starting an agent never moves another pane's name. The same layout gives the same names after a restart. Known limit, accepted: a new split whose id sorts earlier and lands on an existing pane's name takes it, and the existing pane's header shows the next name until that split closes (rare: about 1 in 2,304 per extra pane in a tab). Keeping first-come names would need per-pane state that survives restarts, which the display-only design avoids.
- Display only: never written into the layout or settings.json. `pane.rename`'s prompt starts from the automatic name (hint "empty: its automatic name"), so Enter keeps it as the pane's real name.
- Words: `defaults/pane-names.json` (`{"adjectives": [...], "nouns": [...]}`, 48 × 48, three to seven letters, landscape, weather and light, British spelling, nothing that reads as slang, as a judgement, or as a pane's state next to a status dot), embedded with `include_str!` and checked once when first read (non-empty lists, lower-case words, no duplicates); a file that fails disables the names with a warning rather than crashing. Our own list: Rex's (seen only as strings in its binary, licence unknown) set the flavour, none of its words are reused, the test fixtures included.
- Setting: `pane_auto_names` in settings.json, off by default (left out of the file when off); Settings > Appearance > Panes, "Name panes automatically". Turning it on or off republishes `panes()` at once.
- Perf: nothing in the paint path. The names are worked out in `publish()`, which runs on model changes and title changes, never per frame: one FNV pass over each pane id and a small map per tab. The header mods only read a string from `panes()`. The window keeps the shown names by pane (`auto_names`) for the rename prompt.
- Tests: `pane_names::tests` (the shipped word file holds up, bad word files are refused, a fixed id gives a fixed name, the collision step by id order and its independence from listing order, the generic-title rule and precedence), `window::tests::a_tabs_panes_keep_their_automatic_names_when_a_neighbour_starts_a_command` (four ids that share a name: every pane of the tab takes part, mod panes included, other tabs don't, a mod pane shows none, a neighbour starting a command moves nobody's name), `window::tests::unnamed_panes_at_a_shell_show_an_automatic_name` (off by default, on publishes it, a running command and a renamed pane get none, the rename prompt starts from it, off takes it away, never written as the pane's name), `mods::render_tests::pane_headers_show_the_automatic_name_after_the_persons` (and the old fallback without one), `scripts/settings.test.ts` (the toggle writes and leaves off out, shows on only when set, found by search under Panes), `settings::tests::the_guide_explains_every_setting`.
- Files: `src/pane_names.rs`, `defaults/pane-names.json`, `src/window.rs`, `src/settings.rs`, `src/programs.rs`, `src/mods.rs` (test reader), `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `src/main.rs`, `src/commands.rs`, `defaults/mods/pane/main.js`, `defaults/mods/terminals-pane/main.js`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `docs/commands.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Tab and pane icons

`feat/tab-pane-icons` (branched from `master`, fork only): panes and tabs get icons, resolved once in Rust and published as `icon` (`{kind: "glyph" | "emoji" | "letter", text}`, or null) on `panes()`, `agents()` and each tab in `spaces()`, plus `own_icon` (the choice) on panes and tabs. Every mod draws the same answer; null means "draw the program's icon".

- Pane, first that applies: an icon chosen with `pane.set_icon`; with `space_avatar` glyph, emoji or repo (glyph), a bare shell showing its `auto_name`: the icon of its noun (meadow: 🌾 or md-barley), which every noun has; else null (an agent or command's program icon, a bare shell's terminal icon). Tab: its `tab.set_icon` choice, else its focused pane's icon. A letter choice shows the first letter or digit of the pane's label, or of the tab's name (an unnamed tab: its focused pane's label, a mod pane's too); tabs publish it as `letter` so the picker previews the same one.
- Why Rust and not a JS picker: the shipped `tabs`, `pane` and `menu` mods cannot share code with the person's chezmoi mods (no imports outside a mod's folder, no chezmoi includes in the fork), and a choice has to live where every client and every mod reads one answer. Resolving in `publish` keeps the rule in one place, costs nothing per frame, and lets `herdr-app` scripts set icons.
- Choices: `pane.set_icon {pane?, icon}`, `tab.set_icon {id?, icon}` (null or an absent `icon` clears, as the palette runs them with no arguments; so a misspelt key such as `Icon` clears too). Kept in `Model::icons` and in each machine's layout document (`Document.icons`, by local id), so they persist across restarts and other clients see them. `Document::capture` writes only ids still in that machine's spaces, so an icon goes with its pane or tab the next time that machine's document is written, and a machine whose document isn't loaded keeps its own. A machine's icons are its document's: `Document::replace` swaps all of that machine's icons (by id prefix, not only those of the spaces it had), and a machine leaving the machine list takes its icons with its spaces (`Model::retain_machines`), so an icon cleared elsewhere meanwhile isn't written back on its return. Reading never writes: `Model::icons_read` keeps each entry as the document held it, and `capture` writes that back until someone chooses or clears that icon, so entries this build can't read (a newer build's kind, or extra fields) survive and draw the automatic icon here, and a letter keeps the `value` this build ignores. Nothing migrates from the chezmoi `space.icons` (spaces keep theirs). A build without this feature that rewrites a machine's document drops its `icons`.
- Words: `defaults/pane-icons.json`, one emoji (single code point, emoji presentation) and one Symbols Nerd Font glyph (code point plus its font name) for each of the 48 nouns of `pane-names.json`: 45 distinct emoji, 45 distinct glyphs. Adjectives have none: every name ends in a noun, so an adjective or hash fallback would never be reached.
- Mods: `pane` header and `tabs` draw `icon` (glyph in Symbols Nerd Font, letter bold, 13 px) in place of the program's; `menu` adds Change icon… (pane: in-tab panes, after Zoom, also in the Shift+right-click menu; tab: after Rename and Move left and right) and Clear icon only while `own_icon` is set; an `icon-picker` dialog with Glyph, Emoji and Letter tabs over the space avatar sets (60 glyphs, 60 emoji).
- Perf: resolved in `publish` only; `pane_icons::RESOLVED` counts it in tests.
- Tests: `pane_icons::tests` (shipped file vs the noun list and the font's glyph names, bad files, the noun's icon for every automatic name, choice wins, letter mode null, choice parsing), `mirror::tests::{chosen_icons_are_kept_with_their_machines_document, a_closed_pane_or_tabs_icon_goes_with_it, a_machine_back_in_the_list_takes_its_icons_from_its_document, icons_this_build_cant_read_survive_a_read}`, `window::tests::{panes_and_tabs_show_their_automatic_or_chosen_icon, pane_icons_follow_changes_not_frames}`, `mods::render_tests::{null_icons_draw_every_shipped_mod_as_before, pane_header_and_tabs_draw_an_icon_in_place_of_the_programs, pane_and_tab_menus_offer_change_and_clear_icon, icon_picker_shows_the_sets_and_the_choice}`.
- Files: `src/pane_icons.rs`, `defaults/pane-icons.json`, `src/model.rs`, `src/mirror.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/commands.rs`, `src/mods.rs`, `src/pane_names.rs`, `defaults/mods/{pane,tabs,menu,icon-picker}`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `docs/commands.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Icon search

`feat/icon-search` (branched from `master`, fork only): the `icon-picker` searches every Symbols Nerd Font glyph and every emoji by name.

- Tabs: Suggested (the 60 glyphs and 60 emoji, first), Glyphs (every glyph, a heading and a chip per set: Material Design, Font Awesome, Font Awesome Extension, Codicons, Octicons, Devicons, Seti UI, Custom, Weather, Linux, Powerline (pl and ple), Pomicons, IEC power, Other), Emoji (every fully qualified emoji, a chip per group, no skin tone variants or components), Letter. The search box filters everything (on Glyphs or Emoji, that tab and its chip): exact names, then names starting with the query, then names holding it, shorter names first; queries, names and keywords compare folded (lower case, accents dropped, any run of punctuation or spaces as one `_`), so `gb` finds 🇬🇧 by its `GB` keyword, `family man` finds `family: man, woman, boy` and `curacao` finds `flag: Curaçao`, and emoji keywords count (an exact keyword as a prefix). Typing on the Letter tab, which has nothing to search, moves to Suggested. A tile's tooltip and the footer name it and its set. Type to search, arrows to move (by row and column), Enter to pick, Esc to close. A picker picks once: the search box reports Enter as `submit` and passes it on to the grid's `on_key_down`, and only the first counts.
- Data: `defaults/mods/icon-picker/names/glyphs.json` (Nerd Fonts 3.5.1 `glyphnames.json`, the bundled font's release: 10,617 rows of `[name, hex, prefix, other names?]`, one per code point, aliases folded into the fourth field; 347 KB, 78 KB gzipped) and `emoji.json` (emojibase-data 16.0.3 `en/compact.json`, with CLDR (Unicode Common Locale Data Repository) names and keywords: 1,906 rows of `[emoji, name, keywords, group]`; 111 KB). Built by `scripts/icon-names.ts`, which reads the font's cmap and keeps only code points it has (all of them, at 3.5.1). Licences: `names/LICENSES.md`, `LICENSE-NerdFonts.txt` (MIT), `LICENSE-emojibase.txt` (MIT) and the Unicode License, in `licenses/inventory.json` and THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.
- Loading: the files ship inside the mod folder and are read with `readTextFile` when the picker opens (off the UI thread), never at start; Suggested shows meanwhile. Each file stands alone: one missing or unparseable leaves the other's tiles and names, and a line says which names it couldn't read ("Couldn't read the icon names." when neither). Each tab's tiles are rows of a `v_virtual_list`, so only rows on screen are built, and the shown list is kept until its tab, chip, query or data changes.
- Other mods' icons: props `{shared, id, name, letter?, now?, extra?}` write the pick to `shared(shared)[id]`, so the person's own space menu uses this one picker instead of a copy.
- Perf: `mods::render_tests::icon_picker_reads_every_name_and_searches_them_quickly` times open, reading and parsing the names, and a keystroke's search and description; `scripts/icon-picker.test.ts` times parse and search under Bun.

### Tab reordering

`feat/tab-reorder` (branched from `master`, fork only): tabs reorder by dragging, from their menu and by keys. The shipped `tabs` mod moved a tab only when a press on one tab was released over another, with nothing on screen while it happened, and a release in a gap or past the last tab did nothing.

- Drag: a press on a tab becomes a drag once the pointer moves more than 4 px (`DRAG_THRESHOLD`); a click without that movement still selects (on release over the tab it was pressed on) and a double click still renames. While dragging, the tab dims in its place, a copy of it (the ghost) follows the pointer across the strip, kept where it was grabbed, and a 2 px accent line marks the gap it will land in. Dropping anywhere on the strip moves it there: over a tab, its left half means before it and its right half after; the gaps keep the last place; the strip's padding left of the first tab means first; the empty space after the last tab, the buttons and anything right of it mean last. No line shows where nothing would change. Escape or the pointer leaving the strip cancels; the release after that does nothing. A release the strip never heard ends the drag too: the next move without the left button held, or the next press.
- How: gpui-kit's drag support is the dock's (`drag_tab`), which this strip isn't, so the mod tracks `on_mouse_move` on each tab (its half, from `local_position` against `bounds`), on a `tab-tail` spacer after the tabs, on the `tab-scroll` row (its bounds) and on the strip root (left of the row: first; right of it: last). There is no `on_mouse_up_out`, so leaving the strip (`on_hover(false)`) is what cancels a drag released outside it; a mouse move's `pressed_button` (a `HERDR:` change in gpui-kit's `dispatch_mouse_move`, `vendor/gpui-kit/crates/shell/src/engine/quickjs/mod.rs`, typed in `typings.rs`) ends one whose release went missing. Esc needs the keyboard: a drag focuses the strip's own handle, and `pane.refocus` gives it back to what has the focus, as closing a popup does (`restore_focus`): a focused mod pane gets its `focus` command again, else the focused pane, or the agent shown, without changing what it shows (`pane.focus` would end a swapped-in sub-agent and reset the agent shown; hidden from the palette). The wheel doesn't switch tabs during a drag. `slotOver` and `dropIndex` are the pure halves (exported for the tests).
- Menu: the tab menu has Move left and Move right after Rename, each hidden at its end.
- Keys: `tab.move_left {id?}` and `tab.move_right {id?}` move a tab (default: the shown one) one place; at the end nothing happens. Defaults: Cmd+Shift+Left / Cmd+Shift+Right on macOS (Cmd+Shift+[ / ] already switch tabs; gpui-kit's text inputs keep Cmd+Shift+Left / Right for selecting, their context binding winning), no default elsewhere: Alt+Shift+[ / ] are Meta-{ / Meta-} to terminal programs (emacs and readline paragraph moves, bash's `complete-into-braces`), and GPUI may report the key as `{`, which would never match. `src/keys/shipped/fork-0990622-keys{,.macos}.json` are the defaults before the macOS keys, so untouched keys.json files gain them; `fork-1225b24-keys.json` is the Linux and Windows file that had Alt+Shift+[ / ], so an untouched one loses them; edited ones keep theirs.
- `tab.move` lives in `Model::move_tab`, which fails on a pane id instead of panicking on it.
- Tests: `model::tests::tabs_move_to_a_place_or_one_step_and_stop_at_the_ends`, `window::tests::{pane_text_zoom (pane.refocus keeps a swapped-in terminal), pane_refocus_hands_a_focused_mod_block_its_keyboard_back}`, `keys::tests::untouched_fork_keys_give_alt_shift_brackets_back_to_programs`, `mods::render_tests::tabs_draw_a_drag_in_progress`, `scripts/tabs.test.ts` (slot halves, drop index for every slot, click versus drag, gaps and both ends, Escape and leaving cancel, a lost release, a tab closed mid-drag, menu moves hidden at the ends).
- Files: `defaults/mods/{tabs,menu}/main.js`, `defaults/frame/palette/main.js`, `src/model.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/commands.rs`, `src/keys.rs`, `src/keys/shipped/fork-0990622-keys{,.macos}.json`, `src/keys/shipped/fork-1225b24-keys.json`, `defaults/keys{,.macos}.json`, `vendor/gpui-kit/crates/shell/src/{engine/quickjs/mod.rs,typings.rs}`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `docs/commands.md`, `scripts/tabs.test.ts`
- Upstream: the `Model::move_tab` guard is pull request #752 (`up/tab-move-pane-id`), awaiting review; the rest none yet

### Toasts fit their content

`feat/toast-fit-content` (branched from `master`, fork only): a toast is as wide as its content, 200 to 340 px (titles still truncate at 340), and 40 px high without a body, 72 with one (a body as the toast mod draws one: text that isn't blank, or any other value JavaScript calls true); an explicit `height` still wins, and toasts keep their stack's anchor (`toast_position`). `fix/toast-content-fill`: the toast mod's root fills the pill (inside its border) and its text is vertically centred; a fitted `ShellRoot` grows to its container when `set_fill_fitted` is on, which only toasts use, so fitted popups and floats are unchanged. A keyed (`.id(...)`) script element records its bounds under that id in debug test builds, so tests can measure mod content.

- Files: `src/window.rs` (`toast_height`, `open_overlay`, `render_overlays`, tests `toast_sizes` and `a_toast_is_two_lines_high_only_with_a_body_the_mod_draws`), `defaults/mods/toast/main.js` (`hasBody`), `vendor/gpui-kit/crates/shell/src/root.rs` (`fill_fitted`), `vendor/gpui-kit/crates/shell/src/materialize.rs` (debug selector for keyed elements), `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: issue https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr-app/issues/685 proposes it, with the branch `abhijit-s:feat/toast-fit-content-upstream` ready.

### Copied toast on copy-on-select

`fix/contrast-copied-toast` (branched from `master`, fork only): when a mouse selection in a terminal is copied on release (upstream's `copy_on_select`, on unless settings.json sets it to false), a "Copied" success toast (1.5 s) says so; the next copy replaces it (`AppWindow::show_copied`, as upstream #416 does), so quick selections never stack toasts. Only the terminal knows a copy happened: `TerminalView::select` emits `TerminalEvent::Copied` when `copy` wrote non-empty text, and `AppWindow` opens the toast. A mod can't tell: `on_mouse_down_out` hears every press in the window, Settings and other dialogs included, and `run("terminal.copy")` returns nothing, so a mod that guessed a selection from repeated presses toasted on every quick press of a Settings stepper.

- Files: `src/terminal.rs` (`TerminalEvent::Copied`, `select`, `copy`), `src/window.rs` (the terminal subscription, test `copy_on_select_says_copied_once_and_settings_presses_do_not`)
- Upstream: none yet

### Copy on select switch

`feat/copy-on-select-switch` (branched from `master`): a "Copy on select" switch for upstream's `copy_on_select`, which had no Settings window row, on the Input > Mouse page under Focus follows mouse. Like the fork's other switches it writes the key only away from its default: off writes `copy_on_select: false`, on takes the key out of settings.json.

- Tests: `scripts/settings.test.ts` ("copy on select sits on the Mouse page"), `mods::render_tests::copy_on_select_toggle_shows_the_setting`.
- Files: `defaults/frame/settings/main.js` (`followsMouse`), `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? Yes: the terminal copies on release only while it is on (`TerminalView::select`). (2) Can only the app see it? No: it is a settings.json key the Settings mod writes. (3) Can a mod not do it? The Settings window is a mod and does it. Verdict: A, upstream as is: a row for a setting upstream already has. Upstream's Settings window has no Mouse page, so the upstream branch, `feat/copy-on-select-upstream`, puts the row with the terminal rows on its Look page.

### Toasts stand out, and go where you put them

`feat/toast-look` (branched from `master`, fork only): on `popover` with the panel `border`, toasts were barely apart from the window on many themes (ayu-mirage: surface 1.16:1 and border 1.43:1 on the background; ayu-light: 1.00:1 and 1.14:1), so they were easy to miss. They draw from their own colors, computed once in `settings::resolve` each time the theme resolves (`toast_colors`), never per toast or per frame. The colors are `toast` (the surface), `toast_border`, `toast_shadow`, `toast_foreground`, `toast_muted`, and a level stripe and dot color per level (`toast_info`, `toast_success`, `toast_warning`, `toast_error`). Each is the smallest step that holds a measurable floor:

- **Surface:** on a dark theme, at least 1.3:1 on `background` (`TOAST_LIFT`), stepped toward the terminal's foreground, else white. On a light theme it is white: a white background can't show a lighter surface, so the border and shadow carry it there.
- **Border:** at least 1.7:1 on the background (`TOAST_EDGE`) and 1.25:1 on the surface (`TOAST_SEAM`).
- **Title and body:** at least 4.5:1 on the surface (`TOAST_TEXT`).
- **Level stripe and dot:** at least 3:1 on the surface (`TOAST_MARK`), from `accent`, `done`, `working` and `blocked`.
- **Shadow:** two layers, deeper on dark themes.
- **Overrides:** a theme's or settings.json's own `toast*` colors win, and the rest are held to the `toast` actually drawn. A theme that paints its own panels (Win98) keeps its `popover` and `border` where they hold the floors.
- **Translucent values:** a `#rrggbbaa` value counts as drawn, composited over the background (the toast) or over the toast (everything else), in `toast_colors` and in the `toast_problems` check (`drawn_over`).
- **Results:** ayu-mirage goes to 1.32:1 (surface) and 1.75:1 (border); ayu-light to 1.05:1 and 1.74:1. Every preset, shipped file, public extreme and installed Rex theme holds every floor.

`toast_position` in settings.json (`bottom-right` default, `bottom-left`, `bottom-center`, `top-right`, `top-left`, `top-center`) places the stack, and the core-update notice follows it.

- **Stacking:** bottom stacks grow up with the oldest nearest the edge. Top stacks grow down with the newest nearest the edge.
- **Top chrome:** a top stack starts 16 px below the window's top chrome: the system title strip (where the layout's root starts) plus the lowest `window_drag` bar along the top, as painted. The bounds canvases every layout node already has record it (`top_chrome`, `AppWindow::top_chrome`), so no tree walk runs per render. Bars stacked along the top (a tab bar under a title bar) count, each starting where the chrome so far ends. Only the main layout's nodes count, not an overlay's. While a top toast shows, an O(1) probe, drawn last, asks for one more frame (`on_next_frame`; `refresh` is ignored while painting) if this frame painted a different chrome (zoom, a bar shown or hidden).
- **Offset range:** each of `toast_offset`'s values is kept within -16 to 400 px (`TOAST_OFFSET`), and a value outside is reported as a settings problem.
- **Offset:** `toast_offset {x, y}` moves the stack in from the side and from its edge. The whole stack moves together (`toast_start` clamps once, at the edge), and never under the update pill. The pill is reserved for right and centre stacks only.
- **Settings window:** "Notification position" (Rex's label) on Appearance > Window, a small window of six places in its top row and bottom row, the chosen one the tonal pill.

- Tests:
  - `settings::derivation_tests::toasts_stand_off_every_theme` checks every floor over the presets, shipped files and 7 public extremes, under tinted, shaded and classic panels, plus own-toast and Win98 cases. `theme_file_problems` includes `toast_problems`, so `rex_themes::tests::the_installed_rex_themes_all_load` holds Rex's themes to them.
  - `settings::tests::toast_position_and_offset_parse`.
  - `window::tests::toast_sizes`, extended: every position, with and without an offset, its edge, side and stacking direction.
  - `window::tests::{toasts_stack_away_from_their_edge, a_toast_offset_moves_the_stack_and_keeps_clear_of_the_pill, the_top_chrome_is_the_lowest_bar_along_the_top, top_toasts_sit_below_the_title_and_tab_bars}` (the last: below a 40 px drag bar and the system strip, at zoom 1 and 1.25).
  - `scripts/settings.test.ts` covers the picker.
- Files: `src/settings.rs` (`toast_colors`, `step_until`, `toast_problems`, `ToastPosition`, `ToastSide`, `ToastOffset`), `src/window.rs` (`toast_frame`, `toast_start`, `toast_stack`, `at_toast_spot`, `render_overlays`), `defaults/mods/toast/main.js`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`
- Upstream: none yet

### Toast time, style, corners and level mark

`feat/toast-prefs` (branched from `master`, fork only: it builds on "Toasts stand out, and go where you put them" and "Toasts coalesced and capped"): four settings.json keys, each left out when it holds today's look, so a settings.json without them draws toasts exactly as before.

- **`toast_duration`** (`short`, `normal` default, `long`, `until-dismissed`): scales the level's own time (5 s, or 8 s for errors and warnings, which include "needs you" alerts) by 0.6, 1 or 2, or keeps the toast until clicked. A `timeout` the poster set wins unscaled: the caller chose it, and that keeps the 1.5 s "Copied" brief. Until clicked doesn't pile toasts up or starve new ones: past five, `close_toasts_past_limit` closes the oldest routine toast, else the oldest error or warning, but never the one just opened, so a "Copied" after five kept errors still shows. A toast already open keeps the time it opened with. The core-update notice already stays until an update goes through. Expiry is still the `expires` instant the window's 400 ms poll checks (`toast_timeout`, now `Option`), set when a toast opens or an alert joins it, so nothing new runs per frame, and the mod roots' toast clock (upstream #548) is a different, gpui-kit toast that this doesn't touch.
- **`toast_style`** (`standard` default, `subtle`, `high-contrast`, `accent`): a set of floors (`ToastFloors`) and a starting surface for `toast_colors`, which still runs once per theme resolve, never per frame. Standard is the derivation as it was, value for value (`tests/fixtures/master-toasts.json`, from `master`). Subtle starts from the panels' `popover` where it lifts 1.12:1 off a dark background, with a border at 1.35:1 and 1.1:1 and a lighter shadow. High contrast holds the border at 3:1 on the background and 2:1 on the toast, text at 7:1 (WCAG AAA (Web Content Accessibility Guidelines, enhanced level)) and marks at 4.5:1, with a deeper shadow; it doesn't start from a theme's own `popover` (a mid-grey one, `#6b6b6b` on a dark theme, tops out near 5.3:1), and under a `toast` colour the person set it holds 7:1 where that surface allows. Accent mixes the theme's `accent` into the surface (18% dark, 10% light) and lifts it again if that sank it. Every style keeps text at 4.5:1 and marks at 3:1 or more. A theme's or settings.json's own `toast*` colors still win, and a theme that paints its own panels (Win98) keeps its popover, except under high contrast.
- **`toast_corners`** (`square` 0 px, `slight` 4 px, `rounded` 8 px default): the radius `toast_frame` draws, for mod toasts and the core-update notice alike. Herdr draws the toast's box, so this stays in Rust beside the surface and border.
- **`toast_mark`** (`stripe` default, `dot`, `none`): the level mark. The toast mod draws the stripe and dot, so the choice reaches it as `props().toast_mark`, which `open_overlay` and `join_alert_toast` put in every toast's props (`with_toast_mark`), so a toast's render reads no settings; a replacement toast mod reads it the same way. The core-update notice in Rust follows it. Stripe and dot keep their 3:1 floor (4.5:1 under high contrast). With `none` the level shows only in the wording ("needs you", "failed"): coloured titles would need a fourth set of level colours held to 4.5:1 for a look the person picked to be plain.
- **Separate keys, not one preset:** corners and marks are shape and the style is colour, so each reads as one small picker and switching the style never changes a shape someone chose.
- **Settings window:** on Appearance > Window under "Notification position": "Notification time" (Short, Normal, Long, Until clicked), "Notification style" (each style drawn as a small toast in the current theme's colours for that style, with the chosen corners and mark, from `toastLooks()`: each style's colours and each corner radius, resolved once per `settings::set` and read only by the Settings window, so the `settings()` every mod reads carries none of it), "Notification corners" and "Notification level mark". Each applies live: colours and corners redraw every open toast on the next frame; the mark and the time apply to toasts opened from then on, and open ones keep theirs.
- **Bad values:** like `toast_position`, a value outside the list fails settings.json, which the settings problems report.
- **Upstream's three questions:** (1) Does the app act on it? Duration yes: the window decides when a toast expires, and the toast clock is the window's. Style, corners and marks no: they only change how toasts look. (2) Can only the app see it? Duration yes (expiry is the window's poll); the colours are derived in Rust today, but a mod could compute them from `theme()`. (3) Can a mod not do it? A mod can't change when Herdr's own toasts expire, so duration is a core setting; the toast mod can draw any mark (it already gets `toast_mark` in its props) and a mod could restyle colours with `frame: "none"`, so style, corners and mark bend the rule the way "Toasts stand out" already does. Verdict: duration C (ask Can, with an issue first); style, corners and mark D (fork only, rule-bending like `toast_colors`).

- Tests: `settings::derivation_tests::{toasts_stand_off_every_theme (every style over every theme, panel derivation and shade, a black theme with a near-black accent, high contrast over a mid-grey own popover, and own colours winning in every style), standard_toasts_are_the_derivation_as_it_was, toast_styles_differ_as_named}`, `settings::tests::toast_choices_parse`, `window::tests::{toast_lifetimes, toast_duration_and_mark_reach_new_toasts}` (the second also: five errors kept until clicked, then "Copied" shows), `scripts/settings.test.ts` (the four rows, their writes and previews, search), `scripts/toast.test.ts` (each mark).
- Files: `src/settings.rs` (`ToastDuration`, `ToastStyle`, `ToastFloors`, `ToastCorners`, `ToastMark`, `toast_colors`, `toast_problems_for`, `resolve_toasts_as`, `toast_looks`), `src/mods.rs` (`toastLooks`), `src/window.rs` (`toast_timeout`, `open_overlay`, `join_alert_toast`, `with_toast_mark`, `close_toasts_past_limit`, `toast_frame`, `render_overlays`), `defaults/mods/toast/main.js`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `scripts/toast.test.ts`, `justfile`, `scripts/bench.py` (`--set toasts`: an idle baseline, a burst of 15 agents finishing within a second every five seconds, and a storm of about 30 finishes a second; `--settings` for non-default settings), `tests/fixtures/master-toasts.json`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`
- Upstream: none

### Toast designs and countdown

`feat/toast-designs` (branched from `master`, fork only: it builds on "Toast time, style, corners and level mark"): toasts were one card in different colours. `toast_design` picks a shape, orthogonal to `toast_style` (colours), and `toast_progress` adds a countdown bar to any design. Both are left out of settings.json at their defaults, and with neither set toasts are drawn as before: the card path in the toast mod and the frame are unchanged, and the standard fixture (`tests/fixtures/master-toasts.json`) still matches, because only the outline and terminal designs change the derived colours.

- **Designs** (`card` default, `pill`, `terminal`, `outline`, `icon-card`, `banner`). The toast mod draws each inside from `props().toast_design`; Rust sizes, frames and places it (`toast_box`, `toast_frame`, `render_overlays`), each decision where it already lived (FORK-ADR-001).
  - **Pill:** 32 px, round ends, the dot (a stripe becomes a dot) or icon and the title; the body is its tooltip, so nothing is lost and the pill stays one line.
  - **Terminal:** the terminal's font (`toast_font` in the props, so the mod reads no settings), `[INFO]` `[DONE]` `[WARN]` `[ERR]` in the terminal's own blue, green, yellow and red (`ansi4`, `ansi2`, `ansi3`, `ansi1`), square corners; card heights.
  - **Outline:** a see-through backing and a 1 px border and title in the level's colour. Readability over arbitrary window content can't be promised with no backing, so it keeps one: `toast_ghost`, the toast surface at the least opacity (in steps of 5/255) under which the title, body and level colours hold their floors with 0.1 to spare (`TOAST_FIT_MARGIN`, for the renderer's colour conversion, about 1/255 a channel) over a black and a white window behind. The outline paints no hover fill, which would lighten what shows through. Compositing is per channel and luminance rises with each channel, so black and white bound every colour that can show through; `over_anything` also requires the text to sit outside that span. Each level and the body step at most halfway toward the title's colour to get there, so the levels keep their hue (paler than the card's). Over the presets, shipped files and public extremes the backing is 82 to 94% opaque on dark themes and 73 to 75% on light ones (graphite `#2d2f34dc`, paper `#ffffffb9`); where nothing below opaque holds it is the opaque surface.
  - **Icon card:** 300 to 400 px, a 36 px level icon (`mods/toast/{info,check,warning,error}.svg` in the toast surface's colour on the level's, 3:1 by construction), title and body, and Show (runs the action, e.g. agent alerts' `agent.focus`; `action.label` renames it) and Dismiss when there's an action: 76 px, 112 with buttons.
  - **Banner:** a 40 px strip across the window with title, place (the body), "+N more", Show and Dismiss. It takes the vertical half of `toast_position` (its side does nothing), sits flush on that edge, a top one below the top chrome, and `toast_offset.y` moves it in (for a status bar) while `x` does nothing. A bottom banner sits above the update pill, as bottom-right toasts do. Banners show one at a time, counting the rest: several full-width strips would bury the window. The one shown is the newest error or warning, else the newest (`shown_banner`), so an error never waits behind routine banners, as the cap already closes routine toasts first. One waiting behind doesn't count down (the poll skips it, `waiting_banner`) and gets its whole time when it comes forward or toasts stop being banners (`count_banners`); the cap of five still applies. The core-update notice shows as a second strip past it.
  - **Corners and marks:** `ToastDesign::radius` and `ToastDesign::mark` alone decide where `toast_corners` and `toast_mark` apply (corners: card, outline, icon card; marks: card, pill, banner; the icon card's level icon is its mark). The toast's props carry the mark as its design takes it, and `toastLooks().takes` (`ToastDesign::takes`) tells the Settings window which rows to hide, as it hides Sidebar shade under classic panels.
  - **Live:** a new design (or a new terminal font under the terminal design) redraws open toasts in it (`redesign_toasts`, through `set_toast_props`, which joining alerts share), so the box and the mod always agree.
- **Colours:** every design draws only the theme's derived toast colours: `toast_frame_colors` (fill, border, shadow per design) and the mod's `t.toast*` reads, the progress bar in the level colour. `ToastLook { style, design }` replaces the style alone as `toast_colors`' input: the terminal and outline designs hold their level colours to the text floor (4.5:1, 7:1 under high contrast) because they draw them as text, as do the icon card and banner, whose Show label is the surface's colour on the level's; the outline adds `toast_ghost`. Colours stay computed once per theme resolve.
- **Countdown (`toast_progress`):** a 2 px bar along the toast's bottom in its level colour (3:1 on the surface), in from the sides by the corner radius so the frame's clip never cuts it, as wide as the share of time left; the render checks the setting before reading the time (`time_left`, from `opened` and `expires`). None for a toast kept until clicked. GPUI's animation would draw the whole window every display frame; a toast's box can't present without a render (the spin-clock sprites only turn or cycle), so the cheapest correct path is a coarse step: `tick_progress` runs one timer while any toast counting down shows, about 48 steps over the shortest such toast, clamped to 2 to 10 a second (a 5 s toast steps at about 10 Hz, an 8 s one at 6 Hz, a long 16 s one at 3 Hz), and stops at the first step with none left or the setting off. Expiry is still the 400 ms poll, and the mod roots' toast clock (upstream #548) is untouched. Measured (`scripts/bench.py`, release builds, two alternating rounds against `master`, averages): unset, the branch matches `master` (toasts idle/burst/storm 0.4/6.1/8.4% app CPU and 3.6/3.8 window draws a second under load, against 1.0 (a warm-up outlier; 0.5 in round two)/5.7/8.7% and 3.2/3.9; spinners within noise in every scenario). With `toast_progress` on: idle unchanged (0.2 draws/s, so the timer is off with no toast), burst 3.6 → 8.1 draws/s, storm 3.8 → 11.8 draws/s, about +0.3 to 0.8 ms/s of render and +17% instructions in the storm, app CPU unchanged.
- **Settings window:** on Appearance > Window, "Notification design" draws each design as a small toast in the current theme's colours for that design and the chosen style (`toastLooks().designs`, with `font_family` for the terminal's), never `settings()`; "Notification countdown" is a toggle.
- **Upstream's three questions:** (1) Does the app act on it? Banner placement and stacking, and the countdown (the window's expiry clock), yes; the inside of each design, no. (2) Can only the app see it? Expiry and the window's chrome and pill, yes; the design's drawing, no: it's the toast mod's. (3) Can a mod not do it? A mod can't size a toast to the window's width, stack banners one at a time or know when a toast expires; it can draw any inside. Verdict: D (fork only), like the toast prefs it builds on; the countdown could be C (ask Can) once toast prefs are offered.
