### Terminal font fallbacks

`feat/faint-and-fallbacks` (branched from `master`, fork only): `font_fallbacks` in settings.json (a list of family names, default empty) are the families terminal text takes a character from, in order, when its font lacks it, before the system's choice. Without it CoreText picks for itself: U+23FA, Claude Code's message bullet, comes from STIX Two Math as a small dot, where Ghostty draws a full circle from its own fallback, ZedMono Nerd Font Mono. `Settings::compose` builds one GPUI `FontFallbacks` (None when empty) and `cell_font` shares it on every terminal font (regular, bold, italic). The cell font is part of `painted_with` and GPUI's font and layout caches key on the whole `Font`, so a change reshapes every row; each distinct list loads the family once more, which only edits add to.

- Platforms: GPUI's macOS text system turns the list into the font's `kCTFontCascadeListAttribute`, ahead of the system defaults; Windows' DirectWrite builds an `IDWriteFontFallback` from it ahead of the system's (`generate_font_fallbacks`); Linux's cosmic-text loads it as a fallback chain and takes each character from the first family covering it. Only macOS is verified.
- `HERDR:` change in `vendor/gpui-pre-macos/src/{open_type.rs,text_system.rs}`: the cascade asks each fallback family for the face's weight trait, read when the family loads. A variable face (the bundled JetBrains Mono) loads once, at 400, and `at_weight` copies it at other weights with the stale cascade, so bold cells got the fallback's Regular face. `at_weight` rebuilds the copy's features and cascade at the weight asked for (`apply_features_and_fallbacks` takes an optional CSS weight; `normalized_weight` maps it onto CoreText's trait scale as font-kit reads it back, 700 to 0.4; test `css_weights_map_onto_coretexts_weight_trait`, which `just check` doesn't run: `cargo test -p gpui-pre-macos`).
- Grid: runs of one-cell characters are shaped with the cell width forced per character. `fallback_fit` fits a one-cell fallback glyph whose ink (its typographic bounds, not its advance, which a symbol's ink often overflows) spills out of its cell, as Ghostty fits symbols (`.size = .fit`): ink wider or taller than the cell shrinks about its center until it fits, then ink still past an edge moves inside, left and top first (`fit_ink`); the row draws it at that size and offset (`RowOp::Text`'s `offset`). Ink already inside its cell is drawn as the font draws it. Unlike Ghostty, a symbol followed by a space is not given two cells. Wide (two-cell) characters are shaped unforced and unfitted. On macOS, U+23FA is drawn from the fonts like any other fallback symbol, not as Herdr's sprite (a disc 0.62 of the cell wide, smaller than Ghostty's glyph), and takes minimum contrast as other text does, as in Ghostty; elsewhere the sprite stays, since a fallback may lack it. Tests `fallback_ink_fits_its_cell_as_ghostty_fits_symbols` and, on CoreText with the system fallbacks, `macos_fallback_symbols_are_fitted_by_their_ink` (a `HERDR:` export of `MacTextSystem` from `vendor/gpui-pre-macos` lets a test shape off the main thread). Nerd Font icons (private use) the font lacks never use the list: `fallback_icon` fits them from the bundled Symbols Nerd Font first. The block cursor reshapes its cell's character unfitted. A family that isn't installed is skipped.
- Verified with probes on `MacTextSystem` (not kept: they need the font installed): "a⏺b" in bundled JetBrains Mono shapes U+23FA from `STIXTwoMath-Regular` without fallbacks; with `["ZedMono Nerd Font Mono"]`, from `ZedMonoNFM` at 400 and `ZedMonoNFM-Bold` at 700 (before the `at_weight` change, `ZedMonoNFM` at both). The following glyph stays at its cell's x.
- Settings window: doesn't set it.
- Files: `src/settings.rs` (test `font_fallbacks_default_empty_and_keep_their_order`), `src/sprites.rs` (`is_sprite`), `src/terminal.rs` (`cell_font`, `painted_with`, `fallback_fit`, `fit_ink`, tests `every_cell_font_falls_back_to_the_font_fallbacks`, `kept_rows_rebuild_when_faint_opacity_or_font_fallbacks_change`), `vendor/gpui-pre-macos/src/{gpui_macos.rs,open_type.rs,text_system.rs}`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Agents without a status report

`feat/agent-fallback` (branched from `master`, fork only): the Agents section lists a terminal that has no OSC (Operating System Command) 7501 report but whose foreground process is a known agent, with its state read off its screen and window title. The case it was built for: Claude Code backgrounds a session (←) onto its daemon's own terminal, and resuming it (→) leaves only a relay client (`claude`, argv `["claude"]`) in the pane, so its reports never reach Herdr and the session vanished from the list.

- Rules are data: `defaults/harnesses/*.toml` are tuios's harness manifests, copied unchanged (21 of them: amp, antigravity, claude-code, cline, codex, copilot, cursor-agent, devin, droid, gemini-cli, goose, grok, hermes, kilo, kimi, kiro, maki, opencode, pi, qoder, qwen). `harness.rs` reads `[detect]` (`comm`, `argv0`, `argv_path`, `exe_glob`, `require`), `[screen]` (`fold_case`, `lines`, rules with `state`, `priority`, `region`, `all`/`any`/`not`/`regex`/`not_regex` and nested `all_of`/`any_of`/`none_of`) and `[title]` (the same rules against the window title as the program set it, substrings token-bounded as tuios's `containsToken`) with tuios's semantics: highest priority first, ties in file order, case-folded substrings, multi-line regexes with RE2's (Google's regular-expression library's) ASCII `\s \d \w \b` and their negations, outside and inside brackets (rewritten at load, since Rust's are Unicode; Rust's bracket set operators `--`, `&&`, `~~` are escaped so they stay characters as in RE2), regions (`prompt_box`, `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)`, `after_last_horizontal_rule`, ...). The loader keeps tuios's refusals: an idle screen rule must read the prompt box or pin it with a regex, an empty rule matches nothing, a bare name under 5 characters needs `[detect.require]`, `lines` at most 200, `bottom_non_empty_lines(N)` within `lines`, patterns at most 512 bytes, gates nested at most 8 deep. `[notify]`, `[transcript]`, `[input]`, `[resume]`, rule `answers` and `osc_progress` title rules (the core doesn't pass on OSC 9;4) are ignored. `needs_input` maps to `blocked`, so needs-input alerts ring as 7501 ones do.
- Skipped: `crush` (its only rule uses `show = "dialog"`, tuios's bordered-dialog finder, not ported; such a rule never matches here), `aider` and `omp` (no screen rules). tuios's main has moved on since the copied commit (e.g. a letta manifest); port by copying again.
- Detection: the core's `foreground` event carries the process name and argv but not the resolved executable, so `require.exe_base` is checked against the process name and `exe_glob` (in `[detect]` and `require`) only against a path-like name or argv[0]. So the harnesses whose bare name needs the install path as corroboration are missed when started by bare name: amp, antigravity (`agy`), goose, grok, maki and native kimi. That includes npm shims like `node /usr/local/bin/amp`: the script path is the shim's, not the package's, so `argv_path` doesn't match either. They are recognised only when argv[0] is the full install path. Node-run kilo, kimi, pi and qwen are recognised (their `require.exe_base` is the runtime). As tuios does deliberately, an interpreter's run token counts as the program's name, so `python3 claude.py` or `bash ./claude` is taken for Claude Code (test `a_script_named_after_an_agent_is_that_agent`). `exe_glob` matching is linear in pattern times path (`glob_path`, `glob_one`), so a person's manifest can't stall the UI thread.
- Follow-up (herdr-core): send the executable path in `Foreground` (`crates/herdr-core/src/foreground.rs` reads it and skips it), then pass it to `require` and `exe_glob` as tuios does. That recognises the harnesses above by bare name.
- State: `Watcher::update` runs in `tend_terminals` on every core tick (about 500ms), only for terminals with no report. It identifies a terminal's process again only when its `foreground` record changes (a miss is cached too). It reads the screen only when `Vt::writes` moved (stamped by every write and resize from one process-wide counter, so a reconnected terminal's new `Vt` never repeats a stamp; `TerminalView::writes`), and the title (`TerminalView::raw_title`) only then too, taking the last `lines` non-empty rows (`Vt::tail_text`, from the grid, so hidden tabs' terminals read too) and falling back to the title rules when no screen rule matches; an unchanged screen reuses its verdict, though the state is still advanced every tick. A pane starts `idle`, and a rule that stops matching keeps the last word, except that `working` with no screen change for 30s and no rule matching becomes `idle` (tuios's stall heuristic and default; `blocked` is never stalled, as in tuios). That is what ends a turn for the harnesses with no screen idle rule (hermes's idle is a title rule; kimi, pi, droid, copilot, cursor-agent, goose, kilo, qoder and antigravity have none). An idle reading on a working pane is published after 3 confirming looks 100ms apart or 700ms (tuios's `idleGate` numbers), so about a second at the tick rate. A 7501 report always wins; the entry goes when the foreground stops matching or the terminal ends.
- Seen and `done`: core's turn counter doesn't move for these agents and the watcher keeps no turns of its own, so for a terminal the watcher owns (`Watcher::owns`) `tend_terminals` passes no seen turn to `set_turn` and `Model::status` falls back to the app's own seen flag: a turn ending while the person isn't looking is `done` until they look. `share_seen` skips those terminals, so the shared `herdr.seen` label never runs ahead of core's turns: when the agent starts reporting (its hooks, or a 7501 agent in the same terminal), core's turn and the label decide again and its first reported turn shows `done` (test `an_unreported_agent_is_seen_locally_and_a_report_takes_over_cleanly`). Known limits: the seen state is per app, not shared with other clients, and an unseen `done` is lost when the app restarts (the model starts seen). A watch recreated while the app runs (the agent briefly not in front) keeps the model's flag, so it isn't lost then.
- `classify` finds the prompt box once per scan and joins and folds each region's text once.
- Adding a harness: drop its tuios manifest (or one in the same format) into `defaults/harnesses` and its screens into `tests/harness-screens`; no code changes (test `a_new_manifest_is_data_only`). A person can add or replace one by `id` in `harnesses/*.toml` in the app home, read at start.
- Licensing: the manifests are MIT (tuios, Gaurav Gosain) and their rules derive from herdr's Apache-2.0 manifests: `defaults/harnesses/LICENSE-tuios.txt`, `LICENSE-herdr.txt` and `README.md` (source commit), an entry in `licenses/inventory.json`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` regenerated. Dependencies: `regex`, `toml` (both already in Cargo.lock).
- Tests: `src/harness_tests.rs`: every tuios screen fixture (`tests/harness-screens`, copied unchanged) classifies as tuios says and every screen rule decides one; tuios's title cases, every title rule deciding one; recognition (the relay client, the version-named native binary, npm installs, checkouts and non-interpreters refused, `require`, scripts named after an agent); precedence; the idle confirmation window; the stall (working stops, blocked doesn't, a matching rule keeps it); the watcher (7501 wins, process change, terminal end, an unchanged screen not read again, silence ending a turn); seen kept locally and a 7501 report taking over cleanly; RE2 ASCII classes; globs, including a pathological pattern; refused manifests; a test-only fake harness picked up from data alone. `vt::tests::tail_text_is_the_last_lines_that_carry_anything`.
- Files: `src/harness.rs`, `src/harness_tests.rs`, `src/vt.rs` (`tail_text`, `writes`), `src/terminal.rs` (`TerminalView::tail_text`, `writes`, `raw_title`), `src/window.rs` (`tend_terminals`, `share_seen`, `unreported`), `src/main.rs`, `build.rs`, `Cargo.toml`, `defaults/harnesses/`, `tests/harness-screens/`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `licenses/inventory.json`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES`
- Not verified live: a real backgrounded Claude Code session resumed into a pane.
- Upstream: none yet

### Focus history

`feat/focus-history` (branched from `master`, fork only): the app records where the person settles and shares it as the core document `herdr.focus`, and mods read it with `recentPlaces()`. A mod sees nothing while closed, so a go-to picker (the personal Fetch mod) only knew the moves made through itself; "Back to" and "Recent" need every focus move.

- A place is the model's focus: the active space's active tab's focused pane, with its terminal (null for a mod pane), tab, space, machine and arrival time. Every way focus moves ends in `publish`, which notes it (`note_focus`); overlays, popups, windows and layout terminal blocks (`focused_block`, `window_terminal`) never change the model's focus, so opening Fetch is no place and the place under it stays first.
- Debounce: a place counts once the person stays on it `SETTLE` (300 ms) (`focus_history::Settle`). It is noted when they move on or at the next tick (about 500 ms), stamped with when they arrived; quick hops (a pane passed through by keys) leave nothing, and coming back to the last noted place after a hop notes nothing new.
- Document: `herdr.focus` on this machine's core, `{"v":1,"places":[{pane, terminal, tab, space, machine, at}, ...]}`, newest first, at most 20, one place per pane; `pane` (non-empty string) and `at` (integer) are required; `terminal`, `tab`, `space` and `machine` may be null or absent (all `Option`; the app always sets `tab` and `space`). Places on another machine stay here with their `machine`; remote cores are never written. What the app doesn't read is written back as it was: other top-level fields (`Stored::rest`), other fields of a place (`Place::extra`), and entries without a `pane` or an integer `at` (`Stored::foreign`, the first 20 only so no client grows the document without limit, after the places, outside the merge and the cap). A `v` above 1 is read, never overwritten, and places noted against it are dropped rather than shown unshared. A value that isn't an object with an integer `v` and a `places` list is unreadable: warned once, and the next write replaces it with a fresh `{"v":1}`, since no client can use it and the version check still stops it overwriting a concurrent good write.
- Merge and conflicts: the union by pane, the later `at` winning, sorted by `at` then pane and cut to 20 (`merge`, order-independent, so writers converge). `sync` writes only when merging this window's unsent places keeps a visit the stored list lacks (`Stored::gains`), so another client's untidy list (unsorted, duplicates, over 20) isn't rewritten without a focus change; on `conflict` it reads, merges and retries (4 attempts), then leaves the places unsent. After a failed round the next waits 1 s, doubling per failure in a row up to 30 s, so a core that keeps refusing (a full disk, the size limit, endless conflicts) isn't asked every tick; success, a newly settled place, a `herdr.focus` doc event or a reconnect ends the wait. The window keeps the stored list plus what it noted and hasn't written (`History`), so another client clearing the list isn't undone. It writes at most one round at a time, from the existing 500 ms core tick (`flush_focus`), only while the core's event stream is up, and reads again on a `herdr.focus` doc event (`Pushed::focus`, which doesn't reread the layout documents) unless its version is the one this window wrote (`Pushed::focus_version`, `History::changed`), or on `missed`. A change seen while a round runs is read after it (`due` clears `stale` as a round starts; only a failure sets it). Each round's calls share one deadline (`OnCore`): 5 s, and 1 s for the last write on quit (`drain_documents`, skipped when nothing is unsent). Idle costs nothing: with nothing unsent and nothing changed `due` returns at once, and `publish` (every title change too) builds and compares the list only when a place settled (`note_focus`).
- Mods: `recentPlaces()`, a snapshot read like `panes()` (snapshot key `places`): a copy that reads it redraws when the list changes. Closed panes are not pruned from it; the reader checks them against `panes()`.
- Tests: `focus_history::tests` (merge dedupe, cap, order independence, ties; debounce; unknown fields kept; conflict retry against a fake core, giving up, a newer shape left alone; a window's rounds writing only changes, keeping places noted mid-round, honouring a clear, failure; top-level fields and unreadable places kept on write; an untidy list rewritten only for news; unreadable values warned and replaced; a change during a round read after it, an echo not; backoff against a refusing core and what ends it; idle ticks asking the core nothing; null `tab`/`space` a normal place; the unreadable-entry bound; places noted against a newer document not shown), `hc::tests::the_event_stream_keeps_the_terminals_as_the_core_has_them` (doc events for `herdr.focus` routed apart from layout documents and remote machines), `window::tests::focus_history` (own process, on `hc::answering` with a document store: a settled pane written with its terminal, tab and space; a quick hop not; a popup not; a mod reading `recentPlaces()` redrawing; another client's place merged in on a doc event without a write and its extra field kept; the echo of its own write not read again).
- Files: `src/focus_history.rs`, `src/window.rs` (`here`, `note_focus`, `flush_focus`, `drain_documents`, the core tick, `reconnected`), `src/hc.rs` (`Pushed::focus`), `src/mods.rs` (`Snapshot::places`, `publish_places`, `recentPlaces`), `src/main.rs`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Mod key sections win from the first start

`fix/fetch-tab` (branched from `master`, fork only): a keys.json `"mod:<name>"` section wins inside that mod from the first start, not only after a keys.json reload. gpui-shell bound `ShellRoot`'s keys (tab, shift-tab to move between tab stops, cmd-c) lazily in `ShellRoot::new`, after `keys::install` had taken its snapshot of the kit's bindings (`KitKeys`), so on a fresh start they were live and one context deeper than `mod == <name>`: tab left a mod's search field instead of reaching the mod; a reload rebuilt the keymap from the snapshot and dropped them. `HERDR:` change in `vendor/gpui-kit/crates/shell/src/lib.rs` (`init` binds them up front; `install_key_bindings` in `root.rs` stays guarded by `KeyBindingsInstalled`), so they are always in the snapshot; `keys::tab` matches a mod section as `mod == <name> > ShellRoot`, the kit's depth, where the later binding (keys.json's) wins. Every mod renders under a `ShellRoot`, so all mod sections keep matching. 
- Tests: `window::tests::a_mod_section_key_beats_the_kits_tab_from_the_first_start` (fails with the `keys::tab` half reverted).
- The rule mods follow since then: tab and shift-tab move focus between a mod's focusable elements and never reach its `on_key_down`; a mod that wants them binds them in its `mod:<name>` section and reads the command from `nextCommand()`. Documented in `defaults/AGENTS.md` (Keys) and `defaults/herdr.d.ts` (`nextCommand`); tested by `window::tests::a_mods_on_key_down_never_sees_tab` (fails with tab dropped from the `KitKeys` snapshot) and `window::tests::a_mod_reads_tab_from_its_keys_section` (fails when the section loses its `> ShellRoot` depth).
- Files: `vendor/gpui-kit/crates/shell/src/lib.rs`, `vendor/gpui-kit/crates/shell/src/root.rs`, `src/keys.rs`, `src/window.rs`
- Upstream: pull request #716 (`up/mod-keys-first-start`), awaiting review

### Pane spacing in Settings

`feat/spacing-settings` (branched from `master`, fork only): the Settings window's Appearance > Layout page has a Spacing group for the shown layout: "Pane gap", "Pane container padding", "Pane container gap" and "Terminal padding" (x and y), each a 1 px stepper within 0 to 40 with a Reset where the value differs from the layout file's. Spacing was only settable by editing the layout JSON, and shipped layouts couldn't change it at all without a clone.

- The values are layout clicks, not settings.json keys or file edits, so Herdr's rule that a layout's own look wins stays as it is and shipped layouts take them too. `layout_sync::NodeState` gains `padding`, `gap`, `terminal_padding` and `pane_gap` (the tab block's `pane.gap`, its own field so a file edit of the pane's border or header keeps a gap click and the other way round; `PaneClick` is unchanged). `State::between`, `apply`, `forget_edited` and `without_clicks` carry them like the other click fields, so a later file edit of the same field wins and `save()` never writes them into the file. `State::apply` clamps them to 0..40 (NaN to 0; a number past f32's range arrives as infinity and clamps to its end), so clicks from `layout.set` on a shipped layout or a hand-written click file are bounded too. Click files without these fields load and keep the file's values.
- Command `layout.spacing {pane_gap?, area_padding?, area_gap?, terminal_padding?, reset?}` (`layout_sync::Spacing::apply`, called from `window.rs`): it finds the tab block on screen as the renderer does (`visible_blocks`) and the row or column holding it (`tab_area`), clamps to 0..40 (`SPACING_MAX`) and sets the composed tree (`self.chrome`) all or nothing (on a clone, kept only if every field applies), then `apply_look` only when terminal padding is set or reset (`Spacing::changes_look`: it is the one field in the composed settings; recomposing lists font folders and re-reads the theme, about 10 M instructions) and `sync`, whose `save()` writes the click. `reset` copies the file's value back, which leaves no click. It fails, with a reason, while the layout file has errors (clicks over the shipped stand-in would be dropped on repair), when the layout shows no tab, the tab block has no pane frame (for `pane_gap`) or isn't in a row or column (for `area_*`).
- Every reader already draws from the composed tree, so all of them follow: row and column gaps in `render_node` (`pane_size::gap`, `separator_width`) and `traffic_light_divider_inset`, padding in the free `apply_look(el, n, focused)` and `pane_size::padding`, hidden-pane geometry in `pane_size` (from `self.chrome`; `fit_hidden_tabs` keys on the terminal padding, the panels' scale (`ui_scale`, which padding, borders and headers follow), the tab block's padding and its pane frame besides the area and cell, so a spacing change refits hidden tabs), and terminal padding through `Settings::compose` and `terminal_padding_px` (scaled by zoom).
- Mods: `layoutSpacing()` (`layout_sync::spacing_fields`, snapshot key `spacing`, published beside `layout`): whether the tab block has a pane frame and an area, and the four fields as written. A reader redraws only when they change, not on every resize or click that changes `layout()`.
- Settings window: reads `layoutSpacing()` and the layout file (read only, on entering the Layout page and every 3 s while it shows, redrawing only when the text changed; for Reset), and runs `layout.spacing`. `spacingFieldsOf` gives the file the same shape. Reset shows where the shown value differs from the file's, set or not (`null` counts as unset); values are compared within 0.005 and drawn to two decimals (f32 noise), pairs as x, y like terminal padding. A step not yet in `layout()` is kept for up to a second, so quick clicks each count; Reset and a layout switch drop it, and a one-shot timer, started by a step and replaced by the next, lets a refused one go once it expires; no timer runs while nothing is pending. While the layout file can't be read or parsed the steppers are hidden with a note, since Herdr refuses `layout.spacing` then; a missing file keeps them (Herdr shows its shipped stand-in and takes clicks on it), without a Reset. Controls a layout has no place for are hidden with a note.
- Files: `src/layout_sync.rs` (tests `spacing_is_a_click_the_file_never_takes`, `spacing_stays_within_0_to_40`, `spacing_reset_shows_the_files_values_again`, `a_file_edit_of_a_spacing_field_beats_its_click`, `spacing_says_why_it_cannot`, `click_files_from_before_pane_gap_keep_the_files_gap`, `a_pane_gap_click_and_the_other_pane_clicks_drop_apart`, `a_failed_spacing_call_changes_nothing`, `spacing_finds_the_tab_block_on_screen`, `spacing_clicks_are_bounded_however_they_arrive`, `spacing_past_f32s_range_clamps_to_its_end`, `shipped_layouts_take_spacing_clicks`, `terminal_padding_clicks_scale_with_zoom`, `only_terminal_padding_recomposes_the_settings`, `mods_see_the_spacing_fields_as_written`), `src/window.rs`, `src/mods.rs` (`Snapshot::spacing`, `publish_spacing`, `layoutSpacing`), `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `src/window/pane_size.rs` (`Geometry`), `src/window/pane_size_tests.rs` (`hidden_tabs_follow` extended for terminal padding and pane gap apart, `spacing_refuses_a_broken_layout_file`), `src/commands.rs`, `docs/commands.md` (generated), `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`

### Theme panel derivation

`feat/theme-derivation` (branched from `master`): themes without explicit `colors` get their panel colors (`canvas`, `chrome`, `popover`, `raised`, `border`, `input`, `hover`, `selected`, `muted`, `dim`, `ring`, `idle`, `unknown`) by stepping the terminal background toward the theme's own foreground, not toward plain white, black or warm grey, so panels take the theme's tint. `border` and `input` are solid instead of translucent white or black; `ring` is the accent in use (the theme's, or an overridden `colors.accent` such as the Terminals look's) when it reads at 3:1 or better on the background, else the classic mix (at least 5.4:1 across 90 public themes), else black or white for a background where even that falls short (a mid grey such as `#707070` gives the mix 2.96:1), so the ring always reads at 3:1. An `#rrggbbaa` accent rings in its color; other values are already refused as problems by `parse_theme_file` and `Settings::problems`. The steps (`DARK_STEPS`, `LIGHT_STEPS`) were fitted to the relationships across the 41 built-in themes of the Rex terminal app (median fit error under 1 sRGB level for most tokens). Only the ratios are borrowed, no Rex colors or theme data. One deliberate departure: a light theme's `raised` steps 0.035 toward the foreground (Rex keeps it at the background), because Herdr uses `raised` as a hover fill on the background. Explicit `colors` still replace any derived value. `mix` clamps per channel so negative steps can't overflow.

- Panel text: across 90 public themes, the lowest contrast of `foreground`/`muted`/`dim` on any panel rose from 6.95/3.39/2.10 to 7.31/3.65/2.62. That holds for those themes, not every background: on a mid-grey background `muted` can read up to 0.09 lower than before.
- Tests: `settings::derivation_tests` (dark layers canvas ≤ surface < popover < raised, light canvas < surface and raised < popover ≤ surface, chrome against canvas, flat and shaded; text contrast floors on every panel in both states; ring contrast or fallback; explicit colors win in both states; panels take the foreground's tint; `sidebar_shade` modes against focus, only `chrome` changing, own chrome never shaded; parsing, default and refused values; `mix` clamps); `scripts/settings.test.ts` (the Sidebar shade picker), over the presets, shipped files and seven public extremes (pure black, near black, pastel and plain light).
- `sidebar_shade` in settings.json (`"flat"` default, `"active"`, `"always"`): the sidebars and bars (`chrome`) take a shade darker while the window is active, or always, or never. The step is `chrome_active` in `Steps`: on dark themes −0.022, level with `canvas`, fitted from Rex's window material (its window backdrop is the theme's canvas while the window is key and its sidebar tone while it isn't); light themes keep their sidebar (0.068 both ways), as Rex's light window backdrop doesn't change either. A theme's or look's own `colors.chrome` wins in every mode. `AppWindow` observes window activation (`observe_window_activation`, `shade_sidebar`), notes it with `settings::set_window_active`, and `settings::reshade` resolves the theme again from the palette `set` last read (no theme file read on the focus path, so a focus change can't flip the UI to Graphite on a half-written file; `sidebar_shade_focus` checks it by moving the app home to an empty folder between focus changes, and fails if the focus path reads the file. The file watcher's reload still reads the theme file, as on `master`, and can still fall back on a half-written one). Only `chrome` can change, which neither terminals nor the kit's tokens use, so it skips both: `theme()` reads are recorded as a mod revision, `mods::bump_revision("theme")` redraws only the copies that read it, then the window redraws. Test `a_theme_bump_refreshes_only_theme_readers` calls `theme()` through the real `herdr` host module, so dropping the recorded read fails it (checked). `WINDOW_ACTIVE` starts false, matching the flat theme resolved before the window exists. When focus can't change the theme (`sidebar_shade` flat or always, classic panels in use, a light theme, or a theme's, look's or settings' own `chrome`), `settings::follows_focus` (decided once per `set`) skips `reshade` and the mod bump entirely, so the default flat mode costs nothing per focus change (tests `only_a_dark_tinted_active_shade_follows_focus`, and `sidebar_shade_focus` counts `reshade` calls; it fails with the guard removed). Settings window: a "Sidebar shade" picker on the Appearance > Window page.
- Deliberate looks and weak foregrounds keep classic panels even while tinted (`panels_in_use`): a theme file or layout look whose own `colors` set `surface`, `popover` or `raised` (Win98's theme file, the Terminals layout) draws exactly as with classic panels; settings.json's `colors` never decide this, they only replace colors (`Settings::look_paints_panels` carries the look's part apart from the merged colors). The Settings window's theme swatches use the layout look's choice too. `settings().panels_in_use` tells mods which derivation draws (the whole `settings()` value is built once per `set` and handed out as a shared `Arc`, `settings::for_mods()`, instead of being serialized on every call, as mods call it from render; tests `mods_read_the_settings_built_at_the_last_set`, `settings_hands_out_the_value_built_at_the_last_change`), and the Settings window hides Sidebar shade while it's classic, since tinting toward a terminal foreground means nothing for panels it designed itself (Win98's tinted `selected` was invisible on its grey popover, its `chrome` and `canvas` near-white). A foreground under 3:1 on the background, or on the wrong side of it, can't carry a tint either (borders collapsed into the background). Of 90 public themes only Win98 is affected. A per-key "no less distinct than classic" rule was not used: 27 of those themes have softer tinted borders by design. Tests `deliberate_looks_and_weak_foregrounds_keep_classic_panels`, `own_colors_keep_fills_visible_and_text_readable` (every theme with its own colors: hover and selected at least 4 levels apart from popover and surface once composited, text no worse than classic).
- `panel_colors` in settings.json (`"tinted"` default, `"classic"`): classic keeps the derivation upstream Herdr ships, value for value (`classic_panels`, upstream's fallbacks: white, black and warm grey mixes, translucent hover, selected, border and input, chrome 32% toward black on dark themes). `resolve` takes its panel entries from `tinted_panels` or `classic_panels`; everything else is shared. `sidebar_shade` doesn't apply to classic. Test `classic_panels_are_the_first_derivation_exactly` compares classic against `tests/fixtures/master-panels.json`, the resolved themes dumped from `master` at `5bdbd58` (4 presets, the shipped files with their own colors, 7 public extremes). Settings window: a "Panel colours" picker beside Sidebar shade, which hides while Classic is chosen.
- Files: `src/settings.rs` (`Steps`, `contrast`, `with_alpha`, `resolve`, `mix`, `SidebarShade`, `set_window_active`, `reshade`, `PanelColors`, `tinted_panels`, `classic_panels`), `tests/fixtures/master-panels.json`, `src/window.rs` (`shade_sidebar`, `theme_changed`), `src/mods.rs` (`theme` read recorded), `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`
- Upstream: maybe. It's a derivation improvement and carries no Rex data.

### Mod palette actions

`feat/mod-palette-actions` (branched from `master`): `"actions"` in a mod's `mod.json` adds command palette entries, `[{ "title", "command", "args"?, "keywords"? }]`, listed under the mod's name and run as `run(command, args)`. Mods could only add keys; the palette listed the app's own commands and layouts. `mod.open` takes `props` (what the mod's `props()` holds; while the mod is open, other props open it afresh with them, keeping a keyed window where it was dragged, and the same or none still close it; a mod.json overlay `layout` takes no props and only toggles), and an action's `mod.open` without a `name` (or a blank one) opens the mod itself, so an action can open the mod's own overlay for one purpose (a cloned mod opens itself, not the original). No setting: a mod without `actions` changes nothing.

- Checks (`mods::actions`): an entry needs a title and an app command, `args` an object passing the command's argument check (`validate_args`, and `commands::missing` as the dispatcher uses it: a blank string is missing), `props` an object, a `mod.open` name a string, `keywords` a string. An entry that fails is left out and named, as `mod <name>: actions[<i>]: <why>`, in `herdr-app status` and `herdr-app check <mod folder>` (which exits 1 on them before the script check). A mod.json Herdr can't read (`Found::hint_error`, which leaves the defaults) is named the same way, since it costs the mod its actions; a bad entry, or `actions` that isn't a list, costs the mod nothing else (`place`, `focusable` still read: `Hint::actions` is kept as raw JSON, not typed).
- Mods: `modActions()` (snapshot key `actions`), published when the folders are scanned: at start, on any change under `mods/` (`sync_folders`, which redraws the copies that read it when the list changed, so an open palette follows an edit or a removed mod), and after `mod.clone`. The palette reads it beside `commands()` and `layouts()`; an action's id is `<mod>/<title>`, so recents don't collide with the app's entries or a sibling action, and the palette's `mod.clone`/`layout.clone` special cases apply to its own entries only. A palette key hint (`name:value`) fills one argument, so entries with more arguments show none.
- Perf: no render or frame path changes. The palette builds its entries on each of its own renders (a keystroke, an arrow, a live read changing); actions add one host read per palette render (a copy of a list as long as the actions) and nothing while the palette is closed. `entries()` calls `commands()` once, not twice. The scan computes the list once per folder change.
- Tests: `mods::tests::palette_actions_are_checked_and_malformed_ones_are_named_not_listed` (every check, the defaults, the mod's other fields kept, `herdr-app check`'s reading of a folder), `window::tests::mod_actions` in its own process (listed at start, `mod.open` props reach `props()`, other props reopen and the same props close, status names a bad entry, an edited `mod.json` and a removed mod redraw an open reader), `mods::render_tests::local_state_and_overlay_initial_descriptions` (the palette shows an action under its mod, found by keywords), `scripts/palette.test.ts` (grouped under the mod, run with their args, gone with the mod, distinct ids, no borrowed key hint, the clone special cases). Every guard mutation-checked.
- Files: `src/mods.rs` (`Hint::actions`, `actions`, `mod_json_problems`, `read_hint`, `Snapshot::actions`, `publish_actions`, `modActions`), `src/window.rs` (`scan_folders`, `sync_folders`, `mod.open` props, status), `src/main.rs` (`check`), `src/commands.rs`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/frame/palette/main.js`, `scripts/palette.test.ts`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `docs/commands.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Mod terminal menu items

`feat/mod-menu-items` (branched from `master`: it builds on Mod palette actions' checks, so offering it upstream follows that change): `"menu_items"` in a mod's `mod.json` adds entries to the terminal right-click menu, `[{ "title", "command", "args"?, "when"?, "icon"? }]`, which the shipped `menu` lists after its own pane items and before Close pane, one group per mod (folder order) under a divider. A mod could only change that menu by replacing it whole with a layout's `terminal_menu`, forking the shipped menu and shutting out every other mod's entries. No setting: a mod without `menu_items` changes nothing.

- The clicked target, not the focused terminal: the check records `fill`, the arguments `pane` and `terminal` the command takes and the entry leaves open (null counts as open), plus `props` for `mod.open`; the menu sets them from its `props().target`, and `mod.open`'s props become `{ ...props, target }`. Filled arguments count as given in the required-argument check, so `pane.focus` or `terminal.copy` need no placeholder. The app computes `fill` from its own command table, so a layout's menu doesn't need `commands()` to run an item right.
- `when` (optional): `"selected"`, `"cwd"` or `"pane"`, the three facts a terminal menu's target carries that decide whether an item makes sense (an agent check was left out: no item needs it yet, and `terminals()` already tells a menu). An entry filling `pane` also hides for a terminal outside a tab, whose `target.pane` is a block id, as the shipped items do. `icon` (optional): an `.svg` file name in the mod's folder, published as `mods/<mod>/<file>`; otherwise the command's icon.
- Checks (`mods::menu_items`, sharing `entries` and `runnable` with `actions`): title, app command, `args` passing `validate_args` and `missing`, `props` an object, `when` one of the three, `icon` a bare `.svg` name. A failing entry is named as `mod <name>: menu_items[<i>]: <why>` in `herdr-app status` and `herdr-app check` (`mod_json_problems`, both lists); an unreadable mod.json is named once, by `actions`.
- Long menus scroll: every mod item adds a 31 px row, and a pane menu with a selection, prompt marks and an icon or a kept sub-agent already passed the stock 420 px cap on `master`, leaving Close pane below the edge (unreachable by mouse, selectable by arrow key while invisible). `terminal_menu_spec` passes the room inside the popup to the menu as `props.height`, in the menu's px: the popup's most (420 px, or a share of the window), no more than fits between the window's 8 px snap margins (`POPUP_MARGIN`) at the panels' zoom (`settings::ui_scale`), less the popup's 1 px border on each side, so Close pane stays reachable in a short window or at a high zoom. The shipped menu then puts the items above a trailing Close in a clipped list (`menu-body`) at that height less its padding and the pinned row, places them by a scroll offset (`top`), keeps Close pinned below, scrolls with the wheel, and scrolls the keyboard's selection into view. Row and rule heights are the menu's own constants (31 and 9 px); a menu without `props.height`, or one that fits, renders as before.
- `targeted` sets `props.target` on top of an entry's own props, so the clicked target replaces an entry's own `target` (documented in `defaults/AGENTS.md` and `defaults/herdr.d.ts`).
- Layouts with their own `terminal_menu` get nothing injected: `menuItems()` (snapshot key `menu_items`, published with `modActions()` by `scan_folders`) lists the entries with `mod`, `args`, `when`, `icon` and `fill`, and `defaults/herdr.d.ts` says how to run one.
- Unknown mod.json fields are ignored (`Hint` has no `deny_unknown_fields`; test `unknown_mod_json_fields_are_ignored`), so a mod with `menu_items` loads unchanged on a build without this change.
- Perf: the menu reads `menuItems()` once per render (a copy of a list as long as the items, empty without mods using it) and filters it; nothing runs while the menu is closed. Opening the pane menu, A/B in one process with a temporary render test (never committed): load, run and describe the menu twice in a GPUI test window, master's `menu/main.js` and this branch's alternating, 10 warm-up rounds then 50 each (debug build): master 7.03 ms median (p10 6.83, p90 7.41), branch with no mod items 7.02 ms (6.85, 7.41), branch with four items 7.41 ms (7.28, 7.86). No items costs nothing measurable; four items cost about 0.4 ms, the four extra rows.
- Three questions: (1) does the app act on it? It reads, checks and reports the entries, as with palette actions; the menu that shows them is a mod. (2) Only the app sees it? Yes: other mods' `mod.json` files are the app's folder scan, and `fill` comes from its command table. (3) Mods can't do it correctly? Yes: without it a mod's only route is replacing the whole menu, which no two mods can do at once. Verdict: C, ask Can, after Mod palette actions (upstream #211); it is the same contract for a second list.
- Tests: `mods::tests::terminal_menu_items_are_checked_and_say_what_the_click_fills` (every check, `fill` per command, filled arguments counting as given in a menu item but not in a palette action, `herdr-app check` naming both lists and an unreadable mod.json once), `mods::tests::unknown_mod_json_fields_are_ignored`, `mods::render_tests::pane_menu_lists_mods_items_in_groups` (after Herdr's items and before Close pane, a divider per mod and none inside one (Herdr's clip and close dividers apply to its own items only), `when`, the mod's icon, no pane items for a terminal outside a tab), `mods::render_tests::a_long_pane_menu_scrolls_and_keeps_close_pane_in_reach` (14 rows in 420 px: the clipped list's height, Close pane outside it, a keyboard selection scrolled into view), `window::tests::terminal_menu_preserves_clicked_target_and_uses_mod_dimensions` (`props.height`, none for a fraction), `scripts/menu.test.ts` (a click runs the command with the clicked pane and terminal while another pane is focused, the long menu's pinned Close, room, keyboard scrolling both ways, wheel bounds, and no scrolling when it fits or has no height, `mod.open` gets `props.target` beside its own props, an entry's own `pane` wins, the published entry isn't mutated). Mutation-checked: removing any of the `fill`, filled-as-given, `when`, `icon`, `props` fill or check-reporting guards, or the menu's `when`/pane hiding, group divider or icon, fails a test.
- Files: `src/mods.rs` (`Hint::menu_items`, `menu_items`, `entries`, `runnable`, `menu_item`, `mod_json_problems`, `Snapshot::menu_items`, `publish_menu_items`, `menuItems`), `src/window.rs` (`scan_folders`, status), `src/main.rs` (`check`), `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/mods/menu/main.js`, `scripts/menu.test.ts`, `justfile`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Working spinner

`feat/working-spinner` (branched from `master`, fork only): `working_spinner` in settings.json chooses the spinner mods show while an agent works. `"ring"` (the default) draws the spinning SVG; eight glyph presets (`braille` ⠋⠙⠹⠸⠼⠴⠦⠧⠇⠏ 80 ms, `braille-heavy` ⣾⣽⣻⢿⡿⣟⣯⣷ 80 ms, `orbit` ⠁⠂⠄⡀⢀⠠⠐⠈ 100 ms, `line` | / - \ 100 ms, `arc` ◜◠◝◞◡◟ 100 ms, `quarters` ◴◷◶◵ 120 ms, `pulse` · • ● • 180 ms, `sparkle` · ✢ ✳ ✶ ✻ ✽ and back 100 ms); or `{"frames": [...], "interval_ms": n}`. An unknown name, no frames, more than 32 frames, a frame of 0 or more than 4 characters (code points), or an interval that isn't an integer in 16..1000 draws the ring and is a settings problem; settings.json still loads (`Person` keeps the raw value).

- Mods: `settings().working_spinner` is `null` (ring) or `{frames, interval_ms}`, frames space-padded to one length; `working_spinner_presets` lists the presets for a picker. The pane header, tabs, agents list, picker and peek draw it in the terminal font, `t.working`, centred in a `min_w(12)` box. Peek draws the ring by default like the others instead of its own 90 ms braille. Pane, tabs, agents and picker render byte-identical to master by default.
- Settings window: Agents > Activity, "Working spinner", a chip per choice spinning live; the ring removes the key; an unknown name highlights the ring it draws; own frames highlight nothing.
- Glyphs: every preset glyph is in JetBrains Mono or its ZedMono and AdwaitaMono Nerd Font Mono fallbacks at one cell; in the panels (no fallback list for mods) CoreText draws braille from AppleBraille and the shapes from JetBrains Mono or Menlo, all single width, no emoji. Quarters and sparkle mix JetBrains Mono and Menlo strokes.
- Perf: the ring and the glyph presets both step present-only (see Frames step without a draw): 0 window renders and 0 mod rebuilds while an agent works. Before that change, a glyph preset notified its view each step, about 16 window renders and 16 mod rebuilds a second with braille on screen.
- Hot reload: `working_spinner` is part of `Settings`, so a settings.json edit makes `settings::set` report a change and `apply_look` redraws the mods.
- Tests: `settings::tests::{working_spinner_takes_presets_and_frames_and_falls_back_to_the_ring, a_new_working_spinner_redraws_the_mods, the_guide_explains_every_setting}`, `mods::render_tests::{pane_header_draws_the_ring_by_default_as_it_always_has, agent_spinners_follow_the_working_spinner_setting}`, `scripts/settings.test.ts` ("the working spinner picker…"), `scripts/agents.test.ts` (harness `settings`). Every guard mutation-checked (19 mutations, all caught).
- Files: `src/settings.rs` (`SpinnerFrames`, `SPINNER_PRESETS`, `working_spinner`, `compose`, `problems`, `for_mods`), `defaults/mods/{pane,peek,picker,tabs,agents}/main.js`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `scripts/agents.test.ts`

### Dock badge

`feat/dock-badge` (branched from `master`, fork only): on macOS the Dock icon counts the agents waiting on the person, the way Mail counts unread mail. `dock_badge` in settings.json, on by default; `false` clears it. `dock_bounce_on_wait` (off by default) bounces the Dock once, informationally, when the count rises from zero while Herdr is in the background.

- Waiting: a row of the agents list whose agent `Model::status` calls `blocked` (blocked or error) or `done` (finished, not yet seen), the statuses `agents()` gives mods and the list shows as "Needs you" and "Done". `dock_badge::count_rows` follows the list's rules for sub-agents: one with a pane is nested under its parent (no row, its finish no news) unless pinned, and a live sub-agent that is blocked or hit an error puts its parent's row in. Seen idle agents don't count, or the badge would never clear while agents sit at a prompt. The list's narrow (collapsed) view un-nests sub-agents; the badge always counts the wide view.
- Where: `AppWindow::publish` counts while it builds `agents()`, so the badge follows every state change without polling; `apply_look` re-applies it when settings change. `dock_badge::Badge` remembers the label shown, so AppKit hears only of changes; nothing redraws.
- Code: `src/dock_badge.rs` (count, label, `Badge`, the `NSDockTile` call), kept apart from `src/app_icon.rs`; the icon image and the badge are separate `NSDockTile` properties.
- Settings window: Agents > Alerts > Notifications, "Count waiting agents on the Dock icon", under "Bounce the Dock icon". `dock_bounce_on_wait` is settings.json only.
- Tests: `window::tests::the_dock_badge_follows_listings_and_the_setting`, `dock_badge::tests::{counts_agents_that_need_you_or_finished_unseen, counts_what_the_model_shows, counts_rows_as_the_agents_list_shows_them, the_settings_default_to_a_badge_and_no_bounce, labels_the_count_and_clears_for_none_or_off, tells_the_dock_only_when_the_label_changes}`, `settings::tests::the_guide_explains_every_setting`.
- Files: `src/dock_badge.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/settings.rs`, `src/main.rs`, `Cargo.toml` (`NSDockTile` feature), `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`

### Pane menu copy items

`feat/pane-menu-copy` (branched from `master`, fork only): the pane right-click menu opens with a copy group above Split: Copy (only when the clicked terminal has a selection), Copy all, Paste, and Copy path (only when its folder is known). Each acts on the clicked terminal, not the focused one.

- Commands: `terminal.copy` and `terminal.paste` take an optional `terminal` (default: the focused one). `terminal.copy_all {terminal?}` reads `terminal.read` with all the history the core keeps (`lines` 1e9, not `usize::MAX`: the core adds the screen's rows to it), drops the blank rows below the last text, and toasts "Copied 1,234 lines" (with a note when the core says part of the history is missing). On a full-screen program the core reads history by scrolling it with the wheel while typed keys wait (its stitch, up to 1000 lines and 15 s), so a "Copying…" toast shows first and the read gets 30 s. That toast is the copy's own: it lasts past the read's 30 s, a selection copied meanwhile says "Copied" beside it, and the copy finishing removes only it. The command answers at once; failures and a reply without text are error toasts. `terminal.copy_path {terminal?}` copies the terminal's live folder, else the core record's foreground or start folder. `terminal.copy` by command toasts "Copied" and `terminal.copy_path` "Copied path"; the copy key stays silent.
- The window fills the menu's `target` with `selected` (the clicked view has a selection) and `cwd` when it opens the menu, so the mod needs no new API. A right-click never touched the selection (only a left press, a key or a program-taken click clears it); `pane_menu_copy` pins that.
- A terminal can show in a pane and a peek at once, each with its own selection: `terminal.copy {terminal}` takes the view holding one.
- The menu popup's most height is 420 px; it still fits its items. Icons `copy.svg` and `clipboard.svg` (Lucide) join `workspace-chrome`.
- Tests: `window::tests::{copy_all_text_drops_the_blank_rows_below_the_last_text, pane_menu_copy_items_act_on_the_clicked_terminal}` (own process: drag-select in t2 with t1 focused, right-click keeps the selection and opens the menu with `selected` and `cwd`; copy, copy_path and copy_all with `terminal` act on t2, without it on t1; the read asks for all history), `mods::render_tests::pane_menu_copy_items` (visibility rules and order).
- Files: `src/window.rs`, `src/terminal.rs` (`has_selection`), `src/commands.rs`, `defaults/mods/menu/main.js`, `defaults/mods/workspace-chrome/{copy,clipboard}.svg`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `docs/commands.md`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`
- Upstream: none yet

### Copy last output

`feat/copy-last-output` (branched from `master`, fork only): the pane right-click menu's copy group has "Copy last output" after Copy, only when the clicked terminal has a finished command's output its shell marked (OSC 133). It copies that output and toasts "Copied N lines".

- Where the output comes from: the app's own libghostty-vt view of the terminal (`Vt`), which already tracks OSC 133 per cell (prompt, input, output) and per row (prompt start). No herdr-core change and no per-chunk work: nothing runs until the menu opens or the command runs.
- Finding it (`Vt::last_output_selection`): on the primary screen only, from the newest prompt row up to the prompt above it. Scanning that block bottom-up, right to left, the first cell with text decides: output → `ghostty_terminal_select_output` there (Ghostty's own "output after the prompt above" selection, trailing blanks trimmed); input → the command printed nothing, `None`. A block with neither (Enter on an empty prompt, a right prompt and redraws included when the shell marks them as prompt) is skipped for the one above. Output without a marked prompt above it is not offered: Ghostty would select from the top of the history, which is wrong after the core's first paint (it carries no marks) and when the prompt scrolled out of the view's history.
- Text: `ghostty_terminal_selection_format_alloc` with that selection, plain, `unwrap` (wrapped lines joined) and `trim`, then trailing whitespace dropped.
- Menu: the window adds `last_output` (bool) to the menu `target` on a terminal's right-click and on `pane.menu`, as it does `selected`; the shipped menu shows the item from it (icon `file-text`).
- Command: `terminal.copy_last_output {terminal?}` (default: the focused terminal) reads the view the person sees (`shown_terminal_view`), else any other view of that terminal that has output. Each view (a pane, a peek) holds its own screen copy and one attached later lacks the marks written before it, while the menu reads the view right-clicked: falling back keeps the command from refusing what the menu offered. Without output it fails with a reason naming OSC 133, leaving the clipboard alone.
- Limits: the view's history (10,000 lines, 5 MiB) bounds how long an output can be; the first command after Herdr attaches to a terminal is not offered.
- Shell side: the shell must write the marks. The maintainer's zsh (chezmoi `dot_dotfiles/zsh/dot_zshrc`) gets them in Herdr panes by widening the tuios-only precmd/preexec block to `HC_TERMINAL_ID`; `defaults/AGENTS.md` carries a zsh and a bash snippet. The marks belong inside `PROMPT` (`A`…`B`) and `RPROMPT` (`P;k=r`…`B`): marks printed once from precmd leave a redrawn prompt (resize, `reset-prompt`) and a right prompt tagged as input, so Enter on an empty prompt would read as a command that printed nothing. A transient prompt is set inside the line editor where precmd never runs, so its `PS1` needs marking too: for oh-my-posh the zshrc wraps `_omp_get_prompt` so the `transient --eval` output ends with `_osc133_mark_prompt`, guarded on the function's current body (`[[ $functions[_omp_get_prompt] != *_osc133_omp_get_prompt* ]]`) so a re-sourced `.zshrc`, which re-runs `oh-my-posh init`, wraps the fresh definition and never the wrapper itself; Starship's transient prompt plugins need the same. Herdr can't recover an unmarked redraw itself: an all-input prompt row reads the same whether Enter was pressed on it empty or `cd` was typed there (`vt::tests::last_output_needs_marks_on_the_primary_screen` pins this). A half-typed line left with Ctrl+C is input and reads that way regardless. Verified live in an isolated instance with the real oh-my-posh config: without the wrapper the item went away after two empty Enters; with it, the previous output stayed copyable.
- Tests: `vt::tests::{last_output_is_the_last_finished_command_between_its_prompts, last_output_reaches_into_scrollback_and_joins_wrapped_lines, last_output_needs_marks_on_the_primary_screen}`, `window::tests::pane_menu_copy_items_act_on_the_clicked_terminal` (target `last_output`, the copy and its toast, failure on an unmarked terminal and on the alternate screen), `mods::render_tests::pane_menu_copy_items` (visibility and order).
- Files: `src/vt.rs`, `src/terminal.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/commands.rs`, `defaults/mods/menu/main.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `docs/commands.md`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`.
- Upstream: none yet

### Space avatar setting

`feat/space-avatar-setting` (branched from `master`, fork only): `space_avatar` in settings.json tells mods that draw space avatars (a space's tile in a spaces list) which style to use.

- Values: `"letter"` its first letter on its color, `"glyph"` (the default, see Name-matched icons) a one-color icon on its color, `"emoji"` an emoji on a neutral tile, `"repo"` the language or tool of the project in its folder (else a glyph).
- Herdr only validates and passes it on: mods read `settings().space_avatar`, `"glyph"` when unset. Any other value is a settings problem (`problems`) and reaches mods as `"glyph"`; settings.json still loads (`Person` keeps the raw value).
- The Settings window doesn't set it: settings.json only.
- Tests: `settings::tests::space_avatar_reaches_mods_and_unknown_values_fall_back`.
