### Shared git status and selective mod redraws

`perf/events` (branched from `master`, fork only): mods get one cached git status per folder, and a change redraws only the mod copies that read it. Pane header info's `{branch}`, `{ahead}`, `{behind}` and `{dirty}` read this status.

- Git status: `gitStatus(folder, { on, files })` (branch, head, upstream, ahead and behind, change counts, each file with `files`), shared by every copy and window. A local repo is looked at again only when something in it changes on disk (one watcher; ignored folders, objects, logs and lock files don't count), once a burst settles (`QUIET`, 300 ms) and at most once a second (`MIN_GAP`, or 4 times as long as git took). One look answers every folder asked about in a work tree. Another machine's folder has no watcher: `refreshGitStatus` asks again, and an answer read after a minute (`STALE`) is asked again. `nextGitStatus` serves work outside render. The app's own folder tracking (space names, pane branches) reads the same answers rather than running git itself.
- Selective redraws: `shared()` reads are recorded per key, like snapshot reads, so `setShared` redraws only the copies that read that key. Refreshes skip copies not on screen; a copy coming into view catches up on what it missed.
- Title spins: a terminal listing that changes only an agent's spinning title (stripped before publishing) is an ordinary tick, not a publish.
- Why: Agent Deck's spaces panel ran three git processes per place after every agent status change, and the app ran `git rev-parse` per terminal at each turn's end (about 11% CPU in child processes during a status storm); every `setShared` redrew every loaded copy, hidden ones included.
- Tests: `git_status::tests::{detached_initial_and_linked_worktrees, mods_get_the_file_list_only_when_they_ask, events_in_ignored_folders_objects_and_locks_do_not_count, a_burst_of_events_is_one_look_after_it_settles_and_looks_are_spaced, a_watched_repo_is_looked_at_again_when_it_changes_and_not_for_ignored_churn, a_new_ignored_folder_does_not_count_whatever_its_first_event, a_folder_outside_any_repo_is_false, reads_share_one_look_and_answers_redraw_only_on_change}`, `mods::tests::{a_shared_value_redraws_only_the_copies_that_read_it, a_copy_coming_into_view_is_told_and_catches_up_on_missed_refreshes}`, `window::tests::folder_info_comes_from_the_git_status`.
- Files: `src/git_status.rs`, `src/mods.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/files.rs`, `src/main.rs`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none yet. Issue #549 proposes the shared git status; the title-spin part overlaps upstream #640 and #626.
### Name-matched icons

`feat/name-icons` (branched from `master`, fork only): spaces and panes get an icon that matches their name, with nothing to pick and nothing stored.

- Default: `space_avatar` unset (or unknown) is `glyph`, a Nerd Font icon tinted like the rest of the chrome; mods read `settings().space_avatar` as `"glyph"` then, never null. `emoji` stays selectable; `letter` and `repo` draw as they did, except that repo's glyph for a folder with no known project is the name-matched one.
- Panes: unchanged mechanism (see Tab and pane icons). A bare shell showing its automatic name gets its noun's icon from `defaults/pane-icons.json`, which `pane_icons::tests` checks covers every noun of `pane-names.json` (every automatic name ends in one, so there is no adjective or id-hash fallback). With glyph as the default, that now applies without setting `space_avatar`.
- Spaces: the spaces mod matches a space's name word by word (lower case, split at punctuation and camelCase, a trailing s also tried): first a curated word anywhere in the name, then a word that is a glyph's own name (Material Design, then Font Awesome, Codicons, Octicons and the other icon sets; not Powerline or Pomicons) or an emoji's own name or keyword (no faces, people or flags; the shortest name wins). A name matching nothing takes one of the 60 suggested glyphs or emoji from FNV-1a of the name, stepping past any icon another space shows; such collisions are settled by (hash, name, id), never by the order the list shows, so a reorder swaps no icons. A space's own choice (Change icon…) wins until Clear icon.
- Data, not code: `defaults/mods/icon-picker/names/keywords.json`. `icons: [{words, glyph, emoji}]` holds about 70 entries of common project words (docs, api, infra, notes, deploy, k8s, finance…), each word once, glyphs by their `glyphs.json` name, emoji as `emoji.json` writes them, none a person, a flag or a status (the robot and speech balloon are the only Smileys). `glyph_sets` and `no_emoji_groups` say how the rest is matched, and `deny: {words, emoji}` keeps anything that reads as an agent's state (check, alert, loading, done, a warning sign, an hourglass) from matching beside Herdr's status marks. Herdr ships it beside the picker's name lists so any mod can read it; nothing in Rust reads it.
- Why not Rust: the three questions from upstream's AGENTS.md all answer no. The app doesn't act on a space's icon, mods see the name, and a mod can match it correctly. The only Rust change is the default value.
- Perf: the name files are read once, the first time a glyph or emoji avatar is drawn (off the UI thread, as icon search does), and each name's match is cached, so a render costs a map lookup per space. Until the files are read, automatic tiles are blank in their colour.
- Tests: `settings::tests::space_avatar_reaches_mods_and_unknown_values_fall_back` (unset and unknown read as glyph), `window::tests::panes_and_tabs_show_their_automatic_or_chosen_icon` (letter mode set explicitly), `scripts/icon-picker.test.ts` (keywords.json: each word once, lower case, every glyph and emoji in the name files, its sets and groups real, nothing curated denied, a person or a flag). The matching is the person's spaces mod's, tested there with bun.
- Files: `src/settings.rs`, `src/pane_icons.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `defaults/pane-icons.json`, `defaults/mods/icon-picker/names/keywords.json`, `scripts/icon-picker.test.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`
- Upstream: none yet

### Pane header info

`feat/header-info` (branched from `master`, fork only): `pane_header_info` in settings.json is a template of extra words an agent pane's header shows between its name and its status, as room allows. Off by default: unset or blank, panes publish `header_info: null` and every shipped mod draws byte for byte as without it.

- Grammar (`src/header_info.rs`): `{ask}`, `{state}`, `{elapsed}`, `{branch}`, `{ahead}`, `{behind}`, `{dirty}` are keys; other text, an unknown `{word}` too, is literal. `·` or `|` split segments; a separator shows between two segments that show, drawn as its mark alone with 4 px either side (`sep` keeps it as written, for the tooltip). A segment whose keys are all empty is dropped (one with no keys always shows); spaces left by empty keys collapse. Suggested, not enabled: `{ask} · {branch}{ahead}{behind}{dirty} · {state} {elapsed}`.
- Keys: `{ask}` the agent's 7501 `msg`, else its `kind` (permission, question), only while blocked; `{state}` the status the app shows; `{elapsed}` since the app saw that state begin (`Agent::since`; empty when unknown, such as after a restart until the next change): `<1m` under a minute, then `3m`, `1h`, `1h2m` (it changes only on the minute, so the minute timer keeps it true); `{branch}`, `{ahead}` `↑2`, `{behind}` `↓1`, `{dirty}` `*` (a git prompt's mark from the UI font, on its baseline, and no second dot beside ` · `; keys side by side are drawn a space apart, "main ↑2 *") from the shared git cache (`FolderInfo` gains ahead, behind, dirty; filled only while the template shows them, so otherwise a changed file still redraws nothing; `apply_look` reads the folders again when that turns on or off). Zero or unknown renders empty.
- One implementation, in Rust: `publish` resolves each agent pane's segments when its status, report, folder info or the setting changes, and on the minute for `{elapsed}`: one timer (`header_tick`) for the earliest next minute mark, which publishes; a publish that changes nothing redraws nothing. Mods get `PaneInfo.header_info` `{ text, segments: [{ sep, text, ask }] }` and `fitHeaderInfo(segments, width, fontPx)`; the Settings window gets `headerInfoPreview(template)`.
- Fitting, narrowest last: the name keeps its width (the header estimates it, at most 260 px, and the name stays `flex_none`); whole segments go from the right, a blocked `{ask}` last; a lone segment that still doesn't fit truncates with `truncate()`'s ellipsis, and is hidden under about four characters; while hovered the four buttons keep their 112 px and info yields. The whole line is the info's tooltip. Widths are estimated at 0.6 em a character, wide characters (Chinese, Japanese and Korean (CJK), emoji) twice: `fit` and the headers' estimate of the name beside it share `header_info::width` (`headerTextWidth`). The header measures itself with `on_size_change` only while it has info.
- Pane header (shipped `pane`, and the user's `pane-named` in chezmoi): the info sits after the folder, before the text size and status. Right-click on a header runs `pane.menu {pane?}`, which opens the layout's `terminal_menu` (else `menu`) for the pane, its terminal and folder, with no selection, through the same `open_menu` as a terminal's right-click. The chezmoi header falls back to opening `menu` itself on builds without it. The pane menu gains Rename (`pane.rename`) first among the pane's own items, as the tab menu has.
- Agents panel: a parent row shows a count for its live sub-agents, the rows its tree shows (nested ones too), coloured by the most urgent (see Sub-agent status). Below a 220 px sidebar it goes first; folded, the chevron then counts them.
- Settings window: Agents > Activity, "Header info": the template field (written on Enter or leaving it), "Use suggested", and a live preview of the template on a blocked sample agent.
- Perf: no per-frame work. `header_info_follows_changes` counts resolutions over 30 refreshes (none), one per status change (the agent pane once), one per minute tick, and no timer while unset. Live (`HERDR_APP_BENCH`, debug build, three fake agents working or blocked, 150 s each): template set 23 window renders (0.154/s), unset 24 (0.160/s); both a render every 15 s from the existing ticks, the set run adding its minute ticks within that. A blocked header refreshes `{elapsed}` once a minute.
- Tests: `header_info::tests::*` (grammar and dropping, elapsed, git and ask keys empty, width dropping with the blocked ask last, the lone ellipsis and hiding), `window::tests::{header_info_follows_changes_and_the_minute_not_frames, folder_info_comes_from_the_git_status}`, `mods::render_tests::{null_header_info_draws_every_shipped_mod_as_before, pane_header_fits_its_info_beside_the_name_and_buttons, agents_rows_count_their_live_sub_agents, pane_header_right_click_opens_the_pane_menu_with_rename, pane_menu_copy_items, pane_header_draws_the_ring_by_default_as_it_always_has}`, `scripts/settings.test.ts` (header info field), `scripts/pane.test.ts` (right-click).
- Spacing (`feat/header-spacing`): separators draw as their mark alone with 4 px either side (`separator()` in the header mods), so ` · ` and `|` gaps are even whatever spaces the template has, in the folder's colour and size, and `fit` measures a separator as drawn (`SEP_PAD`); the dirty mark is `*` and keys side by side get a space (`Template::resolve`), so markers don't glue to the branch. The chezmoi `pane-named` header gives a named agent's session the same separator, name, mark and session as one item. Tests: `header_info::tests::keys_side_by_side_are_drawn_a_space_apart`, `mods::render_tests::pane_header_fits_its_info_beside_the_name_and_buttons` (the separators' padding and colour, no size or font of their own).
- Files: `src/header_info.rs`, `src/window.rs` (`publish`, `tick_header_info`, `folder_info`), `src/model.rs` (`FolderInfo`), `src/settings.rs`, `src/mods.rs` (`fitHeaderInfo`, `headerInfoPreview`), `defaults/mods/{pane,agents,menu}/main.js`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`.
- Upstream: none yet

### Sub-agent status

`feat/subagent-status` (branched from `master`, fork only): Claude Code's own sub-agents show in the agents panel and pane headers, and a parent's sub-agent count says how urgent they are.

- Root cause: Herdr's sub-agents were only terminals with a `parent` (`hc run` from inside an agent). Claude Code runs Task sub-agents inside its own process and reports one OSC 7501 root record, so the core had nothing to show: on a live core with several Claude sessions running Task sub-agents, `hc ls --json` listed no terminal with a parent and no child records.
- Reporting: Claude Code (as of 2.1.319) writes its own OSC 7501 child records into its terminal, one per sub-agent: `id=<agentId>` (the bare agent id), `app=claude-code` and `title=<description>` (base64, as every 7501 title), with its state. No hook of the person's is involved. The core keeps child records as they are and drops them when Claude exits, so no core change. The shipped agents mod names a row by the record's `title`, else its whole id.
- Child records are sub-agents: in `terminals()[i].records` (as the core lists them), the agents mods put a row under the terminal for each, named by its `title`, else its id; a click focuses or peeks at the terminal that reported it. They count and colour like child terminals.
- Agents panel: the count is a drawn copy icon (`workspace-chrome/copy.svg`) and N, not `⧉ N`: an isolated app's screenshot showed ⧉ from a fallback font, smaller and thinner than the text beside it. It takes its most urgent live sub-agent's colour, `theme().blocked` for blocked or error, `theme().working` for working, else dim, the same rule the folded chevron already used (`topStatus`). Same size and place.
- `{subagents}` for `pane_header_info`: `3 sub-agents`, `3 sub-agents, 1 blocked`, `2 sub-agents blocked`, empty with none; words, as the UI font may lack any mark. Counted in `publish` (`header_info::count_subagents`: live terminals whose parent chain reaches the agent through live terminals, as the agents mod's tree stops at a finished one, in the app's state for a pane else their own word, plus the child records of the agent and of those terminals), only while the template uses the key, and only when publish runs: a status, record or terminal-list change. A segment showing only the count is `minor` and goes first when the header is short of room (beside another key that shows, it goes no sooner than any segment); with one blocked it is `ask` (blocked colour, last to go). Suggested template: `{ask} · {branch}{ahead}{behind}{dirty} · {state} {elapsed} · {subagents}`; a person who set the earlier suggestion keeps it until they pick "Use suggested" again or add the key.
- Known limit (unverified with Claude Code's own records): whether a foreground Task stopped with Esc clears its record has not been checked; if it doesn't, its row (and count) stays until Claude clears it or exits.
- Upstream's three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? The count is no; `{subagents}` yes, it is resolved in `publish` with the rest of `pane_header_info`. (2) Can only the app see it? No: child records reach mods through `terminals()`. (3) Can a mod not do it? A mod can draw rows and colours, so those stay in mods; the header key belongs where the header template is resolved. Verdict: D (fork only), following `pane_header_info`, which upstream does not have.
- Tests: `header_info::tests::{sub_agents_are_child_terminals_below_and_child_records, sub_agents_render_their_count_and_how_many_are_blocked, sub_agents_go_first_unless_blocked}`, `mods::render_tests::agents_sub_agent_count_takes_the_most_urgent_colour_and_counts_child_records`, `window::tests::header_info_follows_changes` (`{subagents}` follows record changes, not frames), `scripts/settings.test.ts` (suggested template).
- Files: `src/header_info.rs`, `src/window.rs` (`publish`), `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/mods/agents/main.js`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `scripts/bench.py` (`--set subagents`), `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`.
- Upstream: none.

### Sub-agent display

`feat/subagent-display` (branched from `master`, fork only): `agents_subagents` in settings.json says how the agents list shows each agent's live sub-agents: `"tree"` (the default, as before), `"folded"` (every agent starts folded: its count shows, a click opens it) or `"off"` (no sub-agent rows, count or chevron). A fold or unfold the person makes is kept across restarts, and `agents.fold_all` / `agents.unfold_all` fold or open every agent.

- Setting: `AgentsSubagents` in `src/settings.rs`, typed as `sidebar_shade` is (another value is a settings.json error; tree is left out when written). Herdr only passes it on, as `settings().agents_subagents`; builds without it give `undefined`, which the mod draws as a tree. Settings > Agents > Activity, "Sub-agents" (Tree, Folded, Off), under Working spinner.
- Off: no tree, as on the narrow rail, so a kept sub-agent (one with a pane) gets its own row again. A blocked sub-agent still moves its parent into Needs you, the rule the Dock badge counts by: off hides rows, not urgency. `{subagents}` in `pane_header_info` doesn't follow the setting: the header key is its own opt-in, and the Settings help says so.
- Remembered folds: the mod's localStorage (`data/agents/store.json` in the app home: this app's, per mod, not the machine's shared document, since a fold is a view preference), key `folds`, `{ address: true | false }`: only a choice against the setting is kept (a fold that matches it is dropped, so changing the setting later reaches that agent; an unfold all under tree keeps nothing). A fold goes when its agent's `changed` entry does (gone from the list and unchanged for 30 days), so a start that briefly lists nothing, or a machine offline for a while, keeps its folds. Group-header folds stay in memory, as before.
- Fold all / unfold all: `agents.fold_all` and `agents.unfold_all`, the mod's own commands through `nextCommand()`, bindable in keys.json (no default keys), and palette entries "Fold all agents" and "Unfold all agents" from its `mod.json` `actions`. They set every agent listed at that moment; agents that start later follow the setting. Like any `<mod>.<command>`, they reach only a shown copy: with the agents list off screen the app's existing "need the mod loaded" error toasts.
- Palette (Rust): a mod.json action could name only app commands. It may now name the mod's own `<mod>.<command>`, with no args (`mods::own_command`); the palette runs it as a key does, through `run_as`'s `<mod>.<command>` path. Another mod's command is still refused and named in `herdr-app status`; `mod.clone` rewrites `"<from>.` strings in the copy's mod.json, so a clone's entries name its own commands. The command after the dot can't be checked (a mod's commands aren't declared), so a typo lists and does nothing.
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? The setting no: the app passes the value and the mod draws; the palette entry yes, the app checks mod.json actions. (2) Can only the app see it? No: `settings()` reaches mods. (3) Can a mod not do it? Rows, folds, their persistence and the commands are the mod's; only the typed settings key (and its Settings window row) and the palette accepting a mod's own command need the app. Verdict: D (fork only) for the setting, which follows Sub-agent status and Pane header info; B for the palette's mod commands, a small general change that could be offered on its own.
- Limits: pruning counts time since the agent's last state change, as `changed` always has, so an agent idle over 30 days on a machine that drops off loses its fold at the next local state change. Two copies of the agents mod on screen at once write one store, last write wins, as `changed` does.
- Perf: no per-frame work; the mod reads one more setting per render and writes localStorage only on a fold, a fold-all or a prune. Bench (release, A/B against master 319c586, `scripts/bench.py --set subagents`, alternating rounds, a run overlapping a CI job dropped): subagents-churn 216 Minstr/s master (2 runs), 228 tree (3), 201 folded, 178 off; app CPU 2.6–2.7% master, 2.5–2.7% tree, 2.1–2.9% folded and off; window draws 2.1/s in every run; idle 33–36 Minstr/s either way. Tree is within the spread of repeated runs (off alone ranged 170–192).
- Tests: `scripts/agents.test.ts` (each mode in both sorts, a kept sub-agent's own row under off, folds kept across a restart and over the setting from the chevron and the icon, pruning with `changed`, fold all and unfold all, `nextCommand` delivery), `scripts/settings.test.ts` (the Activity row), `settings::derivation_tests::agents_subagents_defaults_to_tree_and_reaches_mods`, `settings::tests::the_guide_explains_every_setting`, `mods::tests::palette_actions_take_the_mods_own_commands` (and the shipped mod.json's entries), `mods::tests::cloned_mods_rewrite_assets_and_self_overlays_and_reject_nonobject_metadata` (a clone's own commands), `mods::render_tests::agents_sub_agents_follow_the_setting_remembered_folds_and_fold_all` (each mode, a fold read back from localStorage at start, fold all, unfold all), `window::tests::mod_actions` (counts its own mod's actions now that the agents mod ships two).
- Files: `defaults/mods/agents/{main.js,mod.json}`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `src/settings.rs`, `src/mods.rs`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `src/window.rs` (test), `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `scripts/agents.test.ts`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`.
- Upstream: none.

### Sub-agent click

`feat/subagent-click-setting` (branched from `master`, fork only): `agents_subagent_click` in settings.json says what a click on a sub-agent row should open: `"claude"` (the default: the sub-agent in Claude Code's own view, which the person's own agents mod reaches with guarded keys) or `"transcript"` (a transcript popup).

- Setting: `AgentsSubagentClick` in `src/settings.rs`, typed as `agents_subagents` is (another value is a settings.json error; claude is left out when written, and the app's own rewrites of settings.json keep transcript). Herdr only passes it on, as `settings().agents_subagent_click`; builds without it give `undefined`, to be read as claude. Settings > Agents > Activity, "Sub-agent click" (Claude's view, Transcript), right after "Sub-agents".
- The shipped agents mod doesn't act on it: a click on a sub-agent row still focuses the parent agent's pane, or peeks it when that agent isn't listed. It is a preference agents mods may honour; the Settings help, `defaults/AGENTS.md` and `defaults/herdr.d.ts` say so.
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? No: the app types the value and passes it on; a mod acts on the click. (2) Can only the app see it? No: `settings()` reaches mods. (3) Can a mod not do it? A mod could keep its own preference, but only the app can type a settings.json key and draw its Settings window row. Verdict: D (fork only): the toggle exists for a fork-only mod behaviour (opening Claude Code's own sub-agent view through guarded keys), and follows Sub-agent display.
- Perf: no render-path change. `Settings::for_mods()` is built once per settings change and shared as one `Arc` (`settings::for_mods()`), so the key adds one short string to a value mods already receive; the shipped mod doesn't read it. No bench run.
- Tests: `settings::derivation_tests::agents_subagent_click_defaults_to_claude_reaches_mods_and_survives_a_rewrite` (the default, each value reaching mods, `write_person`'s text read back, other values refused), `settings::tests::the_guide_explains_every_setting`, `scripts/settings.test.ts` (the Activity row, its place after Sub-agents and what it writes).
- Files: `src/settings.rs`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`.
