### Agent card info

`feat/agents-card-info` (branched from `master`, fork only): `agents_card_info` in settings.json is a template of what an agent's card in an agents list shows beside its name, in `pane_header_info`'s grammar. Unset, its default `@{remote} · {space}` is the person's agents panel as it was: another machine's name as a chip, then the space where no space header names it. Set empty: nothing.

- Keys (`src/header_info.rs`): every `pane_header_info` key, and `{machine}` (the address's machine, `local` for this computer), `{remote}` (the same, empty for this computer) and `{space}` (the space's name as `agents()` gives it). The three work in `pane_header_info` too; they are only words, so there is no reason to refuse them there. `{remote}` exists so a default can name another machine and stay quiet here, while `{machine}` always names one.
- Context: whether a card's space goes without saying depends on the mod's sort, pins and name, which only the mod knows. So with `{space}` in the template Herdr also resolves the info without it (`card_info.no_space`, null when nothing is left), and the mod picks: the person's panel uses it under a space header or when the name is the space, the shipped mod always (its rows name the space in their header or name). A segment such as `{space} {branch}` keeps its branch there, which a per-segment "context" flag would have dropped whole.
- One implementation, in Rust, beside `header_info`: `publish` works out each agent's values once for both templates (status, request, folder info, sub-agents only when a template uses them, space, machine) and resolves the card into `agents()[i].card_info`, `{ text, segments, no_space? }`. Only when publish runs (a status, report, terminal-list, folder or settings change), never per frame; the minute timer (`header_tick`) counts `{elapsed}` in either template; folder info counts ahead, behind and dirty when either uses them (`counts_git`). Mods get `cardInfoPreview(template | null)` for a settings preview.
- Settings > Agents > Activity, "Card info", after "Header info": the field holds the template in effect (the default while unset), written on Enter or leaving it; "Use suggested" and typing the default leave the key out; blank is written as `""` (no card info). The preview draws each segment as a chip, for a sample agent on another machine (`@vps`, `herdr-app`).
- Shipped `agents` mod: draws `card_info` (its `no_space`) in place of the machine's name only once `settings().agents_card_info` is set, so by default its rows are byte for byte as before (it never had a space chip, and drew the machine without the `@`). The person's own panel (chezmoi `agent-deck-agents`) draws a chip per segment, and its chips as before on builds without `card_info`.
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? Yes: it resolves the template where `pane_header_info` is resolved, with values only the app has in hand (folder info, request, sub-agent counts) on the app's own change and minute schedule. (2) Can only the app see it? Partly: a mod sees agents, terminals and the branch, but not ahead/behind/dirty or the request words, and a settings.json key a mod reads must be typed by the app (unknown keys are dropped). (3) Can a mod not do it? A mod could parse a template itself, but it would duplicate the grammar and recount per render. Verdict: D (fork only), following `pane_header_info`, which upstream does not have.
- Perf: no per-frame work (`card_info_follows_changes`: no resolution over 30 refreshes, no timer for the default). Bench (release, A/B against master f2e2d11, `scripts/bench.py --set subagents`, three alternating rounds, none overlapping CI or another bench, none spanning a sleep): subagents-churn 227 Minstr/s master (240, 221, 221), 229 branch with the default card (235, 231, 221), 238 with `{machine} · {space} · {branch}{dirty} · {state} {elapsed} · {subagents}` (241, 238, 236); app CPU 3.0 / 2.8 / 2.8%; window draws 2.1/s in every run; idle 32 / 33 / 33 Minstr/s. The default is within the spread of repeated runs; a template with sub-agent counts and git keys costs about 5% more instructions in the churn, app CPU unchanged.
- Tests: `header_info::tests::{machine_is_always_named_and_remote_only_off_this_computer, the_default_card_is_the_remote_machine_then_the_space, a_card_without_space_keeps_the_other_keys_of_its_segment, card_previews_resolve_the_sample_on_another_machine, templates_split_on_separators_and_drop_empty_segments}`, `window::tests::card_info_follows_changes_and_the_minute_not_frames` (the default on every publish that lists the agent and never per frame, no timer; `{elapsed}` times the minute; empty resolves nothing), `window::tests::header_info_follows_changes_and_the_minute_not_frames` (cards off, so its counts stay the header's), `settings::derivation_tests::agents_card_info_is_null_unless_set_and_reaches_mods_as_written` (and the Settings window's default is the app's), `settings::tests::the_guide_explains_every_setting`, `mods::render_tests::agents_rows_draw_card_info_once_set`, `scripts/settings.test.ts` (the field, the default, blank, Use suggested, the chips preview, the row's place, opening), `scripts/agents.test.ts` (the shipped rows, unset, set, no `no_space`, null, an older build).
- Files: `src/header_info.rs`, `src/window.rs` (`publish`, `apply_look`, `track_folders`), `src/settings.rs`, `src/mods.rs` (`cardInfoPreview`), `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/mods/agents/main.js`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `scripts/agents.test.ts`.
- Upstream: none.

### Floating panes

`feat/floating-panes` (branched from `master`, phase 1 of `fork/design/floating-panes.md`; upstream issue herdrdev/herdr-app#718): any pane of a tab can float above the tab's tiled panes and dock back. Several floats per tab, back to front; the focused one is in front. `Tab.floating: Vec<FloatPane>` holds the same pane block the tree would (id, terminal or mod, name), its rect as fractions of the tab area, and a `DockHint`. Floats are structure: kept in the machine's document beside `root`, so every screen of the machine shows them, with their order; focus and zoom stay this screen's.

- Commands: `pane.float {pane?, rect?}`, `pane.dock {pane?, target?, edge?}`, `pane.float_toggle`, `pane.float_move {direction | rect | to, cells?}`, `pane.float_resize {direction, cells?}`, `pane.raise` / `pane.lower {to?}`, `pane.float_cycle {reverse?}`. `pane.go` reaches floats (front first on a tie), `pane.zoom` on a float fills the tab on this screen, a zoomed tiled pane keeps the floats above it, `pane.swap {target}` trades a float's and a tiled pane's places, `pane.split` on a float splits the tiled pane it would dock beside, and `pane.arrange` leaves floats alone. `panes()[i].floating` is `{rect, z, hidden}`; `herdr-app ls` gives a float's terminal its `floating` rect; the shipped `menu` offers Float pane, or Dock pane and Bring to front, and the shipped `pane` header a float button (Float, or Dock when floating; `pane.float_toggle`) between split and zoom, its room counted in the header info's fit (five hover buttons, 140 px).
- Docking: beside the pane (or split) it was split with, on the same side at the same share; an arranged tab puts it there in reading order and arranges again (a master pane comes back as master); else beside the tiled pane focused last. That gives the same tab back in every arrange mode when the neighbour is still there and the tree unchanged; after other changes it lands beside its neighbour, near but not exact. Neither tuios nor Rex restores more. All of it is in `src/float_pane.rs` (`lift`, `place`, `DockHint`), run on float and dock only.
- Both layers in one place: `Tab::panes`, `terminals`, `addresses`, `find`, `find_mut`, `pane`, `path_to` cover tiled and floating panes, and every caller that asked "which panes does this tab have" goes through them. `float_pane::tests::tiled_only_walks_stay_inside_tab` fails on a `.root.panes()`, `.root.terminals()`, or `.root.find(…)` outside `impl Tab`: a float's terminal missed by such a walk has no pane, and the orphan scan adopts it again as a duplicate tiled pane.
- Drawing: a tab without floats draws as before (`render_tab` returns the tiled tree unchanged). With floats, they draw in an absolute layer over it, each `occlude`d, opaque, framed by the layout's pane frame (else a border and a shadow), at least 20 × 4 cells, the terminal a whole number of cells. The cell size is measured only in frames whose shown tab has floats. Moving a float changes neither the tiled panes' rows nor their bounds.
- Perf (release, A/B against master 5664e1d, alternating rounds, `scripts/bench.py --set floats` for the float rows): idle, burst and spinners within noise on instructions (spinners: 65.7 vs 62.3 Minstr/s over four rounds, app CPU 1.6 vs 1.2%). A static float over a streaming agent costs nothing (rows and frame time as the agent alone). A float moved ten times a second over four streaming agents rebuilds no rows under it (214 vs 211 rows/s still) and costs the same per drawn frame (2.4 ms); the extra is the moves themselves: ten more draws a second, and each keyboard step republishes `panes()` (about 80 mod script runs a second) and writes the document, which a mouse drag (phase 2) must do once per gesture instead.
- Keys: macOS `cmd-alt-enter` (float or dock), `cmd-ctrl-arrows` (move), `cmd-ctrl-shift-arrows` (resize); unbound elsewhere. The person's `ctrl-b` keys live in chezmoi.
- Three questions: yes, yes, yes (the app lays out, focuses, hit-tests, sizes the terminal; only it sees the tab area and cells; mods can't keep a pane's identity or the document). Verdict C: #718 first; phase 1 goes upstream after the maintainer's answer. Mouse move and resize (phase 2) and hide, snapping, drag-to-dock (phase 3) are not built; their hooks are `render_float_pane` and `float_bounds`.
- Limits: a float inside a tiled pane's area isn't reached by `pane.go` from beside that pane (`pane.float_cycle` reaches it); a hidden tab's float keeps its last size until the tab is shown (it isn't refit on a resize); a float's rect can be smaller than its drawn minimum of 20 × 4 cells; a pane drag can choose a drop target a float covers; a zoomed float docked stays zoomed (now as a tiled pane); a pane inside a newer build's floating split is counted (never adopted twice) but can't be closed on its own. Focus follows mouse focuses a float without raising it; `pane.zoom` focuses the pane it zooms.
- Tests: `float_pane::tests::*` (clamping, lift and place, fallbacks, a float-then-dock per arrange mode and pane, identity, last tiled pane, focus and z-order, arrange and swap, zoom, the walk lint), `mirror::tests::floats_go_in_the_machines_document_and_focus_stays_on_the_screen`, `window::pane_floats::tests::*` (move, snap, resize), `window::tests::floating_panes` in its own process (drawn rect, click focus and raise not reaching the tile below, `pane.go` with the front tie, cycle, zoom, wrong-layer errors, no re-adoption by the orphan scan, restart, docking order).
- Files: `src/float_pane.rs`, `src/model.rs`, `src/mirror.rs`, `src/window/pane_floats.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/window/{pane_drag,pane_size,follow_mouse}.rs`, `src/arrange.rs` (test-only `rects`), `src/commands.rs`, `src/main.rs`, `defaults/keys.macos.json`, `defaults/mods/menu/main.js`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `docs/commands.md`, `scripts/bench.py` (`--set floats`), `fork/design/floating-panes.md`
- Upstream: issue #718; pull request waits for the answer.

### Tab to another space

`feat/tab-to-space` (branched from `master`, fork only): a whole tab moves to another space of its machine. Before, `tab.move` only reordered tabs inside their space, and the person's `pane-move` mod moved one pane at a time by parking and keeping its terminal.

- Command: `tab.move_to_space {space, id?, index?}` (id: the shown tab; index: 1 = first, default last). `Model::move_tab_to_space` moves the `Tab` value itself, so its tiled tree, `floating` layer, name, `rest` (a newer build's fields), icon (kept by tab id) and this screen's focus, zoom, `before` and arrange mode go with it; terminals keep running. It shows the tab where it went (space and tab selected, a settled tab back in the lists), as pane-move follows its pane. The space it leaves shows the tab that slid into its place, as after `tab.close`; a space left empty closes through `remove_space` (remembered in recent) and ends nothing.
- Errors, nothing changed: an id that is no tab (a pane id included: only `Space::tab` ids match, not `space_of`'s node lookup that makes upstream's `tab.move` panic), an unknown space, its own space, a space on another machine (a tab's terminals stay on theirs), an index that isn't a position, the machine's layout unavailable (`require_document`, as for every structural command).
- Shared document: the move is structure, written with the machine's document by `flush_documents`. Another screen of the same machine reads the tab under its new space and keeps its own view of it, since `View` keys focus, zoom and settled by tab id; a space the move emptied is gone there too. Unknown tab fields travel in `Tab::rest`.
- Labels: the moved tab's terminals get `herdr.space` set to the new space (the local id on a remote machine), so one ever found without a pane is put back there. `adopt` also prefers the space a labeled tab is in now over the label's space, so a stale label (a write lost or out of order, a terminal not yet listed) never makes the moved tab twice, and a `terminal.keep` in flight lands in its tab's space after a move instead of being parked.
- Menu and picker: the tab menu has Move to space… after Move left and right, only when its machine has another space. It opens the shipped `tab-move` mod (`mod.open {name: "tab-move", props: {id?}}`, also in the palette as Move tab to space…): the other spaces of the tab's machine, 1-9, j/k and arrows, Enter, Esc; `/` or any other printable key searches by name (start matches first, then inside, then scattered), and while searching every character types. A New space… entry is not offered: `space.new` starts a terminal of its own, so it would leave an extra shell.
- Keys: no default (no free key fits the tab-move pattern); `mod.open:tab-move` binds the picker.
- Perf: nothing on the render path. The command runs on demand; the menu scans `spaces()` once when it opens; the picker filters once per keystroke.
- Three questions: yes, yes, mostly. The app owns the model, the shared document and the core labels, and only it can move a tab with its floats and per-screen view intact; a mod could only rebuild a tab pane by pane through park and keep, losing its layout, floats and zoom. The picker could live in a mod outside the app, but the menu entry needs it shipped. Verdict C: ask the maintainer in an issue once floating panes (herdrdev/herdr-app#718) settle, since the move carries `Tab.floating`; without floats the command and the menu entry would go as they are.
- Tests: `model::tests::{a_tab_moves_whole_to_another_space_and_is_shown_there, moving_a_spaces_last_tab_closes_the_space_and_ends_nothing}`, `mirror::tests::a_tab_moved_on_one_screen_keeps_the_other_screens_view_of_it`, `window::tests::a_tab_moves_to_another_space_with_its_panes_and_terminals` in its own process (labels for tiled and floating terminals and a remote space's local id, errors, unavailable layout, emptied space, adoption of a stale label, a keep after a move), `commands::tests`, `mods::render_tests::{tab_menu_offers_move_to_space_only_with_another_space_on_its_machine, tab_move_picker_lists_the_other_spaces_of_the_tabs_machine}`, `scripts/tab-move.test.ts`, `scripts/menu.test.ts`.
- Files: `src/model.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/commands.rs`, `src/mirror.rs` (test), `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/mods/tab-move/{main.js,mod.json}`, `defaults/mods/menu/main.js`, `defaults/frame/palette/main.js`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `docs/commands.md`, `scripts/tab-move.test.ts`, `scripts/menu.test.ts`, `justfile`
- Upstream: none yet

### Frame mods keep a code revision

`fix/frame-mod-revision` (branched from `master`, the upstream pull request's change): the code cache upstream added in `029323e` walks and hashes a mod's whole folder on each load when the file watcher has no revision for it, and only `mods/<name>` had one. The app's own frame mods (palette, settings, welcome) therefore walked their tree on every open. A code file edited under `frame/<name>` now mints that folder's revision the same way, without a reload (frame mods load fresh on each open), and an event on `frame/` itself, as when the app rewrites it at start, invalidates every revision.

- Measured (release, `load_app` timed, 20 opens each, two alternating runs, median): palette 156–162 → 32 µs, settings 170–192 → 32–34 µs, a `mods/` mod (picker) unchanged at about 32 µs.
- Tests: `files::tests::frame_mods_get_revisions_without_reloading` (fails without the change).
- Files: `src/files.rs` (`code_root`, `frame_file_counts`, `changes`, `FileEvents::revision_provider`)
- Upstream: pull request #719 (`up/frame-mod-revision`), awaiting review

### Mod reads faint text

`feat/mod-faint-text` (branched from `master`): `terminalLines(terminal, { lines })` gives a mod a terminal's last lines that carry anything, each `{ text, faint }`, `faint` the `[start, end)` stretches drawn faint (SGR 2) in `text.slice` offsets (UTF-16 units). `hc read` returns plain text, so Claude Code's faint suggestion of the next prompt (`❯ suggested text`) reads the same as text typed there, and a mod that acts only on an empty prompt (the person's agent-deck sub-agent view switch) refuses after nearly every turn.

- Where: `hc read` is served by herdr-core's own screen model (`terminal_read` in `herdr-core/src/api.rs`), which keeps text only; the core stays as it is. The app already keeps a ghostty grid for every terminal in any of its tabs (only visible ones are drawn), with each cell's style, and `Vt::tail_text` already reads its last lines for the agent screen rules (`harness.rs`). `Vt::tail_lines` is the same walk asking `ghostty_grid_ref_style` per cell; `tail_text` passes `styles: false` and skips that call, so the per-tick harness read costs what it did.
- Shape: an async mod function that sends `Request::Lines` to the window and resolves with the reply: an array of lines, or null when no tab in this window holds that terminal (a parked one, one on a closed screen). It rejects when no window takes requests. Wide characters count once (their spacer cell is skipped), a cell without text reads as a space, trailing blanks are trimmed and a faint stretch is cut at the trim. Bold, italic and colors are left out until a mod needs them.
- Cost: computed only when called, on the window thread, over at most the screen's rows (all rows when `lines` is omitted): one grid walk, nothing per frame or per tick.
- Perf (release, A/B against master d4ebb1d, `scripts/bench.py` default set, runs dropped when they overlapped a CI job or another bench, spanned a sleep, or drew nothing): agent 244/255 Minstr/s master against 238/256/271, four-panes 365/358 against 343/331/373, idle-busy-bg 66/68 against 61/75/61, app CPU within 1% in every scenario. ten-agents is bimodal for the same binary: master drew 9.9 and 10.5 window draws a second in two runs (with mod scripts at 2.9/s) and 18.9 and 18.2 in two more, the branch 18.9–19.1 in five; at matching draw rates app CPU is 4.3–7.3% master and 5.0–7.1% here.
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? No, it reports. (2) Can only the app see it? Here yes: the core's screen model drops styles, and the app's grid is the one place a mod can get them without a core change. (3) Can a mod not do it? Yes, it can't: a mod sees only `hc read`'s text. Verdict B: a small general mod API, offered upstream on its own once used here.
- Tests: `vt::tests::tail_lines_mark_what_is_drawn_faint` (a faint suggestion after `❯` and a no-break space, wide characters inside a faint stretch, an emoji's two UTF-16 units, faint and normal runs on one line, faint trailing blanks trimmed, a line of only faint blanks dropped, `tail_text` reading the same text), `vt::tests::tail_text_is_the_last_lines_that_carry_anything`.
- Files: `src/vt.rs`, `src/terminal.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/mods.rs`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none yet

### Cursor trail

`feat/cursor-trail` (branched from `master`, fork only): `cursor_trail` in settings.json (`"off"`, the default, or `"panes"`), as Rex's and kitty's cursor trails do. When the focus moves from one pane of the shown tab to another (keys, a click, `pane.go`, `agent.next`, float cycling, a split or a close), a glowing streak runs from the new pane's cursor back to the old one's and retracts into the cursor within `cursor_trail_duration_ms` (default 120, held within 50 to 1000), in the theme's `cursor` color (kitty's `cursor_trail_color none`), or `cursor_trail_color` (`"#rrggbb"`) when set. Cursor moves inside a pane never draw one.

- Ends: each end is the terminal's `trail_anchor` (through `pane_term`, so a pane showing a swapped-in view reads that view), its cursor cell from the last paint (the `cursor_at` the input method already uses), shown or hidden, since agents hide theirs at their input; when the cursor is scrolled out of view, the terminal's middle cell. The focused pane's anchor is kept at each draw (`Seen`), so closing the focused pane still starts a trail from where its cursor was. A pane that isn't a painted terminal (never drawn) gets no trail, rather than a guessed point. The head reads the new pane's anchor every frame, so it lands where that pane's cursor is drawn.
- Shape, Rex's: a tapered streak (`streak`, a pure function of the two cells, the progress and a width): its head is the new cursor cell's middle from the first frame, a cell high with a round nose; its sides narrow (`COMET_TAPER`, now in `cursor_shapes.rs`) to a point at the tail, which starts on the old cursor's middle and decays exponentially into the head (time constant a third of the duration, scaled by `settle` to land exactly at the end). GPUI has no blur, so the glow is the same outline drawn wider and fainter behind the core (`COMET_LAYERS`: 2.4, 1.7 and 1.0 cell heights at 6%, 15% and 35%, in the cursor color), with a core 0.45 of a cell high in the cursor color mixed 45% toward white, so a yellow cursor reads as a yellow-white streak with an orange-yellow glow. Everything fades as the streak shortens. Four paths a frame, only while a trail runs. A style would be another outline function chosen in `cursor_trail_layer`.
- Cost: nothing in the terminals' output or paint paths. `AppWindow::render` calls `watch_cursor_trail`, which with the setting off reads one setting (and clears its state once if it holds any); on, it compares the active tab's focused pane with the one it saw last and keeps that pane's anchor (a map lookup and a cell read). Any change of the seen pane or tab ends a running trail before another may start, and a trail whose pane has no terminal any more ends at the next draw. A trail is a canvas after the pane tree and before overlays (above tiled and floating panes, below dialogs) that asks for the next frame while it runs; the first render after its time drops it, so it asks for no more. Its duration is clamped and its progress only grows, so every trail ends; a covered window draws nothing and drops the trail on its next draw. "All" mode (in-pane cursor jumps) came later, as a measured exception: see Cursor effects.
- Settings window: the Cursor page (see Cursor effects). The color has no control, as `unfocused_dim_color`.
- Hot-path lines touched: none in `terminal.rs`'s paint or output code; `TerminalView::trail_anchor` is a new reader called only when a trail starts or draws. In `window.rs`: two calls in `AppWindow::render` (`watch_cursor_trail`, `cursor_trail_layer`) and one `.children(trail)`.
- Perf (release, A/B against master c33bae7, `scripts/bench.py` default set and `--set trail`, 15 s per scenario, two alternating rounds of master / branch with the setting off / branch with `"panes"`, the machine's CI runner stopped, none spanning a sleep; Minstr/s is app, core and holder together): at rest the three match, idle-shell 20.2 / 20.4 / 20.9, idle-main 36.1 / 35.0 / 35.3, idle-busy-bg 60.9 / 59.5 / 58.6, window draws 0.1 to 0.2 a second in every one, so no frames at rest. Streaming: agent 352 / 339 / 341, four-panes 530 / 528 / 519, ten-agents 452 / 432 / 433, window draws equal (18.4 / 18.0 / 18.1 for agent). trail-still (two agents streaming beside a shell) 517 / 498 / 515; trail-hops (the same with the focus hopping twice a second) 633 / 607 / 654 Minstr/s and 20.1 / 19.6 / 21.1 window draws a second: about 3% more instructions than master, and a draw more a second, while trails run. App CPU % is bimodal for the same binary and work (about 4% or 8 to 13% on agent, as the work lands on efficiency or performance cores), so instructions are the measure; per run it showed in master, off and panes alike.
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? It draws it; the app alone knows when its focus moves and where each terminal's cursor is painted. (2) Can only the app see it? Yes: window coordinates of the cursor cells are paint-time facts a mod never sees. (3) Can a mod not do it? A mod can't paint over the panes or follow a cursor per frame. Verdict D (fork only), a visual preference.
- Tests: `window::cursor_trail::tests::{a_trail_runs_its_duration_then_ends, the_core_is_the_cursor_color_paler}`, `window::cursor_shapes::tests::comet_{the_trail_fades_as_it_shortens, the_head_is_on_the_cursor_a_cell_high_and_the_tail_a_point, the_tail_retracts_into_the_head, the_glow_layers_share_the_tail_and_widen, the_streak_points_any_way_and_is_finite, every_layer_is_a_convex_outline_at_any_length, nothing_is_drawn_for_the_same_cell}` (moved there with the shapes), `window::tests::cursor_trail_runs_between_panes_then_ends` (off keeps nothing; turning it on starts none; b to a starts one from b's cursor with a layer; after its time the next draw drops it and has no layer; the same pane starts none; closing the focused pane starts one from where it drew its cursor; a trail whose pane is gone ends; a tab change ends a running trail and starts none; off again forgets), `settings::tests::{cursor_trail_is_off_by_default_and_stays_out_of_settings_json, cursor_trail_duration_and_color_are_kept_in_range}`, `settings::tests::the_guide_explains_every_setting`, `scripts/settings.test.ts` (the picker, Trail time shown only while on, its steps and range, the row's place).
- Bench: `scripts/bench.py --set trail` (two agents streaming beside a shell: still, then the focus hopping twice a second).
- Review (independent reviewer, before the pull request): fixed a trail outliving a tab change or its destination closing (it held the terminal view; now it holds the pane and ends on any focus change), swapped views (`pane_term`), the window test's real sleep (the trail's start is backdated instead), a close drawing no trail (the anchor kept per draw), and one extra frame after the end. After looks in an isolated instance and beside Rex, the shape went from a fading comet to kitty's decaying quad to Rex's glowing tapered streak, the default time to 120 ms, and the color is the theme's cursor.
- Styles, arrival pulses and in-pane jumps: Cursor effects, below.
- Files: `src/window/cursor_trail.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/terminal.rs` (`trail_anchor`), `src/settings.rs` (`CursorTrail`, `trail_duration`, `trail_color`), `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `scripts/bench.py`
- Upstream: none

### Cursor effects

`feat/cursor-effects` (branched from `master` after Cursor trail, fork only): more looks for the trail, a pulse where the cursor lands, and trails for cursor jumps inside the focused pane, modelled on [sahaj-b/ghostty-cursor-shaders](https://github.com/sahaj-b/ghostty-cursor-shaders) (MIT), redrawn as GPUI paths rather than ported from GLSL.

- `cursor_trail_style` (`comet`, the default and Cursor trail's streak unchanged; `tail`, kitty's cursor-sized quad whose corners decay into the cursor, those facing the move first, Cursor trail's second look; `sweep`, a solid block from the old cursor shrinking (easing out) into the new one; `warp`, Neovide's stretched block with soft edges from wider, fainter copies, and its half nearer the cursor drawn again so it fades toward the tail). Each is a pure function of the two cells and the progress in `src/window/cursor_shapes.rs`, returning its layers back to front; filled layers are convex hulls (Andrew's monotone chain over the cells' corners), so GPUI's path fill never meets a self-crossing outline.
- `cursor_arrival` (`off`, the default; `ripple`, an expanding stroked ring; `boom`, an expanding disc; `ripple_rect` and `boom_rect`, the same as cursor-shaped boxes), each fading as it grows over `cursor_trail_duration_ms`, which the trail and the pulse share (one duration keeps it simple; Effect time in the Settings window). It fires on the same focus changes as the trail, with the trail off (the pulse alone) or on (both, the pulse at the new cursor), and in `"all"` mode after a settled jump or a cursor shape change in the focused pane.
- Colors: the theme's `cursor`, or `cursor_trail_color`, for both; the comet's core paler.
- `cursor_trail: "all"`: the pane changes, and jumps of the focused pane's cursor, settled as kitty does. `cursor_jumps.rs` (`Jumps`, pure) keeps the last settled spot (cell and shape), the last move and whether a settle timer is pending. In `paint_grid`, where `cursor_at` is already set and the settings were already read for the scroll thumb, one branch on the mode; only with `"all"`: for the focused terminal, compare the drawn cursor with the last one seen and, on a move, note it with the executor's clock and start the one settle timer if none is pending (no allocation, inside the existing borrow); an unfocused terminal forgets its spots once. The timer (`settle_jumps`) waits `cursor_trail_settle_ms`; on waking, if the cursor moved since, it waits the remainder, else it settles: a move of more than `cursor_trail_min_cells` (Chebyshev, so a diagonal step is one cell; kitty's start threshold, `kitty/cursor_trail.c` skips the trail when `abs(dx) <= threshold_x && abs(dy) <= threshold_y`, with one shared threshold here for both axes; a value set before this meant the least distance, so it now ignores one cell more, with no migration) from the last settled cell is a trail from there to here plus the pulse, a shape change alone is the pulse, anything else nothing (`TerminalEvent::CursorJump`). Stillness is measured from the program's own cursor moves, as kitty measures from `position_changed_by_client_at`: the output path (`Grid::write_output`, only with `"all"`) reads the cursor's cell before and after each output chunk (`Vt::cursor_xy`, two field reads) and, when it moved, notes the time in `Jumps::program_moved`; a move paint sees counts from that time, and the settle timer waits on while the program moved since; once still, if the program's last move hasn't been drawn yet it looks again every 8 ms for the frame that draws it (100 ms at most, in case none comes), so a trail never ends on a stale cell; a shape change alone (which the output path doesn't see) counts from when it is drawn; and a terminal not drawn for half a second (another tab, a covered window) gives its pending timer up and forgets, rather than wake on while its output streams. Output-path lines touched: `TerminalView::ingest` binds the settings once per chunk (it read them inline for `clipboard_write`) and writes through `Grid::write_output`, which with `"all"` off is `vt.write` behind one `bool` (`track.then(...)` skips both cursor reads). Measured from paint instead, a streaming cursor looked still between frames (up to ~50 ms apart), so the settle time had to outlast them; from the program's moves it can be kitty's few ms, default 10. Typing settles one cell at a time, even three keys 30 ms apart, so it doesn't draw; output draws nothing until it pauses for the settle time, then once if the cursor moved more than the threshold (as kitty: a program printing a line every 50 ms with the cursor ending far along can draw at each line). A hidden cursor forgets, so reappearing is no jump (and agents, which hide theirs at their input, get no in-pane effects); other panes never take part. Cells are compared only within one view: how far the screen is scrolled back, its size and the settings (`Rc` identity, new on every change) are part of each observation, and a new view only re-settles, so scrolling back, a reflow, or turning "all" off and on again is never a jump. The threshold doesn't apply to pane changes.
- Settings window: a Cursor page (Appearance > Cursor) holds every cursor setting: Cursor trail (Off, Between panes, Panes and jumps), Trail style while it's on, Arrival effect, Effect time while either is on, Settle time (10 ms steps) and Ignore moves up to (cells) for Panes and jumps. The keys are unchanged. The theme's `colors.cursor` stays a theme color, set where theme colors are; there is no cursor shape or blink setting.
- Easing: no `cursor_effect_easing`; each look keeps its own curve (exponential decay for comet, tail and warp, ease-out for sweep and the pulses), which a choice would only blur.
- Hot-path lines touched: `src/terminal.rs` `paint_grid`, the scroll-thumb block: `crate::settings::get()` is bound once as `settings` (it was read inline for `pane_scrollbars`), then `if settings.cursor_trail == CursorTrail::All { ... }`. In the output path, `TerminalView::ingest` and `Grid::write_output` (Cursor effects, `"all"`, above). Nothing else in paint or output code.
- Perf (release, A/B against master a28ae9b, `scripts/bench.py` default set, `--set trail` and the new `--set jumps` (a focused shell at rest; a focused agent streaming; a focused shell bursting twenty commands every half second beside the streaming agent), 10 s per scenario, two alternating rounds of master / branch off / `"panes"` / `"all"` (no arrival pulse), the CI runner and other local builds paused, none spanning a sleep; Minstr/s is app, core and holder together, then window draws a second): at rest the four match: idle-shell 23.2 / 20.6 / 20.5 / 21.1, idle-main 12.2 / 11.9 / 11.8 / 11.9, jumps-rest 20.7 / 20.6 / 20.8 / 20.8, window draws 0 to 0.3 a second in every one. Streaming totals fell through the session for every config alike (agent ran 396 then 281 on master, 406 then 279 off), so the rounds compare best within themselves: round two (the later, steadier one) jumps-stream 253 / 254 / 201 / 203 and jumps-burst 280 / 279 / 244 / 246, agent 281 / 279 / 282 / 230, four-panes 359 / 375 / 589 / 296 (one outlier), window draws 18.9 to 19.1 for all four. `"all"` shows no cost at rest and none above the spread under streaming or bursts: one compare per paint of the focused terminal and one settle timer re-armed about every 80 ms while its cursor moves (these numbers predate the program-time settle; see the threshold change below). trail-hops draws 20.1 / 20.1 / 22.4 / 22.6 a second: trails cost about two draws a second while they run, as before.
- Perf of the program-time settle and the output-path stamp (release, A/B against master 135da19, `scripts/bench.py` default and `--set jumps`, 10 s per scenario, two alternating rounds of master / off / `"panes"` / `"all"`, CI runner and other local work paused, none spanning a sleep; Minstr/s, app, core and holder): at rest all four match (idle-shell 20.7 / 21.0 / 20.9 / 20.4, jumps-rest 20.8 / 20.4 / 20.6 / 20.8, window draws 0 to 0.2 a second). Streaming, master's first run ran high as the first of the session (agent 396 then 358), so its second round is the fair one: agent 358 / 360 / 363 / 360, ten-agents 411 / 413 / 412 / 411, jumps-stream 330 / 332 / 332 / 336, jumps-burst 373 / 371 / 369 / 373, window draws 17.7 to 18.3 for all. Off and `"panes"` equal master; `"all"` (two cursor reads per output chunk, the settle timer on the program's moves) is within about 1% of them.
- Three questions: as Cursor trail. Verdict D (fork only).
- Tests: `window::cursor_shapes::tests` (every style convex, finite and near its cells in four directions from 0.6 to 400 px; drawn as it starts, gone at its end, fading; tail, sweep and warp each as named; every pulse grows, fades, ends, convex, rings stroked and booms filled; the hull; and Cursor trail's comet tests), `cursor_jumps::tests` (fires once still for the settle time; a burst never fires until it stops, one timer, from where it started; small moves settle without an effect; a shape change pulses; hidden forgets; a settle of 0; forgetting keeps the one timer; Chebyshev), `window::tests::cursor_arrival_pulses_with_the_trail_off_or_on`, `window::tests::cursor_jumps_in_all_mode_draw_once_settled_and_never_mid_burst` (a jump draws nothing for under the settle time, then a trail and a pulse; 30 moves 16 ms apart draw nothing, then one effect and no other; a one-cell step, an unfocused pane and panes mode draw none), `settings::tests::{cursor_trail_style_and_arrival_default_to_comet_and_off_and_reach_mods, cursor_trail_all_with_its_settle_time_and_threshold}`, `scripts/settings.test.ts` (the Cursor page, each picker, what shows when, the steppers' ranges).
- Files: `src/window/cursor_shapes.rs`, `src/cursor_jumps.rs`, `src/window/cursor_trail.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/terminal.rs`, `src/settings.rs`, `src/main.rs`, `defaults/frame/settings/main.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`, `scripts/settings.test.ts`, `scripts/bench.py`
- Upstream: none

### Cursor trail frames draw the trail alone

`perf/trail-layer` (branched from `master` after Cursor effects, fork only): a frame that only a running cursor trail or arrival pulse asked for draws the trail and reuses the rest of the window. GPUI's `request_animation_frame` notifies the view painting, and the window was one view, so every trail frame re-ran `AppWindow::render`: the layout (`render_node`), the window frame, `mods::publish_window(window_json)`, the overlays and the trackers.

- Shape: `AppWindow` stays the window's root view. Its render keeps the per-frame settings (rem size, text look, veil) and the root's handlers, over three children: `lower`, a cached `Body` view that draws the layout through `AppWindow::render_lower` (with the checks the render made each draw: the clicked terminal, the window terminal, `tick_floats`, `watch_cursor_trail`, the title bar, `publish_window`); the trail's own view `TrailLayer` (window/cursor_trail.rs), uncached, whose paint asks `AppWindow::trail_frame` for the shapes and asks for the next frame; and `upper`, a cached `Body` drawing the overlays, the float and window trackers and the pane-drag layer through `render_upper`. The stacking is as before: panes, trail, overlays.
- Which frames reuse: the root reuses both bodies only when the trail's view is the one thing notified since the last draw (`Window::only_view_notified`) and neither body has a notified view under it (`view_dirty`); otherwise it marks both to draw again (`redraw_view`), so every other frame draws as the single render did. A refresh (focus, activation, resize) redraws everything as before. When a trail ends, the window draws once more in full, so anything that changed without a notify during it shows then: such a change (a setting or other global read in render, the window state mods get through `publish_window`) can lag by up to the trail's duration, where a full draw each frame showed it on the next frame.
- Terminals on screen: `crate::terminal::next_frame` (the frame number terminals compare `shown_in` with to know they are on screen) is counted in `render_lower`, so a reused layout keeps its terminals on screen and their output keeps drawing.
- Overlays draw after the layout has prepainted, so they read this frame's `top_chrome` and bounds: a top toast follows a bar that grew in the same frame, where it took one more frame (`toasts_below_the_chrome` asserts the new timing). The one-frame probe stays for a bar that moves after that.
- `vendor/gpui-pre` (`HERDR:`): `Window::only_view_notified` and `view_dirty` (read the draw's notified and dirty sets) and `redraw_view` (marks a cached view to draw again this frame, before it is laid out); `ViewElement::reuse_nested`: a cached view drawn again leaves the cached views under it (terminals, mods' content) to reuse their drawing when not notified, as they do under an uncached parent; GPUI otherwise makes them all draw again (`refreshing`). Both bodies use it; without it every layout draw rematerialised every mod (native materialisations went from 0.1 to about 21 a second at rest in the bench). Its doc limits it to a view its parent redraws whenever the parent draws.
- Bench: `scripts/bench.py --set leaps` (one pane whose cursor leaps far every 300 ms, `cursor_trail` off, then `"all"`); `--hc` and `--tmp` (an `hc` and a bench folder of the caller's choosing); `body` and `trail` counters (layout draws, trail frames) beside `window` in each line.
- Perf: release builds, A/B against master e1f167a, `scripts/bench.py` default set (trails off), `--set trail` and `--set leaps` with `cursor_trail: "all"`, `tail`, 140 ms, `cursor_trail_min_cells` 6, 15 s per scenario, five interleaved rounds per side, each an isolated app and core, under `caffeinate -di`, no zero-draw runs; other work kept the machine's load at 7 to 18, so streaming numbers spread widely. Medians, master / branch: leaps-all (a trail every 300 ms) frame time 12.9 / 7.6 ms/s, 0.66 / 0.33 ms a draw, 105.9 / 84.1 Minstr/s (app, core and holder), app CPU 3.3 / 3.3%; over leaps-off (2.8 / 2.3 ms/s) that is about 0.60 ms and 4.3 M instructions a trail frame on master and 0.27 ms and 2.5 M on the branch, the branch's figure including the one full draw at each trail's end (layout draws 6.8 a second: 3.4 of output, 3.4 trail ends; trail paints 13.3). At rest equal (idle-main 35.0 / 35.9 Minstr/s, window draws 0.1). Streaming within noise, both ways: agent 322.8 / 328.3 Minstr/s, frame 33.8 / 37.9 ms/s; ten-agents 428.8 / 444.9, frame 38.3 / 33.3; four-panes frame 37.1 / 45.3 (ranges 36.6 to 54.4 / 29.6 to 50.4); trail-hops (focus hopping beside streaming agents, every frame a layout draw either way) 46.4 / 46.0 ms/s.
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? Yes, it draws the trail. (2) Can only the app see it? Yes: frame scheduling and GPUI's view caching are the app's. (3) Can a mod not do it? A mod can't change how the window's frames are drawn. Verdict D (fork only): the cursor trail is fork-only. The GPUI patch has no separate use upstream: upstream Herdr nests no cached view inside another (terminals are cached under the uncached root; `ShellRoot` is added uncached, its content cached inside it, `src/window.rs` `render_slot`), so GPUI's forced `refreshing` never costs it a redraw. It would matter to upstream only if it cached a view holding others.
- Tests: `window::tests::trail_frames_draw_the_trail_and_reuse_the_rest_of_the_window` (b to a starts a trail; three frames it asks for draw the window and the trail, no layout; the terminals stay on screen; a notify of the window draws the layout again, reusing the terminals under it; over, it paints no more and the next frame draws the layout); mutation-checked: always redrawing the bodies, counting `next_frame` in the root, dropping `reuse_nested` and dropping the end-of-trail redraw each fail it. `window::tests::toasts_below_the_chrome` asserts the same-frame placement. Existing trail, arrival and jump tests unchanged.
- Files: `src/window.rs`, `src/window/cursor_trail.rs`, `src/terminal.rs` (a comment), `src/bench.rs`, `scripts/bench.py`, `vendor/gpui-pre/src/view.rs`, `vendor/gpui-pre/src/window.rs`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none

### Cursor trails steer, and the tail is kitty's

`feat/trail-looks` (branched from `master` after Cursor trail frames, fork only): three changes to how the trails move and where they end, after kitty 0.50's `kitty/cursor_trail.c` (its rules read and redrawn, not ported).

- Steering: a trail that starts while another is still drawn starts from what that one drew, not from where the cursor last settled: the tail's four corners, a comet's tail point, a sweep's or a warp's old end (`Trail::drawn`, noted each frame by `cursor_shapes::trail` and `Chase`; `start_from`). So a second move mid-trail bends the trail, as kitty's corners keep chasing a cursor that moves on. Jumps (`cursor_jump`) and pane changes (`watch_cursor_trail`) alike; a tab change still ends the trail and starts none. A shape change while a trail runs keeps the trail and starts the pulse anew (`Trail::pulse`). A tail steered from a tail keeps its fade, as kitty keeps one opacity. A trail's start is four corners (`cursor_shapes::Quad`) rather than a cell: a cursor's rect, or what was drawn.
- `tail` as kitty draws it (`cursor_shapes::Chase`): a quad whose corners each step `1 - 2^(-10·dt/decay)` of their way to the cursor's corners per frame (`dt` the time since the last frame, so the pace is time's, not the frame rate's), the decay set per corner between the fast and the slow by how far ahead of the move it faces (kitty's dot product, normalised across the four), so the corners ahead lead at the fast rate rather than snapping. Solid: the cursor's color at full strength, fading (at 1/decay a second) only while the cursor is hidden and back while it shows. The cursor's own rect is cut out (an even-odd path: the quad's hull with the rect clipped to it as a hole, Sutherland-Hodgman), as kitty's fragment shader drops what's under the cursor. It runs until every corner is within half a px of the cursor's on both axes (kitty's `needs_render`: `cursor_trail.c` compares against half a pixel, `g.dx / cell_width * 0.5`, not half a cell), capped at three durations; once caught up it stays ended for the rest of the effect, so a pulse still running never brings a tail back as the cursor moves on. Two differences from kitty: Herdr fills the hull of the four corners, where kitty draws its quad as is, so on a steer that reverses, when corners cross, Herdr's outline stays convex where kitty's pinches; and each trail starts at full strength, where kitty's opacity carries across moves (a run of moves between panes whose cursors stay hidden draws a fading tail here, none in kitty).
- The decays from `cursor_trail_duration_ms`: the slowest corner's decay is the duration, the fastest a quarter of it (kitty's `cursor_trail_decay 0.1 0.4` ratio). In that time a corner closes all but 1/1024 of its way, so 400 is kitty's default, and a long jump's tail lasts about the duration (0.39 s for 400 px at 400; at 140, about 56 ms for a cell, 135 ms for 400 px), a short one less. At short durations the fast corners close most of their way in a frame (96% at 140 ms and 60 Hz), as kitty's would at that decay.
- Shape-aware ends: every style ends on the cursor as the terminal draws it (`terminal::cursor_rect`, which paint now uses too: a block's or a hollow box's cell, a beam's 2 px at the left, an underline's 2 px at the foot), and starts from it: a jump's `from` is the rect for the shape the cursor had where it settled, two cells wide on a wide character (`Jump::Moved` carries the shape and `Spot::wide`), and a pane's start is the rect its last paint drew (`TerminalView::trail_end`, with the cell and whether the cursor shows). Sizes (the comet's width, the warp's soft edges) stay against the cell height; the arrival pulse stays on the cell.
- Perf (release, A/B against master d5f1261, `scripts/bench.py --set leaps` at 140 ms, five interleaved rounds per side, 15 s a scenario): per trail frame no dearer, leaps-all Minstr/s (app, core and holder) master / branch tail 86.4 / 82.6, comet 97.9 / 94.4; frame time within the runs' spread (tail 10.3 / 11.5 ms/s, ranges 10.2 to 11.5 and 9.2 to 12.8; comet 10.0 / 9.6).
- Three questions: as Cursor trail. Verdict D (fork only).
- Hot-path lines touched: `paint_grid`'s cursor block builds the cursor's rect once with `cursor_rect` (the rects it painted, unchanged) and keeps it in the grid; with `"all"`, the jump spot reads whether the cursor's cell is wide.
- Tests: `window::cursor_trail::tests::a_caught_up_tail_stays_ended_while_the_pulse_runs`, `window::cursor_shapes::tests::{the_tail_is_solid_and_cut_out_where_the_cursor_is, the_tail_chases_with_its_leading_corners_fastest_and_ends_within_half_a_px, a_tail_steered_mid_run_starts_from_its_corners, every_trail_ends_on_the_cursor_as_drawn_not_its_cell, every_trail_says_what_it_draws_so_a_new_one_starts_there, clipping_keeps_the_part_inside}` and the existing shape tests on quads; `window::tests::cursor_trail_runs_between_panes_then_ends` (a move mid-trail starts from what the trail drew, not the pane's cursor); `window::tests::cursor_jumps_in_all_mode_draw_once_settled_and_never_mid_burst` (a beam's jump starts from the beam, 2 px by a cell, and ends on it; a shape change while it runs keeps the trail). Mutation-checked: dropping the steering, the cut-out, the half-px end (a half-cell end instead), the beam's rect, or the leading corners' fast decay each fail a test.
- Files: `src/window/cursor_shapes.rs`, `src/window/cursor_trail.rs`, `src/terminal.rs`, `src/cursor_jumps.rs`, `src/vt.rs` (`Debug` on `CursorShape`), `src/window.rs` (tests), `defaults/AGENTS.md`
- Upstream: none

### Rex's comet, two-axis thresholds and Reduce motion

`feat/trail-comet` (branched from `master` after Cursor trails steer, fork only): the comet drawn after Rex Beta's shader and a 60 fps recording of it, the jump threshold in kitty's two values, and the effects off while macOS asks for reduced motion.

- Comet (`cursor_shapes::comet`), after Rex's shader (tail `mix(from, head, 1 - (1 - age)^3)`, radius from 0.2 to 1.0 of its width along the trail, strength `(core + glow) × along × (1 - age)` clamped at 1, core mixed 25% toward white) and the recording (a thin core a quarter to a third of the cell high, a tight glow, the whole streak on the first frame): two streaks, the outer then the core, each the hull of a circle at the head and one a fifth its radius at the tail end, so the head is a round nose and the tail end is rounded; half widths at the head 0.30 and 0.14 of the cell height. The tail eases into the head (`1 - (1 - t)^3` of the way over `cursor_trail_duration_ms`) and every layer fades at `1 - t`. Each streak brightens from none at the tail to full 0.65 of the way to the head and stays full (`COMET_FULL`, 1 / 1.55: where Rex's clamped core and glow reach 1 on the axis), painted as a GPUI linear gradient on the path (`linear_gradient`, angle from tail to head) whose stops `trail_paint::along` places on those two points as the GPU's `fill_color` measures them over the path's bounds, so they hold at any angle and length. The core is the cursor color 25% toward white.
- Timing: the recording's tail positions fit the same cubic over about 250 ms after a two-frame hold; at `cursor_trail_duration_ms` 250 the comet retracts at the recording's pace, at 140 it takes a little over half that time. The duration stays the one setting all the looks share.
- Glow round the head: one blurred shadow behind the streaks (`paint_drop_shadows` of a zero-size rect spread 0.3 of the cell height and blurred as much, so nothing is cut from its middle), about 0.44 strong at its middle and gone within about a cell: tight, as in the recording, rather than the shader's wider glow along the whole streak. Three primitives in all: the glow, the outer streak and the core. The paint is `src/window/trail_paint.rs` (with `paler`), apart from the window, so `tests/trail_pixels.rs` renders it.
- `cursor_trail_min_cells` takes one number (both axes; existing settings read as before) or `[columns, rows]`, as kitty's two-value `cursor_trail_start_threshold`: a jump draws when it passes either (`settings::MinCells`, `Jumps::due`). Serialised as one number while the two are equal, so a write of the whole settings (zoom, focus follows mouse) turns `[4, 4]` into `4`, the same meaning. Mods see the number, or the pair while the axes differ (`herdr.d.ts`). The Settings window shows Ignore moves across up to and Ignore moves down up to, rounds what it reads to whole cells within range (a value the app can't read steps to one it can), and writes one number while they're equal, else the pair; a value out of range is reported (`cursor_trail_min_cells [60, 3] is outside 0..50`) and each axis held within it.
- Reduce motion: with `App::reduce_motion` set (macOS: `gpui_base` reads `accessibilityDisplayShouldReduceMotion` at start; `macos::follow_reduce_motion` re-reads it on `NSWorkspaceAccessibilityDisplayOptionsDidChangeNotification`, through `gpui_base::apply_system_reduce_motion`, which defers to any value the app sets itself; the app sets none), no trail or pulse starts, and one running ends at its next frame. Other platforms unchanged. The notification path itself is not under test (the test scheduler never asks the platform); the tests set the flag.
- Bench: release builds, A/B against master 519d9ce, `scripts/bench.py --set leaps` with the comet at 140 ms, five interleaved rounds per side, 15 s a scenario, isolated app and core, `caffeinate -di`, no zero-draw runs: leaps-all Minstr/s (app, core and holder) master / branch 98.5 / 91.4 (a trail frame about 0.5 M instructions cheaper: three primitives where there were four paths), frame time 12.2 / 13.0 ms/s within the runs' spread (11.9 to 14.6, 10.8 to 13.3), app CPU 5.1 / 5.0%, trail frames 13.1 / 13.3 a second; leaps-off equal (34.8 / 34.9). The tail is untouched by this branch (an earlier run of this build before the review fixes: 86.6 / 87.0).
- Three questions: as Cursor trail. Verdict D (fork only). The Reduce motion follower alone would suit upstream (GPUI reads the switch only at start), verdict B once something upstream animates.
- Tests: `window::cursor_shapes::tests::{the_comet_is_a_glow_a_core_and_a_glow_round_the_head, comet_the_whole_streak_shows_at_once_then_the_tail_eases_into_the_head, comet_thin_with_a_round_nose_and_a_rounded_tail_end_brightest_at_the_head, comet_points_any_way_and_a_glint_stays_at_the_cursor_to_the_end}`; `tests/trail_pixels.rs`, harness-less as `tests/shot_pixels.rs` since macOS renders on the main thread, headless GPU renders read back: a streak's gradient from tail to head (9, 68, 126, 186, 248 of 255), stops on their points for a short, a diagonal and a reversed streak (about 0.1, 0.5 and 0.9 of full at 0.1, 0.5 and 0.9 of the way), the glow (112 at its middle, 36 at 8 px, 0 at 30 px, round), and the comet as drawn in amber, level and diagonal (faint at the tail, full from about two thirds on, its nose bluer, toward white, than the streak's edge); mutation-checked: a solid fill, no shadow, or stops at the bounds' edges each fail it. `cursor_jumps::tests::a_move_counts_when_it_passes_either_threshold_and_one_number_is_both`; `settings::tests::cursor_trail_min_cells_takes_one_number_for_both_axes_or_a_pair`; `window::tests::cursor_trail_runs_between_panes_then_ends` and `cursor_jumps_in_all_mode_draw_once_settled_and_never_mid_burst` (reduce motion ends a trail, starts none, and draws no jump); `scripts/settings.test.ts` (the two steppers write a number or a pair, repair values the app can't read, and both show).
- `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES`: the Cargo.toml input hash updated by hand (cargo-about isn't installed here); `block2` 0.6.2 was already listed, the lock's package set is unchanged, and `tests/notices.rs` passes.

### Kitty images by file path on this machine

`fix/kitty-local-file-medium` (branched from `master`): a terminal whose herdr-core runs on this machine accepts kitty graphics images sent by file path (`t=f`). libghostty-vt accepts only inline data (`t=d`) unless its embedder enables more, and Herdr enabled nothing, so `kitten icat`'s probes got `EINVAL: unsupported medium` for the temporary file and shared memory mediums, and snacks.nvim, which sends a local image as a path, never had one drawn.

- Locality: a terminal's address is `tN` here and `<machine>/tN` on another machine (`mirror::machine_of`, already how the header's `{machine}` and resume tell them apart). An address is fixed for the life of a `TerminalView`, and every connection makes a fresh `Vt` in `TerminalView::connect`, so `screen_for(address, cols, rows)` there makes the screen and sets `Vt::allow_image_files(machine_of(address).is_none())`. The screen `attach` makes first is replaced by `connect` before anything is written to it. The app's core is always the local `hc connect`; other machines' terminals are forwarded through it.
- Remote terminals keep refusing: a path from a program there names that machine's disk, and the reply would tell it about files here.
- File medium only. Temporary files (`t=t`, restricted to a temp folder as Ghostty restricts them) and shared memory (`t=s`) stay off: libghostty-vt deletes either after reading it, and several Herdr windows can attach to one terminal, each with its own `Vt` that reads the command, so the second viewer would find nothing and draw no image. Refused, `kitten icat` falls back to inline data, which every viewer decodes. `t=f` leaves the file alone, so every viewer reads it.
- The command need not come from a program that chose to send it: anything displayed in a local terminal can carry it, such as a file shown with `cat`, `curl` output, or the output of an `ssh` session. The app reads the path as itself, with its own access to this machine's disk. A program on a remote host reached over `ssh` inside a local terminal is not told apart from a local one, so from the replies it can learn whether a file exists here, its size (by probing `S=` and `O=`) and whether the app can read it. That is accepted, as kitty and Ghostty accept it by allowing the file medium by default.
- Relative paths resolve against the app's working directory, not the program's (libghostty-vt opens at `AT_FDCWD`).
- Opening never blocks: the patched libghostty-vt (see "Vendored Ghostty patch") opens with `O_NONBLOCK`, so a FIFO (named pipe) or a serial or tty device gets `EINVAL: invalid data` at once instead of holding the UI thread that calls `Vt::write`.
- Reading can stall, and that is accepted: a regular file is read synchronously on the UI thread, by every window's `Vt` separately, up to libghostty-vt's 400 MB file cap (`max_size` in `vendor/ghostty/src/terminal/kitty/graphics_image.zig`). A large file, or a slow or dead network or FUSE (filesystem in userspace) mount, holds the app for the length of that read.
- Inline (`t=d`) is unchanged, as is the 128 MiB image storage limit, which bounds what is kept, not what is read.
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? Yes: the app decodes kitty images in its own `Vt`, and only it can tell libghostty-vt which mediums to accept. (2) Can only the app see it? Yes: whether a terminal is on this machine and the `Vt`'s options are app state. (3) Can a mod not do it correctly? A mod can't reach the `Vt`. Verdict A: upstream as is, a fix for images that never draw.
- Tests: `vt::tests::kitty_images_by_path_load_only_when_allowed` (a new `Vt` refuses `t=f`; allowed, a PNG file loads, answers `OK`, decodes to the red pixel and is not deleted; `t=t` with a properly named temp file and `t=s` stay `EINVAL: unsupported medium`, the temp file kept; disallowed again refuses; inline answers `OK` throughout), `terminal::tests::only_this_machines_terminals_load_kitty_images_by_path` (a view of `t1` answers `OK` and of `remote/t1` `EINVAL: unsupported medium` for the same file after a reattach, inline `OK` for both). Removing the locality check or the enable call fails them. `vt::tests::kitty_image_path_to_a_fifo_is_refused_without_blocking` (Unix: a `t=f` naming a FIFO with no writer answers an error, not `OK`, in under a second; on the unpatched Ghostty `291fd050b` the open blocks, a watchdog writer releases it after 3 s and the test fails on the elapsed time instead of hanging).
- End to end: `python3 -I scripts/kitty_fifo_freeze.py --bin target/release/herdr-app --target fifo|png [--out dir]` launches the built app isolated as `scripts/bench.py` does (its own app home, XDG folders and core session, in the background), runs a program in a new space that sends the `t=f` command for a FIFO or a PNG, and probes the app's control socket before and after. Patched: every probe answers in about 25 ms, the FIFO's reply is `EINVAL: invalid data` and no reader is left waiting in `open()`, the PNG's is `OK`. Unpatched, the probes after the emit time out and a reader is found waiting.
- Files: `src/vt.rs`, `src/terminal.rs`, `scripts/kitty_fifo_freeze.py`
- Upstream: none yet. The freeze is herdrdev/herdr-app#302.

### Vendored Ghostty patch

`fix/kitty-local-file-medium` also moves `vendor/ghostty` from `ghostty-org/ghostty` to the fork's Ghostty, `https://github.com/abhijit-s/ghostty` (https, so anonymous clones and CI checkouts work), at `f39ed1bd7` on branch `herdr/kitty-file-nonblocking-open`: upstream Herdr's pinned Ghostty `291fd050b` plus one commit. That commit opens kitty file and temporary file transmission paths with `O_NONBLOCK | O_NOCTTY` on POSIX, so a FIFO or serial device can't block `open()`, and the existing checks on the handle (regular file only) refuse it before any read. The read of a regular file stays synchronous. It adds a FIFO regression test in `src/terminal/kitty/graphics_image.zig`. Windows keeps the blocking open. It is needed because "Kitty images by file path on this machine" enables the file medium, and libghostty-vt is fed from the app's UI thread.

- Upstream status: the fix for Ghostty's main branch is `fix/kitty-file-nonblocking-open` on abhijit-s/ghostty (commit `f1da2c72d`), waiting on the Ghostty triage and vouch before a pull request. The Herdr issue is herdrdev/herdr-app#302.
- Build: `crates/ghostty-sys/build.rs` reruns on any change under `vendor/ghostty/src`, so checking out another Ghostty commit rebuilds libghostty-vt. CI checks out submodules from `.gitmodules`, and its cache key hashes `vendor/**`, so the move costs one cold build. Keep `herdr/kitty-file-nonblocking-open` on abhijit-s/ghostty: the pinned commit must stay reachable from a branch for clones and CI to fetch it.
- Syncs: `scripts/fork-sync` runs `git submodule sync` before `git submodule update`, so a clone made before the move fetches from the fork's Ghostty. When upstream Herdr bumps `vendor/ghostty`, merging `upstream/master` conflicts on the submodule pointer and `fork-sync` stops. Then:
  - If upstream's new Ghostty already contains the fix (`git -C vendor/ghostty cherry <new commit> f1da2c72d f1da2c72d~1` prints `- f1da2c72d` when the same change landed; either way, with that commit checked out, `cargo test kitty_image_path_to_a_fifo` must pass, which catches a fix written differently; a text search won't do, as `nonblocking = false` already appears in the unpatched file), take upstream's pointer, set `.gitmodules` back to `https://github.com/ghostty-org/ghostty`, run `git submodule sync`, and delete this section.
  - Otherwise, in `vendor/ghostty`, fetch upstream Ghostty's new commit, branch `herdr/kitty-file-nonblocking-open-<short sha>` from it, cherry-pick `f39ed1bd7` (or `f1da2c72d` if upstream Herdr moved to Ghostty's current Zig), run `zig build test -Dtest-filter=kitty`, push the branch to abhijit-s/ghostty, then stage that commit as the submodule pointer, finish the merge and update the commit named here.
- Files: `.gitmodules`, `vendor/ghostty`, `scripts/fork-sync`
- Upstream: needs Ghostty's fix merged and a Herdr Ghostty bump; then this section goes away.

### Changes panel in the terminal font

`fix/changes-panel-font` (branched from `master`): the shipped Changes panel (`defaults/mods/changes`) draws its diff in the terminals' font: family, size, weight, and rows as tall as the terminals' line height makes theirs. It drew `settings().font_family` at a fixed `text_size(11)` in 20 px rows, so the family was right but nothing else was. `?? "JetBrains Mono"` never applied: `settings()` is the resolved `Settings` (layout, else person, else Herdr's), so `font_family` is never null.

- Why a mod can't just read `font_size` and `font_weight`: a mod's `text_size` is drawn at `ui_scale × ui_text_scale` and its `font_weight` takes the panels' `ui_font_weight` shift, where terminals draw `font_size × zoom` at `font_weight` unshifted. With `ui_text_scale: 1.05`, `text_size(settings().font_size)` draws 5% larger than the terminals.
- `settings().terminal_font` (`Settings::terminal_font_for_mods`, added to `for_mods`): `{ family, size, weight, line_height, fallbacks }`, with `size` the terminals' px (`terminal_font_size`) divided by `ui_scale × ui_text_scale`, `weight` `font_weight` less `ui_weight_shift` (clamped to 100..900, so a weight whose shifted value would fall outside that range draws lighter or heavier than the terminals'), `line_height` within the 0.8 to 2 the terminals' rows keep, and `fallbacks` the `font_fallbacks` list. It follows the app's zoom, not a pane's own text zoom. `size` and `weight` hold for the scales of the moment: a mod reads them at each render (an `EditorState` keeps the size it was made with, so AGENTS.md says to make it again when the scales change). Builds without it give `undefined`, and the panel then draws at 11 px in 20 px rows.
- The panel: `terminal_text().text_size(size)` (see "Terminal text in mods"), rows `ceil(em × 1.3 × line_height)` where `em` is the text in mod px, and the text's own line box the same height, so a line sits centred as in a terminal. The rows are close to the terminals', not exactly theirs: 1.3 em is a middle value for the font's ascent plus descent (JetBrains Mono is about 1.32, Menlo 1.16, Fira Code 1.4), which the terminals measure through the window's text system and round to device pixels, and mods can do neither. For JetBrains Mono at 12 px and line height 1 it gives the terminals' 16 px rows. A possible follow-up: give mods the measured cell height (`terminal::cell_size`, from the window) so rows match for every font. The scroll width (0.64 em a character) and the line-number column (five digits) follow the size too. The file list, commit box and the "Select a file" hint keep the panels' font.
- Fallbacks, ligatures, thickening, contrast and smoothing: the diff takes them from `terminal_text()` (see "Terminal text in mods" below), in place of `font_family` and `font_weight`.
- Other mods: `defaults/mods/files` hands its code viewer `settings().font_family` with a fixed `font_size: 12`; it is listed for the same change, not changed here. The spinner mods (`pane`, `peek`, `picker`, `tabs`, `agents`, Settings) use `settings().font_family` for spinner glyphs only, which is right.
- Three questions: (1) Does the app act on it? It resolves the terminal font and the panels' scales. (2) Can only the app see it? The composed font, `terminal_font_size` and the weight shift are app state; a mod can rebuild them from `settings()` only by copying their arithmetic. (3) Can a mod not do it correctly? The panel is a mod and does it once the value is there. Verdict B: upstream the `terminal_font` value; the panel's use of it rests on the fork-only `terminal_text()`, so upstream would take `text_size(size)` and `font_weight(weight)` with it instead.
- Tests: `settings::tests::terminal_font_for_mods_is_the_terminals_in_mod_units` (Herdr's defaults pass through; at zoom 1.5, `ui_zoom` 1.2, `ui_text_scale` 1.05 and `ui_font_weight` 500, `size × ui_scale × ui_text_scale` is the terminals' px and the weight 300 draws at 400), `mods::render_tests::changes_diff_follows_the_terminal_font` (the diff's size 12 from a composed `settings()`, and a line box of 1.6: 24 px rows over 15 px text at `ui_text_scale` 1.25 and line height 1.2; the virtual list's rows themselves aren't in the description; without `terminal_font`, 11 px with no line height).
- Files: `src/settings.rs`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/mods/changes/main.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`
- Upstream: none yet.

### Terminal text in mods

`feat/terminal-text` (branched from `master`): a style method `.terminal_text()` draws an element's text, and its children's, in the terminals' font and glyph rendering, and the Changes panel's diff uses it. Panel text otherwise takes the `ui_` look: the diff got the family, size and (compensated) weight of the terminals, but `ui_font_thicken` and its strength, `ui_text_contrast`, `ui_text_rendering`, no `font_fallbacks` and the system font's ligatures, so it looked heavier and softer than a terminal beside it.
- gpui (`vendor/gpui-pre`): `TextStyle::terminal` and `Styled::terminal_text`, reflected into the mod style API like every no-argument style method (so the mod typings list it with no hand binding). `Window::set_terminal_text(Option<TerminalText>)` says how it draws: a `Font` (family, features, fallbacks, weight) and the `GlyphThicken`, contrast and subpixel choice. `Window::text_style` applies that font before the refinement that set the flag, so the element's own font styles still win; `Window::with_text_style`, which divs call around request layout, prepaint and paint, scopes `with_font_weight_shift(0)`, `with_glyph_thicken`, `with_glyph_contrast` and `with_glyph_subpixel` for the subtree, the same scopes the terminal grid paints in, and deferred draws (popovers) under terminal text prepaint and paint in them too (`in_terminal_text`). `TextLayout`'s measure runs when the root computes layout, outside those scopes, so it shapes at the weight shift in force where the text was laid out (`WeightShiftScope`). `Window::glyph_look` (test support) reads the glyph scopes.
- Herdr: `terminal::terminal_text()` builds the look from `cell_font(false, false)` with `ligature_features(font_ligatures)` and `glyph_look()`, the thickening (`font_thicken` at `font_thicken_strength`), `text_contrast` and `text_rendering` the grid's canvas paints in, so the two can't drift. `window::set_text_look`, called from `AppWindow::render`, sets it each frame beside the panels' `set_glyph_thicken` and friends. Only the main window sets it; elsewhere `terminal_text()` changes nothing.
- The diff: `terminal_text().text_size(size)` plus the row line box. The size still comes from `settings().terminal_font`, since a mod's `text_size` is scaled by the panels' zoom and text scale.
- What it doesn't do, which the grid does: place glyphs on the cell grid (`terminal_cell_width`, two cells for wide characters), draw box, block, braille and powerline characters as sprites, fit fallback glyphs and private-use icons to the cell, use `font_weight_bold` for bold (`font_bold()` inside is 700), fade faint text by `faint_opacity` or dim text for `unfocused_dim_scope: "text"`, follow a pane's own text zoom, and size rows from the measured cell (the diff's rows stay the 1.3 em estimate above). Ligatures: the grid shapes ASCII runs with no two adjacent punctuation marks with ligatures off even when `font_ligatures` is on (`needs_ligatures`, on the ground that JetBrains Mono joins only punctuation), and terminal text shapes everything with them on. gpui-kit's text nodes re-push resolved styles with defaults dropped (`TextStyle::subtract`), so inside them an explicit default (`font_normal()`, weight 400) under terminal text takes the terminals' weight.
- Other shipped mods with terminal-like text, not changed here: `defaults/mods/files`, whose code viewer is an `EditorState` given `settings().font_family` at a fixed `font_size: 12` (a `terminal_text()` around it would bring the glyph look and weight shift, but the editor takes its family and size from the state, not the text style), and the working spinners in `agents`, `pane`, `peek`, `picker` and `tabs`, which draw braille in `settings().font_family` without the terminals' fallbacks (see the glyph note under "Working spinner").
- Tests: `mods::render_tests::changes_diff_draws_as_terminal_text` (the real Changes mod drawn through a recording text system: every glyph in the terminals' family has `font_thicken` strength 30, the `font_fallbacks`, `ligature_features(true)` and weight 400 under `ui_font_weight` 500, and the panel's own glyphs strength 192, no fallbacks, a shifted weight; fails with the master mod), `mods::render_tests::terminal_text_takes_the_windows_terminal_look` (the look's font, shift 0 and glyph look for a nested child, the element's own weight winning, the window's for a sibling, and strings measured at root layout shaped unshifted), `mods::render_tests::changes_diff_follows_the_terminal_font` (the diff is `terminal_text` at size 12 with a 1.6 line box and no `font_family` or `font_weight`; without `terminal_font`, 11 px with no line box), `terminal::tests::terminal_text_draws_as_the_cells_do` (family, `font_weight`, fallbacks, ligature features off and on, and the terminals' thickening, contrast and subpixel over the panels').
- Files: `vendor/gpui-pre/src/{style.rs,styled.rs,window.rs,text_system.rs,elements/text.rs}`, `vendor/gpui-kit/crates/shell/src/plugin.rs` (`DescriptionSession::view`, for the drawing test), `src/terminal.rs`, `src/window.rs`, `src/mods/render_tests.rs`, `defaults/mods/changes/main.js`, `defaults/AGENTS.md`, `defaults/herdr.d.ts`
- Upstream: needs the gpui patches, which upstream Herdr doesn't carry; fork only.

### Rejected text experiments

Two text-quality experiments measured against the shipped thicken, contrast and weight settings, and not kept. The numbers are here so neither is tried again without a new reason.

- **Gamma-correct (linear-light) glyph blending.** A Skia-style preblend (`SkMaskGamma`): a per-sprite gamma in `MonochromeSprite`, the shader assuming the text's perceptual inverse as background. Headless Metal readback, JetBrains Mono 12 px: at gamma 2.2 plain light-on-dark ink went from 1.21× dark-on-light to 1.91×; with thicken 30 and contrast 0.2 (equal ink in both polarities, 1.00) it split to 1.61× and moved light-on-dark ink +23% away from the Ghostty match. Equal polarities needed gamma ≈ 0.6, the opposite of linear light. The polarity gap in Herdr comes from CoreGraphics dilating light text more (`glyph_dilation_for_color`), not from gamma-space blending, and thicken already removes it by giving both polarities one mask. Prototype commit `f7a2a85` (branch deleted).
- **Subpixel (LCD) smoothing on macOS.** CoreGraphics produces no per-channel coverage on macOS 27 with any public or private smoothing setting tried (`setShouldSmoothFonts`, `AppleFontSmoothing`, `CGContextSetFontSmoothingStyle`), so it would need swash rasterising 3× horizontally plus FreeType's 5-tap filter, with CoreText keeping shaping and advances (they match to 0.00000 px). About half the plumbing exists from Zed's Windows/Linux work (`TextRenderingMode`, `SubpixelSprite`); Metal lacks the subpixel atlas kind, shader and batch. Estimated 5–8 days and ~1,000 fork-only lines. Not built at first (since built, see "Subpixel smoothing" above): swash can't reproduce CoreGraphics' smoothing dilation, so it drops the Ghostty match thicken was tuned for, it puts colour fringes into screenshots and screen shares, and side-by-side 1x renders showed fringing without a clear sharpness gain.
